Machine Learning for Agricultural Variable Prediction in Wireless Sensor Networks and IoT Environments: A Comparative Study with Statistical Validation

Mohamed Faye, Joel Rostand Nteupe, Damien Wohwe Sambo, Wajdi Elleuch — 2026-11-30 — openalex

DOI : —

Precision agriculture depends on reliable sensor data for efficient crop and resource management. Wireless Sensor Networks transmit environmental measurements continuously, imposing a high energy cost on battery-powered nodes. This paper evaluates three machine learning models, namely Random Forest, XGBoost, and Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM) networks, for one-step-ahead prediction of agricultural variables collected from two distinct sensor datasets: a greenhouse WSN dataset with four Bluetooth Low Energy and Wi-Fi scenarios (192 observations each), and a large IoT dataset with 37,920 timestamped records. A persistence baseline is included in every comparison as a scientifically rigorous calibration reference. Feature engineering follows a strict anti-leakage protocol using lag, rolling-window, and difference features derived exclusively from past observations. Hyperparameters are selected through exhaustive grid search with TimeSeriesSplit cross-validation for tree-based models and Bayesian optimisation for the LSTM. Statistical significance is assessed with a Friedman test followed by pairwise Wilcoxon signed-rank tests with Bonferroni correction. The numerical analysis reveals that while Random Forest achieved the lowest RMSE among the trained models in 9 of the 12 WSN tasks and XGBoost did so for all three IoT targets, the naive persistence baseline remained the overall best predictor in 9 of the 12 WSN tasks and 2 of the 3 IoT tasks due to the low high-frequency variance of the micro-climate signals. The Friedman test confirms that the structural differences among the trained candidates are significant (χ 2 = 14.00, p = 0.0009). Under an operational dualprediction paradigm, offline transmission-suppression analysis indicates that data-driven virtual sensing can eliminate an upper bound of approximately 68% of communications on nontrivial WSN tasks, offering substantial potential transmission reductions and energy-saving opportunities. Temperature and humidity models transfer across communication protocols without retraining (R 2 ≥ 0.83), facilitating flexible cross-protocol edge deployment scenarios.

Real-Time AI-Driven Detection and Prevention of Over-the-Air Firmware Attacks for IoT Devices

Mama M'Bengue, Damien Sambo, Patrick Sondi — 2026-11-30 — openalex

DOI : —

Over-the-Air (OTA) firmware updates are essential for maintaining the security of connected devices, but they expose systems to interception, downgrade and malicious code injection attacks that cryptographic mechanisms alone cannot prevent. This paper presents a real-time detection and prevention system that analyzes OTA network traffic before firmware installation. The detection problem is formalized within the Neyman-Pearson framework to strictly control the false alarm rate. Five classifiers, Random Forest, XGBoost, SVM, LSTM and CNN-LSTM, are evaluated on the public Edge-IIoTset dataset. XGBoost achieves the best accuracy (0.9676) and the highest AUC (0.9986) with an inference time of 0.0164 ms per sample. A hybrid architecture, where the CNN-LSTM extracts deep features and XGBoost performs the final classification, further reduces inference time to 0.0131 ms while maintaining nearly identical accuracy (0.9664). A realistic OTA simulation on a loopback interface generates 3,600 labeled sessions covering six attack scenarios. Experiments on mixed data show that accuracy remains above 0.93 even when 80,% of the training data are simulated, confirming excellent adaptability to new environments. A four-level prevention mechanism (logging, notification, blocking, rollback) is implemented with a rollback threshold of 0.95, justified by model calibration and an AUC of 0.9986. Statistical significance is confirmed using McNemar's test and SHAP-based analysis identifies TCP related features as key drivers of detection, enhancing model interpretability. Our results show that XGBoost is particularly well suited for resource-constrained IoT devices, while the hybrid architecture offers the best speed-accuracy trade-off for critical real-time deployments.

Adolescent Patellofemoral Bone Shape

Rosemarijn van Paassen — 2026-11-24 — openalex

DOI : —

The chapters within this thesis aim to unravel knowledge on the shape of the knee and associated factors (Figure 1.3). The overarching aim is to identify the association between knee joint geometry, joint loading, and knee complaints in young adolescents (Figure 1.3). Four studies conducted within this thesis utilized data from the Generation R study, and one study utilized follow-up data from the Triple P study [35], a case-control study that included participants with PFP and age-matched controls. The first two chapters of this thesis focus on statistical shape modeling and knee alignment, while the last three chapters examine factors associated with pain. More specifically, the aim of Chapter 2 was to identify shape variations associated with loading (i.e., BMI and physical activity). Chapter 3 examines the validity of calculating knee alignment parameters using automatically extracted landmarks from3D models. Chapter 4 evaluated the relationship between patellar and femoral shape at age 13 and incident knee pain at age 17, while Chapter 5 examines incident musculoskeletal pain within the Generation R study at age 17. Chapter 6 focused on factors associated with the persistence of patellofemoral pain at one-year follow-up. Lastly, Chapter 7 presents a general discussion of this thesis, integrating the most important findings of the aforementioned studies and discussing them within a broader context, while also exploring potential suggestions for future research.

The Impact of Total Patient Care Nursing on Restraint and Seclusion in a Pediatric Inpatient Psychiatric Unit

Lisa LeFeber — 2026-11-21 — openalex

DOI : —

Seclusion and restraint (S/R) are common but high-risk interventions on pediatric inpatient psychiatric units, and nursing care delivery models may influence how often units rely on them. This retrospective, quasi-experimental study examined whether implementation of a total patient care (TPC) nursing model, in which one nurse holds full responsibility for a small group of patients during a shift, was associated with changes in S/R use on a 24-bed pediatric inpatient psychiatric unit. Admissions before and after the unit's transition to TPC were compared on the number of seclusion and restraint episodes, as well as occurrence of restrictive events (at least one seclusion or restraint episode). Comparisons were conducted in the full sample (n = 257 admissions) and, to address non-independence from repeat admissions, in a subset limited to patients with only one admission (n = 201). No statistically significant differences emerged between groups in either analysis. These null findings should be interpreted in light of a short post-implementation window, the absence of a concurrent comparison condition, and limited data on implementation fidelity. Results did not support the hypothesis that TPC could rapidly reduce S/R use; however, S/R rates also did not increase after the transition, suggesting that in early implementation TPC yielded S/R use that was similar to treatment as usual. Future research using longer observation windows and prospective designs is needed to clarify TPC's effect on S/R use in pediatric psychiatric care.

Analysis dataset: High ambient temperatures in pregnancy and preterm birth risk

Maxine Pepper — 2026-11-18 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23809168

This repository contains the analytical datasets and supporting documentation used in the study High Ambient Temperatures in Pregnancy and Preterm Birth Risk by Pepper et al, 2026. The study examined the association between high ambient temperatures during pregnancy and preterm birth in Brazil, with the aim of identifying critical exposure windows. The repository contains: Analysis dataset: The individual-level analytical dataset used in the statistical analyses, including birth outcomes, gestational age, maternal characteristics, geographic identifiers, and weekly temperature and humidity exposure metrics across pregnancy. Temperature percentile dataset: For each Intermediate Geographic Region (RGINT), the dataset contains temperature values corresponding to percentiles from 0.1 to 99.9 of the baseline (1986-2005) temperature distribution. Data dictionary: Documentation describing all variables included across both data sets. The analysis dataset was derived by linking publicly available data from Brazil's Live Birth Information System (Sistema de Informações Sobre Nascidos Vivos) and Mortality Information System (Sistema de Informação Sobre Mortalidade), with climatic data from the Brazilian Daily Weather Gridded Data (Xavier et al, 2022). The analysis scripts are available at https://github.com/cidacslab. Together, these files allow reproduction of the analyses presented in the study. Reference Pepper M, Cerqueira-Silva T, Masselot P, et al. High ambient temperatures in pregnancy and preterm birth risk. JAMA Netw Open. 2026;9(11):e2644788. doi:10.1001/jamanetworkopen.2026.44788

Naar Geünificeerde Ruimtelijke Multi-Omics Een Modaliteitsagnostisch Diep Generatief Model voor Gezamenlijke Integratie, Batchcorrectie en Cross-Modale Vertaling

Jose Ignacio Alvira Larizgoitia — 2026-11-13 — openalex

DOI : —

Biological processes are very complex in nature and analyzing them in the light of only one data source excludes important complementary information out of the study. Single-cell multi-omics, and more recently spatially resolved multi-omics technologies provide a multi-facetted view of cell identity, tissue microarchitecture, cellular communication and pathophysiology. The aim of this project is to develop novel methods that leverage multimodal data, coming from these technologies, to push the state of the art methods for cell type/state identification, cell niche characterization and whole-slide sample classification. These methods will leverage current state-of-the-art deep neural network architectures, adapted and improved to work with highly-dimensional multimodal data. They will be evaluated with both synthetic and real world datasets for single-cell and spatial multi-omics. We will compile the developed algorithms into a complete end-to-end data integration framework. By doing this, we aim to contribute to the next generation of molecular digital pathology, leading to new insights into disease, and improving current diagnostic and prognostic potential. This project will provide important resources and methods to analyze and integrate different data modalities that could be potentially applicable in any field, not only in biomedical sciences.

The Effectiveness of Emotion-Focused Therapy in Improving Psychological Flexibility and Zest for Life Among University Students Who Have Experienced a Romantic Breakup

Samaneh Seyedabadi, Maryam Nasri, Fatemeh Shahabizadeh — 2026-11-10 — openalex

DOI : —

Objective: This study aimed to investigate the effectiveness of emotion-focused therapy in improving psychological flexibility and zest for life among university students who had experienced a romantic breakup. Methods and Materials: This study employed a quasi-experimental pretest–posttest control-group design with a follow-up assessment. The statistical population comprised all university students in Birjand who had experienced a romantic breakup during the 2025–2026 academic year. Participants were recruited through convenience sampling, and 30 eligible students were selected based on the inclusion and exclusion criteria and randomly assigned to an experimental group (n = 15) or a control group (n = 15). The research instruments included the Psychological Flexibility Questionnaire (2010) and the Zest for Life After Romantic Breakup Questionnaire (Shomali & Barkhordari, 2026). The experimental group received group-based emotion-focused therapy in eight 90-minute sessions held twice weekly, whereas the control group received no intervention during this period. The data were analyzed using repeated-measures analysis of variance and the Bonferroni post hoc test. Statistical analyses were conducted using SPSS version 27. Findings: The results indicated that emotion-focused therapy had a significant effect on increasing psychological flexibility and zest for life among university students who had experienced a romantic breakup (p < .01). These effects were also maintained at the one-month follow-up assessment, and the differences between the experimental and control groups remained statistically significant for both variables (p < .05). Conclusion: Based on the findings, emotion-focused therapy can be used as an effective intervention to improve psychological flexibility and enhance zest for life among university students who have experienced a romantic breakup. It is recommended that this therapeutic approach be implemented systematically within university psychological and counseling services, particularly for students experiencing emotional distress following romantic breakups.

The Relationship of Classroom Emotion, Self-Compassion, and Tolerance of Ambiguity with Academic Anxiety among Female Senior High School Students in District 9 of Karaj

Maryam Mohamadbeiki Khortabi, Morteza Tarkhan — 2026-11-10 — openalex

DOI : —

Objective: This study aimed to examine the relationship of classroom emotion, self-compassion, and tolerance of ambiguity with academic anxiety among female senior high school students in District 9 of Karaj. Methods and Materials: The present study employed a descriptive-correlational research design. The statistical population consisted of female senior high school students in District 9 of Karaj, from whom 210 students were selected initially through multistage cluster sampling and finally through convenience sampling. Data were collected using the Friedman and Jacob Test Anxiety Questionnaire (1997), the Classroom Emotion Questionnaire developed by Titsworth et al. (2010), the Tolerance of Ambiguity Questionnaire developed by Weden et al. (2003), and the Short Form of the Self-Compassion Scale developed by Raes et al. (2011). The collected data were analyzed using descriptive indices, Pearson correlation coefficient, and stepwise multiple regression analysis. Findings: The inferential findings indicated a significant negative relationship between classroom emotion and academic anxiety, suggesting that more positive classroom emotional experiences were associated with lower levels of academic anxiety. A significant negative relationship was also found between self-compassion and academic anxiety, indicating that students with higher self-compassion experienced lower academic anxiety. In the stepwise regression analysis, classroom emotion emerged as the strongest predictor and explained 15.50% of the variance in academic anxiety. Self-compassion was added in the second step and explained an additional 2.20% of the variance. Together, classroom emotion and self-compassion accounted for 17.70% of the total variance in academic anxiety. Tolerance of ambiguity did not enter the final predictive model. Conclusion: The findings suggest that classroom emotion and self-compassion are important psychological and educational variables in predicting academic anxiety among female senior high school students. Strengthening positive classroom emotional experiences and promoting self-compassion may help reduce students’ academic anxiety.

The Mediating Role of Self-Injurious Thoughts in the Relationship Between Psychological Distress and Death Obsession with Suicide Attempts Among Adolescents Experiencing War Anxiety

Zeinab Ameri, Maryam Daram, Shida Asobar, Habibeh Khazael, Ziba Gol Aflak — 2026-11-10 — openalex

DOI : —

Objective: This study aimed to examine the mediating role of self-injurious thoughts in the relationship between psychological distress and death obsession with suicide attempts among adolescents experiencing war anxiety. Methods and Materials: This applied study was conducted using a correlational design based on structural equation modeling. The statistical sample consisted of 215 adolescents aged 15 to 18 years living in Mahshahr, who were selected through voluntary sampling. The research instruments included the Shomali and Barkhordari War Anxiety Questionnaire, the Osman et al. Suicide Attempt Questionnaire, the Abdel-Khalek Death Obsession Scale, the Kessler et al. Psychological Distress Scale, and the Shomali and Barkhordari Self-Injurious Thoughts Questionnaire. Data were analyzed using Pearson correlation and structural equation modeling through SPSS version 27 and AMOS version 24. Findings: The inferential results showed that the direct effects of psychological distress and self-injurious thoughts on suicide attempts were positive and statistically significant. The direct effects of psychological distress and death obsession on self-injurious thoughts were also positive and statistically significant. In addition, the indirect effects of psychological distress and death obsession on suicide attempts through self-injurious thoughts were positive and statistically significant. However, the direct effect of death obsession on suicide attempts was not significant in the final structural model. These findings confirmed the mediating role of self-injurious thoughts in the relationship between psychological distress, death obsession, and suicide attempts among adolescents experiencing war anxiety. Conclusion: The findings indicate that self-injurious thoughts are a key cognitive-emotional mechanism linking psychological distress and death obsession to suicide attempts in adolescents exposed to war-related anxiety. Therefore, preventive and therapeutic interventions should prioritize the early identification and modification of self-injurious thoughts, especially among vulnerable adolescents experiencing psychological distress and death-related preoccupations.

Future Perspectives in Hypospadias: From Functional Evolution to Molecular Understanding

George Tsachouridis, Regenerative Medicine and Stem Cells, Urologie Medisch Kinderen, Laetitia M.O. de Kort, Petra de Graaf — 2026-11-05 — openalex

DOI : —

Hypospadias is one of the most common congenital anomalies of the male urogenital tract and remains associated with substantial complication rates and uncertainty about long-term functional outcomes, particularly after completion of puberty and into adulthood. This thesis addresses five major knowledge gaps: (1) the longitudinal evolution of voiding patterns after hypospadias repair, (2) the longterm performance of oral mucosa grafts in growing boys, (3) the microscopic and molecular characteristics of the urethral plate, (4) the potential of tissue engineering and regenerative strategies for posterior hypospadias, and (5) the need for coordinated, lifelong, patientcentred care with standardized outcome registration Chapter 2 presents a longitudinal cohort study on uroflowmetry patterns from childhood through puberty after hypospadias repair, showing that many boys with abnormal prepubertal flow achieve spontaneous normalization during puberty and that only a minority require surgical intervention, usually in the presence of both abnormal flow and urinary symptoms. These findings support a conservative, symptomdriven followup strategy rather than routine intervention based solely on uroflowmetry. Chapter 3, a systematic review, demonstrates that the evidence on longterm outcomes of oral mucosa graft urethroplasty in prepubertal boys is sparse, heterogeneous, and at serious risk of bias, with relevant reoperation rates and complications often emerging around or after puberty. Chapter 4 explores androgen receptor localization and expression in human oral mucosa and shows predominantly cytoplasmic localization, lack of clear nuclear translocation after androgen stimulation in vitro, and very low AR expression levels compared to androgenresponsive control cells, suggesting limited androgen responsiveness of oral mucosa grafts. Chapter 5 reveals that the hypospadiac urethral plate differs structurally and molecularly from normal urethral tissue, with persistent multilayered epithelium, an altered cytokeratin profile, reduced subepithelial capillary density, and abnormal elastin organization, consistent with developmental delay or arrest and providing a biological explanation for its surgical vulnerability. Chapter 6 summarizes the conclusions of the 2023 ERN eUROGEN and EJPRD networking meeting on lifelong posterior hypospadias, emphasizing the need for structured transitional care, lifelong multidisciplinary followup, Europeanlevel registries with standardized clinical and patientreported outcomes, and closer collaboration between clinicians, basic scientists, and patients. In the general discussion, the clinical and molecular findings are integrated, arguing for a shift from uniformly techniquedriven surgery towards stratified, biologyinformed and patientcentred hypospadias care, embedded in international collaboration and longterm followup.

A randomized dose-escalation phase I clinical trial of a nucleoprotein-based genetic vaccine against Crimean-Congo Hemorrhagic Fever

Alì Mirazimi, christian nordentoft, Per T Ljungman, lara sweetapple, Jingy Yan — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10776782/v1

Abstract Crimean–Congo haemorrhagic fever virus (CCHFV) is an emerging tick-borne virus transmitted primarily by Hyalomma ticks, whose geographic distribution is expanding. CCHFV infection is associated with high case-fatality rates, and there are currently no widely approved vaccines available globally. We were the first to demonstrate that a genetic DNA vaccine (CCHF-N-pVAX1) targeting the nucleoprotein (N) of CCHFV can confer robust protection against lethal disease in mice and attenuate clinical symptoms in non-human primates (NHPs), providing proof-of-concept for a previously underexplored vaccine strategy through activation of NP-specific T cells and antibodies. The CCHF-N-pVAX1 vaccine was tested in a phase I clinical trial in 15 healthy volunteers (HVs) with three dose groups (0.45 mg, 0.90 mg, and 1.80 mg). Five HVs per group received vaccinations at day 0, 28, and 84 given intramuscularly using in vivo electroporation (EP; IGEA Medical), followed by 3 months of follow-up. The protocol-defined completed study reports the primary safety evaluation and secondary immunogenicity assessments through day 169. No power calculations were performed. All patients experienced temporary pain in close proximity to the vaccination/EP procedure. The vaccine was safe, and no serious adverse event (SAE) occurred. 139 other Adverse Events (AEs) occurred; 69 reactogenicity and 70 other. The AEs were mostly of mild grade (n = 123); 10 AEs were of moderate intensity, and 6 AEs were of severe intensity (diarrhea (n = 1), headache (n = 1), and fatigue (n = 4)). Most AEs (114/139) were likely or possibly related to the vaccine/EP. CCHFV N-specific antibodies and T cells appeared after the 2nd dose, and mean levels peaked after the 3rd dose at 1207 ± 2747 (endpoint titres) and 621 ± 561 (IFN-g spotforming cells), respectively. The T cell responses remained high during the 3-month follow-up. The data was consistent with our previous studies in NHPs. In conclusion, we show for the first time that a genetic nucleoprotein-based vaccine against CCHF is safe and immunogenic in humans, inducing strong T- and B-cell responses in most vaccine recipients, and suggesting a potential to confer protection against severe disease in humans. EU clinical trial number 2023-508556-18-00/IN, ClinicalTrials.gov identifier NCT06684431.

Targeting the inflamed paraventricular hypothalamic-bone marrow axis rejuvenates lymphopoiesis in aged mice

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Wei‐Na Jin, Meng Jia, Shihe Jiang, Yiyuan Xu, Tian Lan — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-9350529/v1

Abstract Older individuals are prone to infectious disease, cancer and exhibit reduced vaccine efficacy due to age-associated immune dysfunction. The immune and nervous systems are anatomically and functionally connected, and brain aging contributes to the development of neurodegenerative disorders, including Parkinson’s and Alzheimer’s diseases. However, how the aging brain contributes to age-associated immune dysfunction, and which organ systems are involved, remains poorly understood. Here, we show that bone marrow from elderly humans and mice displays a reduced frequency and aberrant transcriptomes of common lymphoid progenitors (CLPs), accompanied by impaired lymphopoiesis. Neural denervation preserved lymphopoiesis and CLP abundance, indicating neural regulation of the bone marrow. Retrograde tracing from the bone marrow identified corticotropin-releasing hormone-expressing neurons in the paraventricular nucleus (PVN-CRH neurons) as a major participant in controlling lymphoid output. Mechanistically, aging triggers Toll-like receptor 4 (TLR4)–interferon signaling in PVN-CRH neurons, eliciting inflammatory hyperactivation. This hyperactivity destabilized DNA nucleotidylexotransferase (TdT/DNTT) expression in hematopoietic stem and progenitor cells (HSPCs) and disrupted lymphoid developmental trajectories in bone marrow via sympathetic output-β adrenergic receptors signal. Chemogenetic inhibition of aged PVN-CRH neurons or CRH neuron-specific TLR4 ablation restored CLPs and B-cell production in bone marrow and improved the H1N1 vaccine response in aged mice. In conclusion, our findings reveal a brain–bone marrow axis that becomes dysregulated with aging and propose neuromodulation as a potential strategy to rejuvenate immune function in older individuals.

The Density Distribution of Dark Matter in the Milky Way Based on Stellar Velocities Measured by GAIA DR2

Yuxin Qin — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.70267/icmpes.20261588100

Since the early stages of the formation of the universe, dark matter has played a crucial role in mapping the large scale structures, being the frame of matter overdensities which grew up to form galactic and intergalactic structures. However, due to its mysterious nature and the property only interacting with gravitational fields, not much light is shed on the discovery and understanding of dark matter’s possible particle nature. This paper aims to interpret the density distribution of dark matter in different regions of the Milky Way(Bulge, Disk, Halo) from most recent models(NFW, Einasto, Isothermal, Burkert, Moore) and match it with the data of stellar rotational velocities measured by GAIA DR2 database. We do not directly derive velocities from raw GAIA DR2, but instead use compiled rotation curve data calibrated with GAIA measurements taken from Sofue et al. 2025[20]. We have also extrapolated parameters and normalization factors regarding each model, and evaluated with a more rudimentary method offered from Cirelli, M. et al. 2011[7]. Through our method, we provide a rudimentary overview on how the dark matter density profiles correlate with the newest DR2 data and evaluate their applicability. In turn, we hope the results offer insight on which model could be compatible concurrently in our galaxy.

Well-Log LLM Benchmark: The First ADNOC Domain-Expert Curated Benchmark for LLM Reasoning of Well Logs on Carbonate Reservoirs

Nader Gerges, M. X. Lee, L. Barillas, J. Thomas, W. Muhamad Nasir — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/236501-ms

Abstract Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly being explored for technical tasks in the oil and gas industry. Well-log interpretation requires combining depth-indexed measurements, derived petrophysical properties, and quality-control information to characterize lithology, porosity, permeability, fluid saturation, and reservoir quality within intervals and across formations and wells. Reliable interpretation depends on cross-curve consistency, data quality, and the availability of complementary measurements. Recent work introduced WellLogBench and WellLog-LLM (Shikhar, S. et al., 2026) to provide such a reproducible evaluation setting. WellLogBench established an expert-validated benchmark based on public LAS data and structured petrophysical contexts. These contexts are constructed using Petrophys (Shikhar, S. et al., 2026), an open-source library for standardized well-log processing, normalization, derivation of petrophysical properties, and assembly of model-ready interpretation contexts. WellLog-LLM combines Dynamic Feature Selection with supervised fine-tuning, direct preference optimization, and Group Relative Context Optimization (GRCO). GRCO is a training-free, GRPO-inspired method that derives reusable guidance from relative comparisons of model rollouts and applies it during inference without updating model weights. Although the aforementioned work provides a reproducible setting for well-log reasoning, its scope does not cover the full conditions encountered in operational carbonate fields. Operational carbonate datasets contain operator-specific mnemonics, incomplete log suites, logs from different vintages and acquisition types (e.g., wireline and LWD), borehole and acquisition effects, legacy interpreted curves, and sparse core coverage. Models must distinguish direct measurements from derived properties and qualify their conclusions when the required evidence is absent. To address these challenges, this paper presents an ADNOC field-scale benchmark and context-optimization framework based on 172 wells from an onshore supergiant carbonate field. The dataset includes raw and interpreted logs, formation, zone, and subzone markers, operator-specific log definitions, from all available wells, and routine core analysis data from five cored wells. Approximately 1,500 question-answer pairs were generated from practical petrophysical workflows, and a fully expert-reviewed subset was used for the reported experiments. The framework extends the context pipeline of (Shikhar, S. et al., 2026) with field-specific mnemonic normalization, explicit tracking of data source and quality, automated query construction with Near Duplicate Detection (NDD) & Maximal Marginal Relevance (MMR) Selection, and Well-Adaptive Branch-Solve-Merge (BSM) for queries that exceed the model context window. The study also examines adaptation to the ADNOC setting through generic and ADNOC-specific GRCO experience contexts. Six open-source, and petroleum-domain LLMs were evaluated under the base and GRCO-assisted conditions. Proprietary models supported benchmark generation, but SME review still identified domain-critical failures, due to insufficient petrophysical grounding. Across the reported SF and CF experiments, Gemma-4-31B-IT was the strongest open-source baseline, with a two-scope mean score of 7.47 on a 1–10 scale. For single-formation tasks, ADNOC-specific GRCO produced more consistent improvements than generic open-source experience, increasing the score from 7.4 to 7.6—a 0.2-point gain, equivalent to 2% of the 10-point scale. In contrast, cross-formation gains were limited and varied across models. Expert review indicated that the principal failure modes were petrophysical rather than linguistic, including inappropriate log selection or use, unsupported conclusions about permeability or reservoir quality, and inadequate qualification of uncertain interpretations. The proposed benchmark provides a reusable framework for evaluating and improving future petrophysics-oriented LLMs intended to support decision-making by qualified domain experts.

From Scanned P&IDs to Engineering-Grade Plant Inventories: A Production-Deployed Pipeline Integrating Symbol Detection, Verification, and Automated Parts Counting

Parth Tulsyan — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/236567-ms

Abstract Objectives / Scope This paper addresses the end-to-end conversion of scanned Piping and Instrumentation Diagrams (P&IDs) into structured plant data and, on top of that, the automated generation of engineering-grade component inventories suitable for direct industrial consumption. The scope covers multi-class symbol detection, detection verification, geometric line-tracing for pipe-segment connectivity, tag-string optical character recognition for component labels, and the downstream parts-counting workflows that underpin material take-off (MTO) generation, bid preparation, and asset-registry compilation in process-facility engineering. Methods, Procedures, Process The pipeline integrates several tightly coupled stages within a single production system. Symbol detection identifies the taxonomy of P&ID symbology, with verification applied downstream to suppress false positives and resolve ambiguous class assignments. Geometric line-tracing based on morphological processing recovers pipe segments, while a tag-string OCR component associates alphanumeric component labels with their respective detections. A counting subsystem performs class normalization and category-level roll-up to yield engineering-grade inventories for consumption by downstream MTO and reporting workflows. Because a template library curated for one drawing standard cannot be scored on another, the evaluation reported here additionally trains a detector on the public benchmark's own training split. Results, Observations, Conclusions All results are obtained on a public, licensed benchmark using its published splits, so that every figure is reproducible by a third party. Detection accuracy is reported as per-class precision and recall against the corpus ground truth, reaching 0.995 mean average precision. Parts-count accuracy is reported as per-class precision, recall and exact-match rate against ground-truth counts: count precision reaches 0.992 with an exact count on 96.9% of sheet-and-class pairs, of which 95 of 3,096 are overcounted and none undercounted. Line-graph fidelity is reported as per-style recall, 0.989 on solid runs against 0.993 published, and symbol-to-symbol connectivity against published reference graphs reaches a recall of 0.856 at a precision of 0.997, an F1 of 0.921. Under the benchmark authors' own joint criterion — localization, class and associated tag all correct, with the association predicted rather than read off the corpus — the pipeline reaches 0.945 against 0.922 published. Eight symbol classes withheld from training entirely are identified from a single reference example at 98.8% against a chance rate of 12.5%. Two choices govern inventory quality while leaving detection unchanged: containment-aware duplicate merging raises the exact-count rate from 0.633 to 0.969, and the confidence threshold moves it from 0.819 to 0.996 before the error direction inverts above the optimum. Characteristic failure modes are cataloged, and the principal conclusion is drawn that the operations converting detections into counts, rather than isolated detection accuracy, dominate inventory fidelity in practical industrial use. Novel / Additive Information This work makes three additive contributions. First, a per-class count-level evaluation protocol for P&ID component inventories, applied on a public licensed benchmark: automated counting is not itself new, but measurement of the count has been absent. Second, evidence that per-class detection accuracy does not identify which components are counted reliably, validated against a split-half noise floor. Third, a quantified account of domain-specialization failure observed independently in two unrelated stages, with the mechanism identified in each.

A Master Well, Multilayer Artificial Intelligence / Machine Learning (AI/ML) Framework for Scalable Porosity Permeability Prediction and Log Reconstruction in Carbonate Reservoirs

Muhammad Faisal, Russell Farmer, Jahan Ahmed, Riaz Khan, Noura Al Saadi — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/235984-ms

Abstract Carbonate reservoirs exhibit complex petrophysical behavior arising from heterogeneity, uneven core coverage and variable log data quality. These challenges significantly limit the scalability and repeatability of conventional petrophysical workflows, particularly when extended to large well populations. To address these limitations, this study presents a Master Well centric artificial intelligence and machine learning (AI/ML) framework for scalable prediction of porosity and permeability and log reconstruction with a focus on improving model generalization, validation robustness and operational efficiency across the field. Conventional petrophysical machine learning is bound to the well in which it was calibrated. This study shifts the unit of training from the well to the field by constructing a Master Well, a single core calibrated reference population assembled by randomized sampling across nine wells. Two advances underpin the approach. Pore aspect ratio is estimated from the fractional lithology curves of the multimineral solution, so the elastic properties follow the local calcite and dolomite volumes and stay consistent with theoretical endmember values. The soft and stiff pore endmembers defined by Kumar and Han (2005) and Xu and Payne (2009) provide the envelope within which each sample is placed, and the aspect ratio contours between those limits separate the pore classes. Each pore system is normalized within its class to decompose total porosity into comparable fractions, which enter the models as engineered features alongside the pore classes carried through training and testing as categorical classifiers. The secondary porosity index and the pore aspect ratio emerge as the strongest permeability indicators of those tested, and both outrank porosity alone. Seventy two models were developed across multiple algorithm families, with the best realization of each retained for comparison. Held out testing returns R2 between 0.71 and 0.95, the wireline responses being reconstructed most accurately and the rock properties limited by genuine reservoir heterogeneity rather than by model capability. Because the reference population spans the full multilayer succession rather than a single interval, the models carry that layered behavior into every well to which they are applied. Deployment to a new well requires only environmental correction and normalization to the Master Well reference distributions, with no additional core. Accuracy is expected to hold within the calibration envelope of approximately 20 km lateral extent defined by the present dataset, beyond which predictions should be treated as indicative until confirmed against local core.

Beyond Binary: How Single-Transit Photonic Resonance and Dynamic Quantum Biophysics Solves the Decoherence Barrier ABSTRACT This work synthesizes a fundamental paradigm shift in computational physics and hardware architecture, uniting non-binary photonic wave interference with lossless, open-system quantum dynamics. Traditional computational paradigms suffer from two foundational bottlenecks: binary CMOS architecture bound by Landauer switching heat and von Neumann memory bus latency, and quantum simulations constrained by exponential Hilbert-space state blowup and environmental decoherence. Here, we establish the mathematical and physical foundations of Discrete Lattice Resonance Architecture (DLRA) operating alongside the Classical Quantum Biophysics (CQBP) simulation framework. 1. Photonic Substrate and Non-Binary Instruction Set We present a fixed-topology, single-transit optical core inscribed within an \\(\alpha\\)-quartz dielectric lattice containing core-shell Schottky junction nodes. Logic execution shifts from binary (1/0) gate switching to the continuous Octad Instruction Tuple \\(\Theta = (A, \phi_1, \phi_2, \phi_3, \phi_4, \sigma, \chi, \omega)\\), parameterizing pulse amplitude, quadrant phases, duration, polarization, and spatial carrier frequencies. Global matrix-vector transformations (\\(10^{12}\\) MACs/transit) execute within a single 21-picosecond optical transit (\\(\tau \approx 21\text{ ps}\\)), eliminating transistor gate-switching heat and memory bus delays entirely. 2. Strict Conservation Laws: Fourth-order Runge-Kutta (RK4) and exact matrix exponential solvers explicitly enforce matrix Hermiticity \\(0.5(\rho + \rho^\dagger)\\) and unit trace normalization \\(\text{Tr}(\rho) = 1.0\\) at every integration step, preventing unphysical probability leakage or floating-point norm drift. Entropy Generation Kinetics: Real-time diagnostic observers measure quantum state populations \\(\rho_{ii}\\), off-diagonal coherences \\(\rho_{ij}\\), state purity \\(\text{Tr}(\rho^2)\\), and von Neumann entropy \\(S = -\text{Tr}(\rho \ln \rho)\\) with numerical zero-eigenvalue filtering (\\(\lambda > 10^{-10}\\)). Path Integral Discretization: Quantum statistical mechanics and zero-point thermal delocalization (mean radius of gyration \\(R_g = 0.0524\text{ nm}\\) at \\(T = 300\text{ K}\\)) are computed deterministically via ring-polymer bead chains (`PathIntegral`). 4. Quantitative Benchmarks & Physical Analysis Validation across a \\(3.0\text{ ps}\\) trajectory under a \\(1.5\text{ THz}\\) vibrational drive demonstrates real-time vibrational sideband imprinting onto off-diagonal quantum coherences \\(|\rho_{01}(t)|\\), thermal equilibration at \\(101.30\text{ K}\\), population relaxation to ground state \\(\rho_{00} = 0.5263\\) and excited state \\(\rho_{11} = 0.4737\\), and monotonic entropy stabilization at \\(S = 0.5789\\) with zero trace drift (\\(\text{Error} < 10^{-16}\\)). By uniting single-transit wave-interference hardware with zero-drift open quantum software, this framework replaces continuous differential singularities with exact discrete algebra, providing an exascale computational foundation for room-temperature quantum biology, bio-photonic engineering, and hardware security. Beyond Raw Data: Implementing An Automated Multiphase Flow Meter (MPFM) Validation and Diagnostic Workflow for Enhanced Measurement Integrity

R. Mohan, S. Samajpati, Liliya Kunakbayeva, Aloysius Heru Danardatu, M. Mokhlis — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/236197-ms

Abstract In modern oil and gas operations, Multiphase Flow Meters (MPFMs) have become indispensable for applications ranging from single-well production metering to fiscal allocation. Yet the reliability of the high-frequency data they produce remains a persistent operational vulnerability. Engineers frequently receive well-test results without visibility into whether the meter operated inside its calibrated envelope, whether the test environment was truly stable, or whether the reported rates are physically consistent with the rest of the production system. This opacity erodes confidence in MPFM technology and introduces risk into reservoir management and production-optimization decisions. This paper presents an automated MPFM validation and diagnostic workflow developed as a foundational component of ADNOC's Artificial Intelligence Production System Optimization (AiPSO) initiative. Within the broader AiPSO engineering-driven feedback-loop architecture, the workflow continuously monitors meter health and operating conditions, identifies statistically stable test periods, and normalizes in-situ rates to a common Path-to-Surface reference. Only those tests that satisfy rigorous integrity criteria are released downstream, feeding automated well-test validation, model calibration, production allocation, and optimization workflows. By converting raw multiphase sensor streams into auditable, exception-based insights, the approach enables true "management by exception": engineers engage only with high-quality, validated well-test data. Field experience demonstrates faster detection of underperforming wells, earlier identification of meter or PVT-model drift, and a measurable reduction in the engineering effort required to qualify well-test data, establishing a practical foundation for higher-confidence digital-twin workflows across diverse asset environments.

Correlating Stimulation Efficiency and Lateral Petrophysical Variability Through the Use of a Novel Normalized Treating Pressure Approach for Unconventional Wells

R. S. Alghamdi, A. M. Alowaid, T. S. Altayyar — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/236329-ms

Abstract Although treating pressure is a frequently used parameter to qualitatively evaluate stimulation placement, relying solely on treating pressure can be misleading, as it fails to account for stage-to-stage variations in friction components such as pipe friction and near-wellbore friction, in addition to the natural variations induced by changes in petrophysical properties at each stimulated area. The objective of this paper is to introduce a novel normalized treating pressure (NTP) approach that overcomes such limitations, enabling a standardized comparison of all fractured stages in a horizontal lateral. The study then correlates that newly-established parameter, with raw petrophysical logs to investigate variabilities along the lateral and assess their impact on the overall stimulation treatment. The methodology outlined in this paper centers on estimating a normalized treating pressure, which ensures consistency across all evaluated hydraulic fracturing stages within an ultra-low permeability unconventional horizontal well. This normalized pressure represents the equivalent treating pressure if the friction reducer (FR) concentration was maintained constantly at 0.5 gallons-per-thousand (gpt), effectively converting excess friction reduction into a comparable pressure value. The normalized treating pressure was then graphed alongside raw petrophysical logs, including GR and bulk density across all stages of the horizontal lateral. The evaluation of these plots identified correlations between stimulation parameters and petrophysical logs, serving as a foundation for further investigation, enabling a more detailed correlation between specific stimulation parameters, such as perforation friction and instantaneous shut-in pressure (ISIP), and raw petrophysical data. This subsequent analysis provided an underlying physical explanation for minor variations in stimulation treatment across all stages of the horizontal lateral. A comprehensive evaluation was conducted on three independent unconventional horizontal wells, utilizing the aforementioned methodology. To ensure a fair comparison, the stimulation parameters were kept constant across the lateral of each well. Outcomes of the study indicated a correlation between stimulation parameters and petrophysical variability. Two of the wells exhibited relatively notable changes in GR and density logs, which were accompanied by variations in normalized treating pressure and perforation friction. This suggests that the variations in petrophysical properties had a profound impact on fracture initiation and geometry. In contrast, the third well had relatively stable petrophysical logs along its horizontal lateral, which was mirrored by stable stimulation parameters. The novelty of this study lies in introducing a practical normalization technique that enhances the diagnostic value of readily-available treating pressure data, enabling a more reliable prediction of stimulation performance along unconventional horizontal wells. This approach has the potential to serve as a predictive tool identifying potential problematic stages, inform stage-level design adjustments, and improve the overall stimulation efficiency of unconventional reservoirs.

Cell IO: a database linking single-cell transcriptomes to literature-derived knowledge of cell functions and perturbations

Haruka Ozaki — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11558063/v1

Abstract Single-cell transcriptome databases tell us which genes a cell type expresses, but what a cell type does, and what happens to it when a gene is perturbed, remains scattered across the literature. Cell IO (https://cell-io.riken.jp/) links these two kinds of knowledge through Cell Ontology identifiers. Its transcriptome layer re-processes 10x Chromium single- cell and single-nucleus RNA-seq experiments of human, mouse, and rat from the Sequence Read Archive with a uniform pipeline, annotates clusters with Cell Ontology terms restricted by the tissue of origin, and stores the cell type composition and UMAP embedding of every experiment (currently 14,931 experiments and 82.9 million cells). Its literature layer holds 895,258 claims extracted with a large language model and verified in a second pass from open-access abstracts, review articles, and gene perturbation studies in immunology and neuroscience, each with the verbatim sentence, highlighted entity spans, evidence strength, and ontology-normalized cell, gene, and context. The web interface lets users search cell types by name, synonym, or identifier, find the experiments in which a cell type is most abundant, inspect the composition and embedding of each experiment, read and filter the claims about a cell type by knowledge base, direction, evidence strength, and organism, and identify cell types whose functional characterization is missing from part of the literature. Cell IO is freely available without registration.

Enhancing 3D Static Geological Models Using Unsupervised Machine Learning (IPSOM) for Rock Typing: A North Oman Case Study

M. S. Al Badi, A. Al Harthi, A. Das, Selma E. Huisman, B. Al Habsi — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/235749-ms

Abstract Reliable rock typing is fundamental to static reservoir modelling, but conventional methods often struggle in thin-bedded, heterogeneous clastic reservoirs where subtle lithological changes cannot be fully captured from core data alone. In the Shammar reservoir of North Oman, this limitation added significant uncertainty to facies distribution, petrophysical modelling, and hydrocarbon volume estimates, with a direct impact on field development planning. Conventional deterministic workflows often grouped intervals with different flow characteristics into the same rock class because the available data could not distinguish subtle differences in reservoir quality. As development progressed, this reduced confidence in the static model and pointed to the need for a more objective way to represent reservoir heterogeneity while staying consistent with geological observations and production behaviour. This study presents a workflow that applies an Iterative Probabilistic Self-Organizing Map (IPSOM), an unsupervised machine learning technique, to classify electrofacies from standardized well-log data. The workflow combines quality-controlled wireline logs, core measurements, and production data to generate data-driven rock types, which were then checked against core observations, Production Logging Tool (PLT) data, and dynamic field performance. Before clustering, the datasets were quality-controlled, environmentally corrected, depth-matched, and normalized to ensure consistency across the field. Principal Component Analysis (PCA) was used to assess how much each logging parameter contributed to the clustering and to identify which variables gave the best discrimination between electrofacies. The resulting electrofacies were built into a three-dimensional static geological model to improve facies distribution, petrophysical modelling, reservoir characterization, and uncertainty assessment, while keeping the model consistent with dynamic production behaviour. The workflow identified three electrofacies: Excellent Rock, Good Rock, and Non-Reservoir. Compared with the previous modelling approach, the IPSOM-derived facies gave a better representation of vertical heterogeneity and reservoir continuity and reduced interpreter bias. Facies-dependent saturation-height functions increased the Base Case STOIIP estimate by 4.3%, improved the match with historical production, and made it easier to identify reservoir flow units and bypassed pay. The updated model also supported ranking of future infill drilling opportunities with greater confidence. Independent checks showed good agreement between the electrofacies classification, core descriptions, PLT observations, and production history, confirming that the electrofacies reflected real geological differences rather than statistical clustering alone. Wells intersecting a larger proportion of the Excellent Rock facies consistently showed higher injectivity and better production performance, adding further confidence in the workflow. The study shows that IPSOM offers a practical, repeatable, and scalable way to classify electrofacies in heterogeneous clastic reservoirs. Combining machine learning with conventional geological interpretation, petrophysics, and production data improves the reliability of static models, reduces uncertainty in reservoir characterization, and supports better-informed development decisions. The method is not intended to replace geological workflows but to give them a more objective, data-driven basis for combining multiple subsurface datasets. It is readily transferable to other mature clastic reservoirs where conventional rock typing cannot capture fine-scale heterogeneity.

Unsaturated fatty acid metabolism and FADS2 as candidate factors in triple-negative breast cancer

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Ying Chen, Yu-Hui Ma, Zhi-hui Hu, Qi Liu, Bin Song — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.36922/cp026340053

Triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) is aggressive and lacks effective targeted therapies. Using estrogen receptor (ER)-negative breast cancer as a proxy for TNBC, we explored circulating metabolite associations and downstream candidate genes. A two-sample Mendelian randomization (MR) analysis used large-scale metabolome genome-wide association study data (~1,400 metabolites). Candidate metabolites underwent Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes enrichment. Computationally predicted targets were intersected with common differentially expressed genes from three Gene Expression Omnibus datasets and Weighted Gene Co-expression Network Analysis modules. Least absolute shrinkage and selection operator-Cox and random survival forest prioritized genes in the Cancer Genome Atlas–TNBC. Single-cell RNA-seq (GSE176078) explored heterogeneity and pathways. MR identified 38 candidate metabolites nominally associated with ER-negative breast cancer risk, enriched in unsaturated fatty acid and arachidonic acid metabolism. Integration yielded 39 candidate genes, and survival-based machine learning prioritized KRAS, MGST3, FADS2, UGT8, IDO1, and SRC. Single-cell analysis showed higher FADS2 in a presumed malignant epithelial subpopulation (log2FC = 2.53, false discovery rate < 0.05), associated with cholesterol biosynthesis, sterol metabolism, and cell-cycle programs. These exploratory findings nominate abnormal circulating unsaturated fatty acid metabolism and FADS2 as candidate factors in ER-negative/TNBC biology. FADS2 is a candidate biomarker and potential target requiring TNBC-specific genetic, patient-aware single-cell, prognostic, and experimental validation.

Exploring copper toxicity mechanisms in Magnetospirillum gryphiswaldense MSR1 using transcriptomics

Josh Bond, Vesna Najdanovic‐Visak, Matthew Carlile, Graeme Fox, Tim William Overton — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12864-026-13477-1

Abstract Background Magnetospirillum gryphiswaldense MSR1 biomineralizes iron into chains of magnetic nanoparticles called magnetosomes, yet remains vulnerable to copper (Cu) stress. Here, we present the first transcriptomic characterisation of the MSR1’s response to Cu, identifying coordinated changes associated with metal homeostasis, redox control and metabolic restructuring. Results Exposure to 5 ppm Cu halted exponential-phase growth within 3 h, was accompanied by an altered cellular magnetic response and reduced the proportion of healthy cells from 99 to 48%, indicating substantial membrane damage. Differential expression analysis was centred on a time-matched Cu-stress comparison, which identified 113 upregulated and 8 downregulated differentially expressed genes (DEGs; shrunken log fold change > |2 | ; Benjamini-Hochberg (BH) adjusted p- value < 1E-20). Across the full analysis, 28 genes of interest were discussed, with additional genes used for contextual support. Strong upregulation was observed in metal-handling systems including dmeRF , zntA1 and czcAB . Previous detection of CzcA in the magnetosome membrane raises the possibility that Cu handling may intersect with magnetosome-associated processes, although transcriptomic association does not establish a direct functional connection. Additional induction of redox-associated genes, including wrbA , and sulfur assimilation pathways, including cysIH , fccAB , ssuA , cysK , metZ and eamA , suggests increased demand for thiol and redox-active metabolites. Co-activation of Arsenic (As)-associated resistance genes indicated transcriptional overlap between metal-stress pathways. Conversely, genes associated with chemotaxis, respiratory activity, genome maintenance and cell envelope biosynthesis were downregulated, reflecting metabolic reallocation toward detoxification and maintenance. Gene Ontology (GO) analysis supported these trends, showing enrichment of upregulated functions associated with the TCA cycle, electron transport and protein folding, alongside enrichment of downregulated glycosyltransferase activity. Conclusions Together, these findings establish a transcriptomic framework for the Cu-stress response in MSR1 and identify candidate genes and pathways for investigating Cu tolerance and testing whether Cu stress influences magnetosome-associated processes.

Influence of dielectric environment on the optical properties of the CVD-grown monolayer WSe2

Xinxin Yue, CHEN Lenan, Yingjie Huo, Jingchao Liu, Xiang Chen — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0344968

Regulating excitonic emission in two-dimensional (2D) semiconductors through external perturbations offers an effective route to improving the performance of their optoelectronic devices. Here, we investigate the evolution of exciton and trion emissions in monolayer tungsten diselenide (WSe2) under different dielectric environments using temperature-dependent Raman and photoluminescence (PL) spectroscopy. Through deconvolution of the PL spectra, it is observed that both low temperature and high-dielectric environment facilitate the formation of trions, leading to an increase in their integrated intensity compared with that of the neutral excitons. This phenomenon can be attributed to the higher trion binding energy at low temperature and the dielectric-induced deep-to-shallow transition of defect levels, which modifies the effective charged defect levels in WSe2 and consequently influences the formation and recombination dynamics of the excitons and trions differently. Our results provide deeper insight into the emission mechanisms of excitonic complexes in 2D semiconductors under dielectric environments and offer useful guidance for dielectric engineering of optoelectronic and photonic devices based on transition metal dichalcogenides.

Propose-Validate-Correct Shadow-Plant Architecture for Safe AI Agents in Process Operations

M. I. Khan, M. Khanna, A. Shivkumar, T. Sharma, A. Sakala — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/235393-ms

Abstract Large-language-model (LLM) agents are increasingly explored for autonomous process-plant operations, yet they can propose physically unsafe control actions due to hallucination and imprecise numerical reasoning. We introduce the Propose-Validate-Correct (PVC) architecture, a framework-agnostic safety layer in which every agent-proposed action is first simulated in a shadow plant, a lightweight physics-grounded process simulator, before reaching the real process. When the shadow simulation detects constraint violations, the proposal is rejected and the agent receives structured feedback for correction. PVC is an advisory safety layer and does not replace certified safety instrumented systems (SIS). Experiments are conducted on OpsEnv, an open-source continuous stirred-tank reactor (CSTR) benchmark featuring mass and energy balance ordinary differential equations (ODEs), Arrhenius reaction kinetics, and proportional-integral cooling control. Four agent configurations (ReAct and Plan-and-Execute, each with and without PVC) are evaluated on four LLMs: Google Gemini 2.5 Flash (cloud), Alibaba Qwen 2.5 32B, Meta Llama 3.1 8B, and Mistral 7B (all three local via Ollama). The evaluation spans 440 experiment cells organized in four tiers covering eleven scenarios, with three feedback granularity levels (binary, descriptive, suggested fix) for shadow-equipped agents. Both shadow-equipped setups record zero actual violations in all 282 PVC cells, while react_direct accumulates 10.4 mean violations per episode (Tiers 1–3) and plan_exec_direct incurs 0.2. Across all tiers, PVC intercepts 1,552 proposals that would have violated safety constraints, predominantly in upset scenarios where every agent proposal at every timestep triggers a constraint violation. In optimization scenarios (A-series), the correction loop salvages 40.3% of rejected proposals (Self-Correction Success Rate, SCSR), with descriptive per-variable feedback improving SCSR 3.0× over binary reject/accept for matched Gemini Flash cells (50.9% vs. 16.7%). In upset scenarios (B-series), SCSR is 0% under the current stability threshold; PVC acts as a conservative safety gate during disturbed states. Completion scores show a negligible trade-off (Cohen's d = −0.053, 95% CI [−0.283, 0.174]): no statistically significant performance cost is observed, though formal equivalence remains undemonstrated at the current sample size. Plan-and-Execute with PVC runs 31× faster than ReAct with PVC (60 s vs. 1,835 s for Gemini Flash). Shadow-plant validation is a viable safety mechanism for deploying LLM agents in safety-critical process environments; structured physics-based feedback markedly improves self-correction in optimization scenarios. AI-assisted tools were used in the experimental evaluation; all outputs were reviewed and validated by the authors.

Predictable Execution Through Quantitative Evidence of Digital Unification Across a Multi-Billion-Dollar LNG Megaproject

Y. C. Srivastava, C. Granata, S. Singh — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2118/235810-ms

Abstract Objectives Large LNG megaprojects continue to experience schedule overruns and execution variability due to fragmented workflows, disconnected engineering and construction information, and inconsistent visibility of evolving execution conditions. This study evaluates how a Digital Construction Block-enabled execution framework can connect project information across hierarchical work packages and support more context-rich execution management within a live LNG construction environment. Methods, Procedures, Process The study examined operational reporting populations and historical analytical records generated at different stages of execution. The retained evidence included more than 4,000 Installation Work Packages spanning Civil, Mechanical, and Piping scopes, together with wider Construction Work Package reporting, engineering-release information, IWP status, piping and material records, and planned and forecast schedule information. Digital Construction Blocks were used as structured execution units connecting Engineering Work Packages, Construction Work Packages, and Installation Work Packages with associated engineering, package-status, material, and schedule information. The analysis examined whether aggregated management indicators retained traceability to identifiable work packages and whether planned and forecast schedule conditions could be interpreted in relation to the engineering and construction scope potentially affected. Results, Observations, Conclusions The case-study results demonstrate that the digital execution environment established hierarchical visibility across substantial engineering and installation work-package populations. Engineering-release and IWP-status information could be consolidated into management and leadership views while retaining relationships with underlying areas, disciplines, Construction Work Packages, Installation Work Package types, and individual packages. Discipline-specific piping analytics also connected line-level scope with bill-of-materials, procurement, delivery, and allocation information. Planned-versus-forecast schedule reporting associated engineering forecast timing with planned and forecast Construction Work Package execution dates, enabling potential schedule exposure to be examined against identifiable scope. However, differences between live operational populations and retained historical datasets, together with incomplete actual dates and man-hour records, prevented defensible project-wide normalization of planned-versus-actual performance or causal attribution of execution outcomes. Novel Information This paper provides field-based evidence of a Digital Construction Block-enabled information architecture operating at LNG-megaproject scale. Unlike approaches focused primarily on standalone dashboards or consolidated reporting, the framework preserves relationships between engineering information, hierarchical work packaging, package status, material information, and planned and forecast schedule conditions. The case demonstrates how this connected structure can support execution traceability and context-rich management visibility while identifying data governance as a prerequisite for reliable advanced analytics. The architecture also provides a foundation for future readiness assessment, package-centric constraint management, quality analytics, mobile field reporting, and machine-assisted decision support, although these additional capabilities were not deployed or evaluated on the case-study project.

The Fold of Pride Status-Gated Attribution, the Negative-Attribution Regime, and the Identifiability of Channel-Gated Agents

Qinfu Li — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23863669

Abstract Traditional ethics places pride first among the sins because it is the most hidden and the hardest to repent of. This paper writes the computable side of these two reasons as a minimal model: the agent’s belief bias x is updated by an asymmetric rule, and the share a of failures that are booked falls exponentially with the height of the agent’s public commitment, a(x) = a₀·e^{−χx} + a_ext, where χ is the number of nats by which the cost of admission rises per noise unit of claimed overstatement, and a_ext is an external channel not gated by status. First, a threshold theorem. Under Gaussian feedback, let h(x) be the restoring elasticity, the negative log-slope of the ratio of expected upward and downward deviations. The paper proves that h is even, strictly increasing on the positive half-line, and satisfies h(x) ≥ √(2π)/σ with equality only at x = 0 (Theorem 1). Hence under exponential gating a saddle-node with hysteresis appears if and only if χ > √(2π)/σ; near the threshold the fold location scales as (χ − χ_c)^{1/2} and the width of the bistable window as (χ − χ_c)^{3/2}, with closed-form coefficients in which π − 3 appears. Second, two versions of gating side by side. Symmetric gating gives two mirror wells—a pride branch and a self-abasement branch—with a bistable window symmetric about a₀ = 1 and an upper fold strictly above 1: once the fold is crossed, returning to “fully honest booking at zero commitment” is not enough to leave the pride branch. One-sided gating is more pessimistic: above the threshold, the pride branch is the unique attractor whenever a₀ ≤ 1, the lower edge of the bistable window is a non-smooth corner at the onset of gating rather than a saddle-node, and the low well is shallow; in simulation, at a₀ = 1.15 every replica falls onto the pride branch. There are only two ways out: temporary over-booking (a₀ > 1), or a sufficiently strong ungated external channel. At χ = 3, a_ext ≥ 0.065 suffices to leave at the honest baseline, and for a_ext ≥ 0.164 the fold disappears. Third, heavy-tailed feedback. The existence of the threshold is a property of light tails. Under Laplace and Student-t feedback, h attains its maximum rather than its minimum at zero, and the geometry flips: instead of two bounded wells there is a calibrated well plus an unbounded creep of pride. Under t feedback any positive gating suffices to produce this creep, and no baseline a₀ removes it; the creep rate is set by the tail of the feedback, x ~ t^{1/ν} under t(ν) (simulated slopes 0.303 and 0.189 against theoretical 1/3 and 1/5). Adding a_ext makes the pride branch bounded again and restores bistability. Under heavy tails the external channel changes from “helpful” to “a necessary condition for boundedness”. Fourth, the negative-attribution regime. Between a = 0 and a < 0 there is a boundary of sign, not of degree: at a = 0 belief creeps upward ever more slowly, while for a < 0 it runs away at least as e^{α|a|t}. This writes the statement of [3]’s fifth component, that a sign flip is worse than a decline to zero, as a growth law. Fifth, a negative identifiability result and its repair. The second scope clause of The Law of Action [4] separates estimation problems from value problems by asking whether stating the probabilities clearly would change the choice. For an agent with a → 0, this test classifies it as a value case with probability one: the Kullback–Leibler divergence between the observation distributions under the two hypotheses is identically zero. A two-sided probe on the same channel separates them by the asymmetry index Γ = (1 − a)/(1 + a), and Γ is precisely the order parameter of the model in the first part. Six falsifiable predictions are registered, the sharpest being the threshold prediction: in populations where the elasticity of the probability of admitting error with respect to the size of public commitment exceeds √(2π)/σ, the cross-sectional distribution of Γ should be bimodal. Number of executions on external data: zero.

Predictors of Shingles Vaccine Hesitancy and Potential Interventions: Findings from a Cross-Sectional Study in Makkah.

Aeshah Hamdan Almutairi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.7417/ct.2026.2145

Background: Vaccination uptake for shingles remains low in many populations, influenced by various demographic, social, and psychological factors. Objectives: The study aimed to assess the relationship between demographic variables and vaccination status, evaluate participants' awareness of the shingles vaccine, identify barriers to vaccination, and simulate intervention strategies to predict their potential impact on vaccination uptake. Methods: A cross-sectional descriptive study design was employed, utilizing a convenience sample of 385 participants aged 18 and older from healthcare facilities and community centers in Makkah. Data were collected through a structured self-administered questionnaire covering demographics, vaccination status, awareness levels, barriers to vaccination, and sentiments related to the vaccine. Cluster analysis revealed distinct groups based on barriers, with those facing cultural barriers and lacking awareness showing the lowest likelihood of vaccination.
