NEXT-GENERATION ADVANCEMENTS IN POLYMERIC MICRONEEDLES AND NANO CARRIER-MEDIATED TRANSDERMAL DRUG DELIVERY SYSTEMS

Manav Upadhyay*, Dr. Nidhi Chauhan, Yash Sheth, Het Patel, Ayush Mallik, Azba Patel — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23757002

Transdermal drug delivery systems (TDDS) provide several advantages over conventional oral and parenteral routes, including avoidance of gastrointestinal degradation and hepatic first-pass metabolism, improved patient compliance, and controlled drug delivery. However, the stratum corneum acts as a major barrier, limiting the passive transport of hydrophilic and high-molecular-weight therapeutic agents. This review focuses on recent advances in polymeric microneedles and nanocarrier-mediated transdermal drug delivery systems developed to overcome these skin-permeation limitations. Literature from PubMed and Scopus was reviewed with emphasis on microneedle classification, polymeric biomaterials, nanocarrier systems, drug-loading capacity, permeation mechanisms, safety, and clinical translation. Polymeric microneedles, including solid, coated, dissolving, hydrogel-forming, and hollow systems, enable controlled and minimally painful delivery by creating temporary microchannels through the stratum corneum. Nanocarriers such as liposomes, ethosomes, solid lipid nanoparticles, nanostructured lipid carriers, polymeric nanoparticles, and inorganic nanoparticles further enhance dermal and transdermal drug transport through lipid fluidization, follicular pathways, and controlled drug deposition. The integration of these technologies provides promising opportunities for delivering macromolecules, vaccines, and localized therapeutics. Future developments involving stimuli-responsive systems, wearable biosensors, artificial intelligence, microfluidics, and personalized manufacturing may further enhance transdermal therapy. Despite challenges related to manufacturing scalability, regulatory standardization, and long-term biocompatibility, advanced microneedle and nanocarrier platforms have considerable potential for clinical translation and personalized drug delivery. Background: Traditional transdermal drug delivery systems (TDDS) provide a non-invasive, patient-compliant route that avoids gastrointestinal degradation and hepatic first-pass metabolism. However, the impermeable lipid matrix of the stratum corneum strictly limits the passive transport of molecules to those possessing specific hydrophobic profiles and low molecular weights. Objective: This comprehensive review examines contemporary physical and chemical breakthrough technologies, with specific focus on polymeric microneedles (MNs) and nanocarrier-facilitated delivery platforms engineered to overcome these macro-molecular transport constraints. Methodology: A systematic screening of literature from the PubMed and Scopus databases (spanning foundational models through 2026) was conducted to evaluate the mechanical structural properties, biopolymer selections, and systemic pharmacokinetics of advanced skin-permeation platforms. Results: Polymeric microneedles (dissolving, hydrogel-forming, and stimuli-responsive types) demonstrate precise disruption of the stratum corneum without contacting dermally located pain-sensitive nerve endings. Concurrently, lipidic, polymeric, and inorganic nanocarriers capitalize on hair follicles and transient lipid fluidization to deposit high-molecular-weight biologics, vaccines, and localized therapeutics. Conclusion: Combining active mechanical microneedles with target-specific nanocarriers represents the vanguard of modern personalized diagnostics and therapeutics, bridging laboratory innovation with clinical scale-up paradigms.

A REVIEW ON REPURPOSED DRUGS IN COVID-19 MANAGEMENT: A PHARMACEUTICAL AND CLINICAL PERSPECTIVE

Kamidi Sunitha*1, Mortha Greeshma Sree2, Pilli Durga Malleswari2, Pabbineedi Sai Kumari2, — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23798140

The COVID-19 pandemic, caused by SARS-CoV-2 in late 2019, created an urgent need for immediate treatments in the absence of specific vaccines and antivirals, making drug repurposing the most feasible strategy. This review evaluates the clinical utility of repurposed therapeutics for COVID-19 and emphasizes the vital role of pharmacy professionals. Initially, treatment was based on in-vitro evidence using antivirals, antimalarials, and antibiotics; however, as understanding of the disease progressed, management became more targeted. Antivirals like remdesivir and nirmatrelvir/ritonavir were used to control early viral replication, while immunomodulators such as dexamethasone, tocilizumab, and baricitinib proved life-saving in severe cases by controlling hyperinflammation. Additionally, anticoagulants addressed the thrombotic complications and monoclonal antibodies benefited high-risk outpatients. Furthermore, the review focuses on the safety, adverse effects, and the significant contributions of clinical pharmacists in dose optimization, drug interaction management, and pharmacovigilance, alongside pharmaceutical scientists who ensured formulation and supply chain stability. Despite challenges like the early use of ineffective drugs and inequitable global access, adaptive trials provided rapid evidence. Ultimately, lessons from COVID-19 repurposing offer a valuable blueprint for future pandemics through prepared drug libraries, AI-based screening, and permanent trial networks to build a more resilient healthcare system.

FACTORS ASSOCIATED WITH INCOMPLETE ROUTINE IMMUNIZATION AMONG CHILDREN UNDER FIVE YEARS

2Dr. Maraj Abdulsattar Mohammed 1*Dr. Salah Hadi Salih — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23803876

Background: Routine childhood immunization is one of the most effective public health interventions for preventing vaccine-preventable diseases and reducing childhood morbidity and mortality. However, incomplete immunization remains a significant challenge, particularly in populations affected by socioeconomic disadvantage and barriers to healthcare access. Objectives: To assess the prevalence of incomplete routine immunization and identify factors associated with incomplete immunization among children under five years of age in Al-Madaen, Baghdad Governorate, Iraq. Methods: A hospital-based cross-sectional study was conducted at Al-Madaen General Hospital from 15 April 2025 to 30 September 2026. The study included 921 children under five years of age. Data were collected using a structured questionnaire and review of available vaccination records. Information included sociodemographic characteristics, caregiver knowledge and attitudes, vaccination practices, and accessibility of immunization services. Data were analyzed using IBM SPSS Statistics version 31. Categorical variables were summarized as frequencies and percentages, and associations were assessed using appropriate statistical tests. Binary logistic regression was proposed to identify factors associated with incomplete immunization, with statistical significance defined as a P value of less than 0.05. Results: Among the 921 children, 276 (30.0%) were classified as incompletely immunized, whereas 645 (70.0%) were classified as completely immunized for their age. Incomplete immunization was more frequent among children aged 36–47 months than among those younger than 12 months (33.3% versus 22.7%). Higher proportions were also observed among rural children compared with urban children (35.9% versus 26.3%), and among children of fifth birth order or higher compared with those of first or second birth order (39.8% versus 24.4%). Incomplete immunization was more frequent among children whose mothers had primary education or less than among those whose mothers had higher education (42.0% versus 18.4%). Similarly, the proportion was higher among children from low-income households than among those from adequate-income households (43.5% versus 19.4%). Poor caregiver knowledge, greater distance from vaccination facilities, reported vaccine supply interruptions, and absence of appointment reminders were also associated with higher proportions of incomplete immunization in the proposed results. In multivariable model, poor caregiver knowledge had the highest adjusted odds ratio (2.24; 95% confidence interval: 1.61–3.12). Conclusions: Incomplete routine immunization represented a potential public health concern in the study population. Caregiver knowledge, maternal education, household income, geographical accessibility, vaccine availability, and appointment follow-up may be important determinants of vaccination completion. Strengthening caregiver education, improving vaccine supply continuity, and implementing reminder and follow-up systems may help improve routine childhood immunization.

SCALABLE DOWNSTREAM PROCESS FOR PURIFICATION AND INACTIVATION OF MICROCARRIER-PROPAGATED JAPANESE ENCEPHALITIS VIRUS ANTIGEN: PROCESS DEVELOPMENT AND VALIDATION

Pavan Kumar Chodavarapu1, Prudhvi Chand Mallepaddi1,2*, Sudhakar Podha1* — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23842427

Japanese encephalitis is a mosquito-borne viral disease of major public-health importance in Asia, and vaccination remains the principal strategy for disease prevention because no specific curative therapy is available for this disease. Inactivated whole-virion vaccines require robust and reproducible manufacturing processes that ensure adequate viral yield, efficient impurity clearance, and reliable viral inactivation for safe product release into the market. The current manuscript describes an integrated process for development, purification, and inactivation of Japanese encephalitis viral antigen propagated in microcarrier-based Vero cell culture and its validation. The upstream platform employed Vero cells expanded on Cytodex 1 microcarriers in a controlled bioreactor environment to support reproducible cell growth and virus production. The viral harvests were concentrated by tangential flow filtration, purified by Celufine Sulfate affinity chromatography, further processed through Sartobind Q membrane filtration for reduction of host-cell DNA, and subsequently inactivated with formaldehyde under defined conditions. The downstream process was assessed across the three independent batches of viral antigen using plaque assay, protein estimation, host-cell protein analysis, host-cell DNA testing, and in vitro and in vivo inactivation confirmation. The workflow consistently generated purified viral antigen with acceptable impurity reduction and complete loss of detectable infectivity after inactivation. Bioreactor harvests yielded higher virus titers than static cultures, and the downstream processing remained reproducible across three batches prepared using the described workflow. Collectively, these findings support the suitability of the proposed platform for scalable manufacture of purified and inactivated JEV antigen for vaccine applications.

RETHINKING EXTRACTABLES AND LEACHABLES RISK FOR ADVANCED THERAPEUTIC MEDICINAL PRODUCTS (ATMPS)

Pallab Mandal*1 Priyabrata Pattnaik1 — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23842702

Extractables and leachables (E&L) science has matured over three decades around a relatively narrow product archetype: small-molecule drugs, monoclonal antibodies, and conventional vaccines formulated in stable, well-characterized matrices and assessed primarily against systemic toxicological endpoints. Advanced Therapeutic Medicinal Products (ATMPs) — CAR-T and other engineered cell therapies, in vivo and ex vivo gene therapies, CRISPR-based editing products, exosomes and other extracellular vesicles, mRNA-lipid nanoparticle (LNP) constructs, organoids, and complex cell culture media — do not fit this archetype. Their critical quality attributes are not merely chemical purity and systemic safety margins but biological function: potency, transfection or transduction efficiency, immune quiescence or activation, genome-editing fidelity, and structural integrity of nanoscale or supramolecular assemblies. This review critically examines whether the toxicological logic underpinning current E&L thresholds — the Threshold of Toxicological Concern (TTC), Permitted Daily Exposure (PDE), and Analytical Evaluation Threshold (AET) — remains scientifically defensible for living-cell and nanoparticulate products, and proposes a next-generation, function-first risk assessment framework tailored to the biology of ATMPs. The Cancer Policy Lab Model: Strengthening Health Systems for Evidence-Informed Human Papillomavirus Vaccination Policy in Uganda

Annet Nakaganda, Gertrude Tumuhairwe, Aggrey Tumwiine, Pauline Nalweyiso, Bpharm — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-14600

PURPOSE To establish and implement the Cancer Policy Lab (CPL) model as a structured platform to strengthen national health system capacity for translating global evidence into actionable cancer control policy and practice in Uganda. METHODS The CPL Uganda (CPL-U), convened by the ANANDA Centre for Cancer Research (ACCR), brought together stakeholders across different levels of the health system, including policymakers, clinicians, researchers, civil society organizations, patient representatives, and implementing partners. The Lab applied a systems-oriented approach combining evidence synthesis, multi-sector dialogue, and structured group work across four domains: policy and governance; financing and sustainability; equity and access; and implementation and health systems. The Lab followed a four-step process: (1) defining a national policy question on human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination prioritization; (2) synthesizing global and local evidence, including vaccine effectiveness, cost-efficiency, and delivery strategies; (3) facilitating structured multi-sector deliberations to assess feasibility within the Ugandan health system; and (4) co-producing policy recommendations and drafting a policy brief for decision-makers. RESULTS The Lab generated actionable recommendations to optimize HPV vaccination in Uganda and produced the first CPL policy brief on HPV vaccination prioritization for national dissemination and policy engagement. Recommendations included maintaining the single-dose HPV vaccination strategy, prioritizing girls aged 9-14 years with catch-up vaccination up to 20 years, and institutionalizing regular policy review to align with emerging evidence. Financing recommendations emphasized strengthening domestic resource mobilization through dedicated Primary Health Care budget lines, increased government co-financing, and use of national cost-benefit analysis to guide investment decisions. Equity recommendations focused on targeted outreach to underserved populations, including out-of-school girls and vulnerable groups, alongside strengthened communication strategies to improve vaccine acceptance and demand. Implementation recommendations included integrating HPV vaccination delivery across service platforms, strengthening health worker training, improving supply chain systems, reinforcing multi-sector coordination, and enhancing data systems for accountable programme scale-up. CONCLUSION Structured, facilitated multi-sector engagement enhanced ownership, feasibility, and policy relevance of recommendations. The CPL demonstrates a practical and scalable model for linking global evidence to local policy and implementation while strengthening health systems. The model has strong potential for replication across cancer control priorities and other health policy challenges in diverse settings.

Fear, trust, and safety perceptions as key drivers of parental acceptance of typhoid conjugate vaccine uptake among urban slum parents in Dhaka City Corporation,Bangladesh: a cross-sectional study

Md Rofiqur Rahman¹, Archi Shams Moitree¹, Anwarul Karim, Danny Theotonius Gomes¹, Md Rofiqur Rahman¹ — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10953147/v1

Abstract Background Typhoid fever is a public health concern in Bangladesh; among children younger than 15 years old, it contributes to two-thirds of the fatality rate. Studies show that the burden of typhoid fever is substantially higher in densely populated low-income urban settlements (slums). To address this situation, the Government of Bangladesh conducted nationwide typhoid conjugate vaccine (TCV) campaigns in October and November 2025. This study aimed to evaluate the role of parental knowledge, trust, and the relationship between immunization status and social or personal perspectives to strengthen routine immunization in the urban slum population in Dhaka city. Methods This cross-sectional study was conducted among slum dwellers, parents, and caregivers of children aged nine months to fourteen years in two city corporations of Dhaka using a structured questionnaire. The questionnaire consisted of two sections: demographic information, awareness, parental attitude, vaccination status, and Vaccine Attitude Scale (VAS). Bivariate and multivariate logistic regression analyses were conducted to investigate the relationship. Results A total of 446 parents/caregivers participated in the study. Among these participants, 69% of the children were vaccinated. Most participants believed that the TCV was safe (91%). Approximately 36% of parents expressed fear about the vaccine, with 33% fearing the side effects. In the 14-item VAS, 53% of the parents had a low score, between 0 and < 31. Participants with a low VAS score (< 31) were significantly more likely to have their children vaccinated than those with high scores. Comprehensive parental knowledge was significantly associated with higher odds of TCV uptake (aOR = 3.36; 95%CI: 1.25-9.00). Participants with the belief that the vaccine was safe (aOR = 17.3; 95% CI: 1.89-159.86) and trust in the campaign information (aOR = 15.49; 95% CI: 1.85–29.47) were significantly more likely to vaccinate their children. Conclusions Despite relatively high TCV uptake, fears and concerns related to vaccination remained important barriers among the parents and caregivers. To enhance both TCV acceptance and coverage among the underserved urban population, we need to focus on strengthening vaccine confidence through targeted risk communication and improving trust in vaccine campaigns.

Optimization of Loading and Unloading Service Performance and Facility Requirements at Sofifi Sea Port toward 2030

Merry Mardianti, I Nyoman Teguh Prasidha, Wahyu Dewanto — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.59261/bustechno.v7i5.842

Background: Sofifi Port is an important logistics distribution hub for the capital of North Maluku Province, but its service performance has not fully kept pace with increasing vessel traffic and cargo volumes. Objective: This study evaluates the optimization of cargo-handling services at Sofifi Port and formulates a phased development strategy through 2030. Methods: A convergent parallel mixed-methods design was employed, integrating operational performance analysis for 2024–2025, multiple linear regression analysis involving 195 port users, field observations, and interviews with five groups of informants. Operational indicators included berth time (BT), effective time (ET), the ET/BT ratio, waiting time (WT), cargo-handling productivity, and berth occupancy ratio (BOR). Results: BT decreased from 53.9 to 28.7 hours, ET decreased from 18.7 to 10.8 hours, and the ET/BT ratio increased from 34.7% to 37.5%. WT remained relatively stable at 18.2–18.5 minutes. However, cargo-handling productivity declined from 12.6 to 5.3 tons per hour, while BOR increased from 74% to 77%. The regression model was statistically significant, F(2, 192) = 88.720, p < 0.001, with R² = 0.480 and adjusted R² = 0.475. Physical infrastructure (β = 0.546) showed a stronger association with service performance than system administration (β = 0.411). Conclusion: Services at Sofifi Port cannot yet be considered optimal. Optimization efforts should prioritize short-term operational improvements, medium-term facility upgrades through 2030, and longer-term regional logistics integration based on a phased smart port concept.

A Secure IoT–Blockchain Framework for Vaccine Cold‐Chain Monitoring With Predictive Analytics

Murugan Lakshmanan, Saravanakumar Sengottaiyan, Joshuva Arockia Dhanraj, R. Sriramkumar — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/puh2.70431

ABSTRACT Objective The objective of this research is to design and test a framework for secure monitoring of a vaccine cold‐chain with the help of Internet of Things (IoT) sensors, blockchain‐based provenance verification, and predictive analytics. Methods The architecture of PharmaChain IoT Vault is an integrated system comprising low‐power IoT telemetry acquisition, secure Message Queuing Telemetry Transport (MQTT)‐based communication, integrity verification using SHA‐256, a permissioned blockchain infrastructure, off‐chain telemetry storage, and a forecasting module for transformers. A real‐world vaccine cold‐chain logistics (VCL) dataset of 26,674 observations was used for experimental evaluation, spanning 30 vaccine batches, 12 monitoring sites, and multiple distribution steps. To evaluate the feasibility of secure predictive monitoring under heterogeneous operational conditions, descriptive analysis and baseline forecasting evaluation, along with blockchain‐enabled provenance verification, were performed. Results There was a significant temporal variability in the dataset due to the changes in storage, transportation, and distribution conditions. Forecasting experiments using a baseline autoregressive model showed that it outperformed a persistence baseline, with lower mean absolute error (12.58°C) and root mean square error (15.15°C), thereby validating the use of vaccine logistics telemetry for predictive monitoring. The blockchain layer preserved the provenance of telemetry records in a tamper‐evident manner by anchoring their SHA‐256 hashes in the ledger, enabling cryptographic integrity verification. Conclusions It is shown that secure telemetry acquisition, blockchain‐based provenance verification, and predictive analytics can be integrated into a single framework for vaccine cold‐chain monitoring. The proposed architecture can be used to develop a system for trustworthy, intelligent management of cold chains and to illustrate the challenges of forecasting in a heterogeneous logistics environment. Future research will involve testing more complex forecasting models and testing the framework in operational conditions. Clinical Trial Registration Not applicable. This study does not involve a clinical trial.

Project Description:This dataset contains the processed single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) gene expression matrices for a preclinical study evaluating a TOPK-targeted NitraTh vaccine in the GL261 orthotopic glioblastoma (GBM) model. Study Design:A total of 3 experimental groups were sequenced. Data Content:For each group, we provide the standard filtered feature-barcode matrices generated from the 10x Genomics platform. Technical Details:The data is ready for downstream analysis using standard bioinformatics tools such as Seurat (R) or Scanpy (Python). All files are fully compressed in GZIP format (.gz) to ensure data integrity and optimize storage efficiency for high-throughput sequencing data. The raw FASTQ sequencing data have been deposited in the NCBI Sequence Read Archive (SRA) under BioProject PRJNA1550462.

A Review of Cervical Cancer Prevention Policies in Kenya and Malawi

Corrina Moucheraud, Lynette Kamau, Moreen Nkonge, Erick Muge — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-46000

PURPOSE Cervical cancer is a major public health concern, and one of the leading causes of cancer-related mortality among women in Kenya and Malawi, yet the coverage of the HPV vaccine and cervical cancer screening remains below WHO targets. This study reviewed national policies in the two countries to identify key actors, contextual factors, and policy processes informing cervical cancer prevention and recommends policy-focused strategies to improve coverage of prevention services. METHODS We analyzed 12 national documents from Kenya and 5 from Malawi, all government-issued and applicable from 2018 onward (or earlier if no updates were available). The Walt and Gilson Policy Triangle Framework guided the review. RESULTS In both cases, cervical cancer prevention through vaccination was addressed within broader policies on cancer and reproductive health. Among policies reviewed, 8 of 12 in Kenya and 2 of 5 in Malawi were outdated or had unclear operational status. Weak implementation and monitoring and evaluation guidance for HPV vaccination were also observed. In Kenya, few documents outline specific interventions to improve vaccination coverage, while Malawi falls short in integrating vaccination into existing health programs. Across the board, M&E frameworks on vaccination targets or indicators were inconsistently defined. The review also revealed limited expert input and youth involvement in the development of existing policies. CONCLUSION Cervical cancer prevention strategies require up-to-date health policies co-designed with expert and youth input to ensure relevance. Explicit guidance on implementation and robust monitoring and evaluation frameworks are key to operationalizing these policies and achieving the goal of cervical cancer elimination.

Cervical Cancer Mortality in Brazil, 2010-2023: Rising Crude Rates and a Nearly Twofold North–Southeast Gap

Eleonora Balzan Fleck, Analissa Ferraz, Manuela Carneiro de Mattos, Lucas Mazzo, Camila Cunha da Silva — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-59000

PURPOSE Cervical cancer is a preventable malignancy whose mortality can expose inequities across the cancer control continuum. In Brazil, a middle-income country with universal health coverage but marked socioeconomic/geographic disparities, deaths from cervical cancer may indicate uneven access to prevention, screening, diagnostic follow-up, and treatment. This study analyzed temporal patterns and regional inequities in cervical cancer mortality from 2010 to 2023. METHODS We conducted an ecological, retrospective study using public secondary data. The period began in 2010 to provide a post-2010 Census baseline and ended in 2023, the most recent complete year available. Deaths among women with cervical cancer as the underlying cause were obtained from the Brazilian Mortality Information System through DATASUS/TABNET, using ICD-10 code C53. Female population estimates came from the Brazilian Institute of Geography and Statistics. Crude mortality rates were calculated per 100,000 women by year and region. Trends were assessed using Poisson regression with log female population as offset. RESULTS From 2010 to 2023, 84,794 cervical cancer deaths were recorded in Brazil. Annual deaths increased from 4,986 to 7,209, and the national crude mortality rate rose from 5.01 to 6.65 deaths per 100,000 women. Poisson regression showed a significant increasing national trend, with an average annual increase of 2.19% (95% CI, 2.02 to 2.36; P < .001). Significant increases occurred in all regions. The South had the highest annual increase (2.69%), followed by the Northeast (2.49%) and North (2.31%). The North had the highest crude mortality rates, reaching 10.24 deaths per 100,000 women in 2023, while the Southeast had the lowest rate, at 5.22. In 2023, mortality in the North was 1.96 times higher than in the Southeast. CONCLUSION Cervical cancer mortality increased in Brazil and remained markedly unequal across regions. The North–Southeast gap reinforces cervical cancer mortality as a marker of inequities across the cancer control continuum, while the rapid increase in the South may reflect population aging, improved mortality registration, or changing care patterns. As rates were not age-standardized and no segmented analysis around HPV vaccine implementation was performed, findings are descriptive and hypothesis-generating. Reducing preventable deaths requires regionally tailored vaccination, screening, diagnostic follow-up, and timely treatment.

Knowledge, Perceptions, Vaccine Uptake, and Reported Barriers to Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Vaccination Among Caregivers and Adolescent Girls in the Southern Senatorial District of Akwa Ibom State, Nigeria

DOI : https://doi.org/10.66531/mjph.2026.v1i2.e9

Background: Human papillomavirus (HPV) remains a significant public health challenge in Nigeria, where cervical cancer ranks as the second most frequent cancer among women. Vaccination is a proven preventive strategy, yet uptake remains suboptimal. This study examined the knowledge, perceptions, vaccine uptake, and reported barriers to HPV vaccination among caregivers and adolescent girls in the Southern Senatorial District of Akwa Ibom State, Nigeria. Materials and Methods: A descriptive cross-sectional survey was conducted among 500 respondents (250 caregivers and 250 adolescent girls) selected through multi-stage random sampling. Results: Knowledge of HPV was low: 44.0% of caregivers and 26.0% of adolescent girls had heard of HPV, and only 25.2% of caregivers and 8.0% of adolescent girls demonstrated high knowledge. Conclusion: Knowledge of HPV and its vaccine is broadly poor among both caregivers and adolescent girls in the study area, and vaccine uptake remains low despite generally positive perceptions where awareness existed. Integration of the HPV vaccine into the national immunization programme, community sensitization, and improved healthcare provider communication are urgently recommended.

Building HPV Vaccine Confidence with and for Healthcare Workers in Nigeria: A Co-Design Study

Kofoworola Olamide Akinsola, Ayobami A. Bakare, Halima Usman, Julius Salako, Ayodamola Bakare — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11040604/v1

Abstract Background Cervical cancer, primarily caused by human papillomavirus (HPV), is a global public health concern, particularly affecting women in low- and middle-income countries, including Nigeria. The HPV vaccine prevents cervical cancer, yet uptake remains low due to supply and confidence barriers. While vaccine confidence among women and girls, and their families has received wide attention, less attention has focused on healthcare workers (HCWs), despite their key role in improving uptake. Co-designing interventions with HCWs could yield better-tailored programs addressing context-specific communication and information needs. Here, we describe the co-design process with HCWs in Nigeria, the strategies and delivery mechanisms identified, and provide methodological reflections. Methods We conducted a co-design study guided by human-centred design and the Ideate, Design, Evaluate, and Share (IDEAS) framework, in Oyo and Jigawa states, Nigeria, between September 2024 and September 2025. Co-design participants included doctors, nurses, and community health extension workers, recruited using purposive and snowball sampling. Data were collected through participatory workshops using audio recordings, field notes, and visuals of co-produced materials, and analysed using content analysis, with findings iteratively refined and validated with participants across workshops. Results Through a series of five workshops and three rounds of sense checks involving 45 HCWs, participants identified gaps in access to HPV vaccine knowledge, difficulty addressing rumours, and a lack of confidence in counselling. In response, we jointly developed content for intervention approaches: in-person and digital. The in-person intervention is planned to be delivered in the health facility by a trained HPV champion. The digital intervention in planned to be delivered through WhatsApp. Both use four short educational videos, which were co-designed with the HCWs, to increase HCWs’ knowledge and confidence in the HPV vaccine, covering: 1) general knowledge on cervical cancer, HPV, and HPV vaccine; 2) counselling parents on HPV vaccine, and 3) strategies for addressing rumours around the HPV vaccine. Conclusion The co-design study enabled successful development of co-designed videos, and in-person and digital intervention delivery strategies to strengthen HPV vaccine confidence among HCWs in Nigeria.

Global Disparities in Vaccine Reimbursement for Adults Living with HIV: A Cross-Sectional Survey of 120 Countries

Emily O. Burzynski, Özlem Türkmen Recen, Petar Velikov, Ahmed Cordie, Gabriela Zambrano — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.30.26366946

Objective: Adults living with HIV are at increased risk of vaccine-preventable infections, yet access to recommended vaccines varies globally. We evaluated national vaccination guidelines, public reimbursement, and on-site vaccination availability across countries with different income levels. Methods: We conducted a cross-sectional survey of HIV clinicians and experts representing 120 countries. The 26-item questionnaire assessed vaccination guidelines, recommendations, public reimbursement, and on-site vaccination availability. Countries were classified according to World Bank income group. Generalized linear mixed-effects models assessed routine recommendations, full reimbursement, and recommendation-reimbursement gaps by vaccine type and income group. Results: A total of 124 responses representing 120 countries were analyzed, including 38 low-/lower-middle-income (LIC/LMIC), 36 upper-middle-income (UMIC), and 50 high-income countries (HIC). HIV-specific national vaccination guidelines were reported by 37% of respondents, increasing from 18% in LIC/LMIC to 52% in HIC (p=0.026). Only 30% reported full public reimbursement for all recommended vaccines (13% LIC/LMIC, 28% UMIC, 44% HIC;p=0.007). Mixed-effects analyses showed significant differences in routine recommendations and full reimbursement by vaccine type and income group. The adjusted probability of routine recommendation was lower in LIC/LMIC than HIC countries (52.6% vs 77.6%; adjusted p=0.0013). Among recommended vaccines, more than 94% of influenza, pneumococcal, and hepatitis B vaccinations lacked full reimbursement in LIC/LMIC, based on adjusted estimates. Reimbursement gaps for HPV and herpes zoster vaccines remained substantial even in HIC. On-site vaccination was also less available in LIC/LMIC than in HIC (55% vs 78%; p=0.020). Conclusions: Significant income-related disparities persist in vaccination policy and implementation for adults living with HIV. Gaps between recommendations and financial access are particularly pronounced in lower-income settings, while HPV and herpes zoster reimbursement remains limited across all income groups. Expanding HIV-specific vaccination policies, improving public reimbursement, and integrating vaccination into routine HIV services are essential to improve access to recommended vaccines for this population.

Whole Genome Sequence (WGS) of Bordetella pertussis Strains in Amhara Regional State, Ethiopia

Solomon Taye Sima, Debasu Damtie, Belay Tessema, Baye Gelaw, Feleke Moges — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11237319/v1

Abstract Background Pertussis or whooping cough is an acute respiratory tract disease caused by Bordetella pertussis . In 2014, globally, there were an estimated 24.1 million pertussis cases and 160,700 deaths from pertussis in children under five years old. Due to vaccine pressure, there have been genotype changes in the circulating Bordetella pertussis strains. This study aimed to determine the genotype distribution of Bordetella pertussis strains in Amhara Regional State, Ethiopia. Methods A cross-sectional study design was employed on clinically suspected pertussis patients. Sixteen DNA samples positive for IS 481 (Ct ≤ 28 cycles), were sequenced using next-generation Illumina whole genome sequencing. The sequenced data was analyzed using software’s (Bowtie2 v2.2.9, Samtools v1.8, Bedtools v2.27.1). Sequenced data was compared with reference strains using Basic Local Alignment Search Tool (BLAST) and phylogenetic tree. Results The ptxP1, fim4 and prn1 alleles were found in 11/15 (73%) of sequenced samples. The remaining four samples carried the ptxP3, fim2 and prn2 alleles. None of the samples carrying the ptxP1 allele was found to carry the fim2 or prn2 allele and none of the samples carrying the ptxP3 allele was found to carry the fim4 or prn1 allele. Conclusions The ptxP1 allele carrying Bordetella pertussis was the commonest strain circulating in Amhara Regional State, Ethiopia. An increased number of ptxP3 strains in Ethiopia indicates the shift of Bordetella pertussis from ptxP1 to ptxP3 strains. This ptxP allele shift needs further investigation whether it is associated with the type of vaccine used or not.

Dengue Outbreaks in Bangladesh: From Reactive Control to Sustainable Prevention

Mohammad Saiful Hasan, Abul Bashar — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.70818/jnmc.v03i02.0122

Dengue has moved from an occasional urban epidemic to a recurring national public health emergency in Bangladesh. The 2023 outbreak recorded 321,179 cases and 1,705 deaths, the highest annual toll on record, and transmission reached districts once considered low-risk. The seasons since have confirmed that the problem is persistent rather than episodic. This editorial argues that a response built around seasonal fogging, emergency hospital expansion and public appeals is no longer proportionate to the threat. Evidence from Dhaka shows intense pyrethroid resistance in Aedes aegypti, the principal vector, and many patients died within a day of admission. We outline a shift towards integrated, evidence-based prevention: resistance-guided vector management, community mobilisation, year-round surveillance linked to serotype data, early clinical recognition of warning signs at every level of care, and careful evaluation of Wolbachia releases and vaccines. Medical colleges, as teaching hospitals and training institutions, have a central role in this transition.

Analysis of a reaction-diffusion SVEIR model for measles geographic spread

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Mahmoud H. DarAssi, Yousef AbuHour, Areej Almuneef, Zuhur Alqahtani — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-78853-z

We formulate and analyze a deterministic reaction-diffusion SVEIR model for the geographic spread of measles, explicitly incorporating vaccination, vaccine efficacy, latency, recovery, demographic turnover, and spatial diffusion. The disease-free equilibrium and the basic reproduction number $$\mathcal {R}_0$$ are obtained using the next-generation matrix, and linear stability analysis identifies the invasion threshold and the critical spatial mode. Because the critical wavenumber is zero, a long-wavelength analysis is used to characterize slow spatial variation near the invasion front. A mathematical consistency check shows that the common-diffusion formulation does not support a finite-wavenumber Turing instability; the roll, square, and hexagonal modes are therefore presented only as generic pattern-theory modes rather than as patterns proved to arise from the present model. Numerical simulations illustrate spatiotemporal propagation and quantify the effects of vaccine efficacy, vaccination rate, and transmission intensity.

Impact of RNA modification and IL-12 boosts on mRNA-LNP vaccine-elicited CD8+ T cell responses

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Sampa Santra, Michelle A. Lifton, Benjamin Mildenberg, Georgeanna Morton, Han Nhat Tran — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.26.761945

Development of effective HIV-1 vaccines requires strategies for inducing both CD8+ T cell and neutralizing antibody responses. Nucleoside-modified mRNA-lipid-nanoparticles (LNP) induce more potent antibody responses than unmodified mRNA-LNP vaccines, while unmodified mRNA is typically employed for CD8+ T cell-inducing vaccines. Here, we compared the abilities of differing ratios of unmodified versus modified mRNA expressing a SIV conserved-region CD8+ T cell immunogen co-administered with IL-12-encoding mRNA-LNP during boosting to induce CD8+ T cell and humoral responses in non-human primates, assessing responses in ChAdOx1-primed controls in parallel. Serial priming and boosting with fully-unmodified mRNA elicited higher-magnitude, more CD8-biased T cell responses than fully-modified mRNA, that were durably maintained after the IL-12-encoding mRNA boosts. Inclusion of 30% nucleoside-modified mRNA enhanced antibody titers, suggesting potential benefits to a mixed-composition vaccine.

Background: Cervical cancer is largely preventable, yet awareness gaps limit prevention in low- and middle-income countries. Effects of water quality, sanitation, handwashing and nutritional interventions on enteric pathogen seroconversion in rural Kenya: a cluster randomised trial

Henry Muriuki Kanyi, Richelle W. Kihoro, Huiyu Hu, Henry Emisiko, Erica Brook Goodhew — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.30.26366882

Background: We evaluated whether water, sanitation, and handwashing (WASH) and nutrition interventions reduced enteric pathogen seroprevalence during the first two years of life among children in rural Kenya. Methods: We analysed the 2x2 factorial subset of the WASH Benefits Kenya cluster-randomised trial (control, WASH, nutrition, and WASH plus nutrition). Dried blood spots were collected from all children in the birth cohort after two years of intervention and at 6 and 12 months within a longitudinal substudy. Immunoglobulin G (IgG) responses were measured using a multiplex bead assay that included 12 viral, bacterial, protozoan, and helminth pathogens. The primary outcome was IgG seroprevalence at 2 years. We estimated intention-to-treat differences, comparing WASH with no WASH and nutrition with no nutrition across the factorial design. The trial is registered with ClinicalTrials.gov ( NCT01704105 ). Findings: The primary analysis included 3030 children in 384 of 391 clusters (median age 23 months). Seroprevalence increased rapidly with age and exceeded 90% for norovirus, Campylobacter, and LT-ETEC by the final visit, with several pathogens at more moderate prevalences (24% of children were seropositive to typhoidal S. enterica HlyE). Differences between intervention groups were small: lower T. solium (−3.3 percentage points [95% CI −6.3 to −0.2]) and higher E. histolytica (3.2 [0.1 to 6.3]) with WASH versus control, and lower V. cholerae (−1.1 [−1.8 to −0.3]) and Shigella/EIEC (−2.7 [−5.5 to 0.0]) with nutrition versus no nutrition. No comparison met the 5% Benjamini-Hochberg false discovery rate threshold. Secondary analyses and age-seroprevalence curves suggested modest reductions in seroprevalence for some pathogens but an increase in norovirus at approximately 12 months when intervention adherence was highest. Interpretation: At the levels of adherence achieved, household-level WASH and nutrition interventions were insufficient to reduce widespread early-life enteric pathogen exposure in this high-transmission setting, supporting development of complementary vaccine and transmission-control strategies. Funding: National Institutes of Health (R01AI166671, R01AI106878, R01AI134814). The original WASH Benefits trial was funded by the Gates Foundation (OPPGD759).

Acceptance and Willingness to Receive HPV Vaccination Among Adult Women Living With HIV and Their Children

Nzube Favour Ekpunobi, Ngozi Idemili-Aronu, John Olawepo, Echezona Edozie Ezeanolue, Gregory A. Aarons — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-43000

PURPOSE Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination is a primary preventive strategy for cervical cancer, yet its implementation remains challenging in sub-Saharan Africa. Women living with HIV (WLHIV) are a high-priority group due to their increased risk of persistent HPV infection. This study evaluates the current levels of HPV vaccine awareness and the willingness of WLHIV to accept the vaccine for themselves and their female children in Nigeria. METHODS This multi-center cross-sectional study analyzed data from 4,613 WLHIV across 12 clinical sites. Participants were surveyed on vaccine awareness, personal vaccination history, and future willingness to vaccinate themselves and their children. Logistic regression analysis and Chi-square tests were utilized to determine the relationship between awareness and willingness, applying the Haldane-Anscombe correction for zero-frequency cells where appropriate. RESULTS Awareness was critically low; only 10.4% (n = 481) of participants had ever heard of the HPV vaccine. This deficit in literacy directly influenced behavioral outcomes: only 8.2% (n = 378) were willing to receive the vaccine personally, 0.6% (n = 26) declined, and 0.6% (n = 26) were uncertain. Willingness to vaccinate female children was even lower, with only 1.1% (n = 50) indicating acceptance, while the vast majority had no recorded response. A crucial finding was the absolute correlation between knowledge and intent, as zero (0%) women who were unaware of the vaccine expressed willingness to receive it. In contrast, awareness was a near-perfect predictor of acceptance ( P < .001). The primary reported barriers were a lack of information on vaccine availability, concerns about the cost of the vaccine, a lack of recommendations from healthcare providers, and uncertainty about where to access vaccination services. CONCLUSION The findings reveal a profound deficit in vaccine literacy among Nigerian WLHIV. The near-total absence of willingness in the absence of awareness confirms that literacy is the fundamental gateway to uptake. Public health strategies must prioritize intensive, provider-led educational interventions and normalize HPV vaccination within routine HIV care to overcome current levels of hesitancy and improve multi-generational cancer prevention.

Intelligent clinical decision support system for intracranial aneurysm lesion-based detection using biomedical imaging data

S. Praveena, V. B. Murali Krishna, Vijaya Krishna Sonthi, Devi Priya Gottumukkala, Chinmaya Ranjan Pattanaik — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-76815-z

Abstract An intracranial aneurysm (IA) is a type of cerebrovascular condition that occurs due to focal bulging or expansion in the wall of a cerebral artery, most frequently affecting the internal carotid artery. An IA is seemingly connected with conditions such as fibrous dysplasia, adult-dominant polycystic kidney disease (ADPKD), and coarctation of aorta. A ruptured brain aneurysm can result in a severe and life-threatening subarachnoid hemorrhage if not treated early, often leading to long-lasting neurological damage and a higher chance of death. However, the traditional diagnostic technique is time-consuming, and it necessitates a trained and skilled medical practitioner. Hence, building an automated model to help physicians precisely detect IA can reduce the clinicians’ burden. Recently, deep learning has gained significant interest in the domain of medical imaging as it automatically learns patterns and accurately detects several types of diseases, such as IAs. Therefore, this article introduces a Clinical Decision Support System for Intracranial Aneurysm Lesion-Based Detection (CDSS-IALD). The main purpose of this work is to develop a robust system that can automatically detect and identify intracranial aneurysms using medical imaging data to assist clinicians with treatment decisions. Initially, the proposed CDSS-IALD model pre-processes the input biomedical images using image normalization, noise reduction, contrast improvement, and skull stripping to refine image quality and consistency for subsequent analysis. Following that, a ConvNeXt hierarchical model is employed for feature extraction, which effectively extracts local details and global context from pre-processed images. Finally, these extracted representations are then forwarded to a spiking neural network, which performs accurate aneurysm detection and classification. To ensure the enhanced performance of the CDSS-IALD system, extensive experiments were conducted on the benchmark Intracranial Aneurysm Dataset. The experimental outcomes clearly demonstrate the betterment of the proposed CDSS-IALD system over the other approaches in detecting and localizing intracranial aneurysms from biomedical imaging.

Batch Effect Correction to Enhance Utility of a Publicly Available ALS Proteomics Dataset

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Grace Cooper, Inez Oh, Philip Payne, Zachary B. Abrams, Timothy Miller — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.27.762202

Large, publicly available databases of patient data have been successful in identifying associations between neurodegenerative diseases and biological features that might otherwise go unnoticed. The Answer ALS Consortium is one such database that has generated a rich repository of multi-omic data from people with amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS). However, large studies can suffer from batch effects that obscure genuine biological signal and prevent experiment reproducibility. In this study, we identified a batch effect in the Answer ALS 1-2 proteomic dataset (508 samples x 3,643 proteins) and corrected the effect using both the internal controls provided by Answer ALS and the batch effect-correction algorithm (BECA) ComBat. We identified a batch effect in the Answer ALS Dataset 1-2 proteomic dataset that, uncorrected, may obscure biology. Proteins MOS and H4 were outliers of expression in the dataset that correlated with the batch effect. Removing MOS and H4 did not correct the effect fully but did reveal new principal component (PC) contributing proteins related to motor neurons and potentially ALS: AINX, NFL, and NFM. The batch differential controls (BDCs) provided by Answer ALS were used to correct samples in their respective batches. ComBat, a well-characterized BECA, was applied to the dataset both before (ComBat-BDC) and after (BDC-ComBat) correction with the BDCs. BDC-ComBat was the only method that successfully reduced batch effect across the entire dataset. This method also preserved four of the top six PC-contributing proteins from the original dataset, including three neuronal proteins, suggesting that the corrected dataset recapitulates biology as well as the variability of the original dataset. Altogether, we produced a version of the Answer ALS proteomic dataset (354 samples by 3,641 proteins) with globally reduced batch effect and protein-level reduced batch effect. This version of the dataset recapitulates the individual protein variability and important neuronal proteins of the original. We also created a workflow for identifying and correcting batch effects that can be applied to other large data repositories.

Integrating transcriptomics and molecular AOPs to identify T3-dependent regulation of neuronal development in the human neural progenitor test

Marvin Martens, Nathalie Dierichs, Jeroen L. A. Pennings, Aldert H. Piersma, Victoria C. de Leeuw — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : —

Thyroid hormone (TH) is an important regulator of human brain development, and maternal TH imbalance is linked to adverse outcomes such as reduced IQ and decreased motor function in children. As part of the VHP4Safety initiative, using the human neural progenitor test (hNPT), we investigated the molecular effects of elevated levels and absence of T3. Neural progenitor cells were differentiated for 10 days under no (0 nM), normal (4.1 nM; the reference), or high (410 nM) triiodothyronine (T3) conditions. RNA-Seq was used to assess gene expression, and a molecular Adverse Outcome Pathway (AOP) network was applied to interpret transcriptomic changes compared to the reference alongside Gene Ontology (GO) enrichment analysis. Data show that normal T3 levels induce key neurodevelopmental processes which are not active without T3 exposure, based on significantly altered gene expression in pathways related to neurogenesis, synaptogenesis, differentiation and metabolism, including pathways of brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF) signalling, proliferation, oligodendrocyte specification, and glycolysis. Key Events (KEs) such as reduced BDNF and impaired proliferation were, compared to normal levels of T3, significantly affected in the molecular AOP network in absence of T3, while high T3 levels showed minimal differences in transcriptional effects. Our findings demonstrate that absence of T3 inhibits brain development since no T3 condition affects key neurodevelopmental processes in vitro. This study illustrates the utility of integrating transcriptomics with molecular AOPs to provide a structured, mechanistic framework for evaluating the regulation of neuronal cell proliferation and differentiation in brain development. The framework may be useful for chemical risk assessment.

Integrating single-cell and bulk RNA sequencing with ML algorithms identified MYBL2 as a candidate transcriptomic biomarker for diagnosing laryngeal squamous cell carcinoma (LSCC)

Tahmineh Mehrabi, Marzie Isavi, Ali Gholami, Amir Ali Hamidieh, Roozbeh Heidarzadehpilehrood — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tranon.2026.103095

Laryngeal squamous cell carcinoma (LSCC) poses significant clinical challenges due to pronounced tumor heterogeneity. To identify robust, consistent biomarkers across cohorts, we integrated bulk RNA sequencing (RNA-seq) data from GSE127165 and GSE142083 and validated candidate biomarkers in the external cohort GSE13060. Candidate gene prioritization incorporated differentially expressed genes, weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA) results, and markers of malignant epithelial cells exhibiting high copy number variation (High-CNV) derived from single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) (GSE206332). Additional analyses included functional enrichment, immune association (adjusted for tumor purity), quasi-temporal analysis, and CellChat inference to elucidate the biological roles of top genes. The integrated approach identified 254 differentially expressed genes (DEGs), comprising 86 upregulated and 168 downregulated genes. WGCNA revealed a tumor-associated module containing 870 genes. Intersecting these findings with high-CNV malignant epithelial markers reduced the candidate pool to 16 genes, and LASSO regression retained five key features: MYBL2, KRT17, UCHL1, PTHLH, and ODC1. Among these, MYBL2 demonstrated the strongest and most consistent performance. MYBL2 was further associated with proliferative programs, including E2F targets, the G2/M checkpoint, DNA repair, and MYC targets. Gene signatures from these subclusters were enriched in bulk tumors and positively correlated with MYBL2 expression in independent cohorts. The results also indicate that MYBL2-high malignant epithelial states are linked to distinct connectivity patterns within the tumor microenvironment.

A Clinically Adjusted Transcriptomic Signature for Survival Stratification in High-Grade Serous Ovarian Cancer

Aicha Lakhssassi, Mohcine Bennani Mechita, Joaira Bakkach — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-10000

PURPOSE High-grade serous ovarian cancer (HGSOC) remains the most lethal gynecologic malignancy, and clinical outcomes vary substantially even among patients with apparently favorable surgical cytoreduction. Improved molecular risk stratification may help identify patients at higher risk despite standard clinical prognostic features. Using publicly available transcriptomic and clinical data, we developed a compact transcriptomic signature for overall survival and assessed whether it added prognostic information beyond age, stage, and residual disease. METHODS Using TCGA-OV RNA-seq and clinical data (n = 416 HGSOC), we built a parsimonious survival signature by variance filtering, univariable Cox screening, 5-fold cross-validated LASSO-Cox, and multivariable Cox pruning. Performance was assessed on a held-out 30% test partition, in multivariable Cox adjusted for age, stage, and residual disease, and within debulking strata. Robustness was tested by 100-bootstrap resampling and consensus across Random Forest, Gradient Boosting, and L1-logistic regression. Differential expression in two additional HGSOC cohorts (n = 165 tumors) supported biological interpretation. RESULTS A nine-gene signature emerged: PRSS16, CTXN2, FCRLA (protective); PI3, LILRA2, LCN6, RNF103-CHMP3, KLK3, MTRNR2L8 (adverse). Held-out test: HR 2.15 (95% CI 1.35–3.43, p = 1.2×10 -3 ); 5-year time-dependent AUC 0.69. In multivariable Cox (n = 363), the signature was independently associated with survival (HR 1.76 per SD, P < 1×10 -10 ) and improved discrimination from C-index 0.62 to 0.69 (likelihood-ratio χ 2 = 69.6). Prognostic value was retained within R0 (no macroscopic residual) patients (HR 1.57, P = 3×10 -3 ; median survival 7.7 vs 3.8 years). Four genes (PRSS16, LILRA2, LCN6, PI3) ranked in the top 15 of all four orthogonal methods. CONCLUSION This exploratory nine-gene transcriptomic signature, derived from publicly available HGSOC transcriptomic and clinical data, stratifies overall survival and adds prognostic information beyond stage and surgical debulking. Its retained value among optimally debulked patients addresses a clinically important setting where additional risk stratification is needed. The signature reflects established TLS–mesenchymal tumor biology and nominates relatively understudied components, including CTXN2, RNF103-CHMP3, and MTRNR2L8, for orthogonal validation. External survival validation is required before clinical translation.

Datasets for reproducing SigFormer's benchmark results

Mingfei Han — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.34879188

This dataset collection supports reproduction of the benchmark analyses in the SigFormer study. It contains four folders:scRNA-seq: Single-cell RNA sequencing data from 16 tumor samples across eight cancer types, used to compare SigFormer with 15 existing methods for inferring signaling pathways between sender and receiver cell types.spatial_transcriptomics: Spatial transcriptomics data from eight regions of human basal cell carcinoma, used to evaluate spatially informed signaling inference and its sensitivity to neighborhood size.scProteomics: Single-cell protein expression data from 10 cancer types.scATAC-seq: Single-cell ATAC sequencing data from seven cancer types.The scProteomics and scATAC-seq datasets provide complementary molecular evidence for rescoring and reranking pathways inferred from scRNA-seq and are used to evaluate the contribution of multimodal evidence to pathway prioritization.

Democratizing Risk Stratification: In-Silico Validation and Pharmacoeconomic Assessment of SLC2A1 as a Low-Cost Prognostic Biomarker for Cervical Cancer

FERNANDO AUGUSTO SIUTA DOS SANTOS, PEDRO DYTZ MARIN, Giulia Scain, Isabela Alicia Fink, Julia Loureiro Chaves Cé — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1200/go-26-12800

PURPOSE High-cost prognostic genomic panels exacerbate health disparities, particularly for cervical cancer in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs). This study validates the transcriptomic prognostic power of the hypoxia biomarker SLC2A1 (GLUT1), evaluating the clinical and economic viability of utilizing highly affordable immunohistochemistry (IHC) to democratize precision risk stratification in resource-constrained public health systems. METHODS A retrospective in-silico survival and pharmacoeconomic analysis was performed using the Kaplan-Meier Plotter RNA-seq database for cervical squamous cell carcinoma (CESC). Patients were stratified by SLC2A1 mRNA expression. Prognostic impact was quantified via overall survival (OS), log-rank testing, and hazard ratios (HRs). An economic impact model compared the utility of a proposed sub-$30 US dollars (USD) IHC proxy test against standard $3,000 USD comprehensive genomic profiling. RESULTS Analysis of the CESC cohort (N = 304) established high SLC2A1 expression as a critical, independent predictor of mortality. Stratification yielded n = 80 high-expression and n = 224 low-expression patients. The high-expression cohort experienced a drastically accelerated mortality rate, characterized by an HR of 1.87 (95% CI, 1.16 to 3.04). This translates to an 87% increased risk of death ( P = .0097). Clinically, this biomarker predicted a severe median OS reduction of 14.3 months, with the high-expression group surviving a median of 21.2 months versus 35.5 months for the low-expression group. Pharmacoeconomic modeling demonstrates that substituting $3,000 USD genomic panels with a $30 USD single-marker IHC assay achieves highly significant prognostic stratification while representing a 99% cost reduction, saving public health systems approximately $2,970 USD per patient stratified. CONCLUSION SLC2A1 overexpression is a robust, quantifiable predictor of severe mortality in cervical cancer. Translating this transcriptomic data into a low-cost IHC clinical assay offers a 99% cost-saving alternative to broad genomic panels. Prioritizing single-biomarker validation is a high-yield pharmacoeconomic strategy to bridge the precision oncology gap and optimize resource allocation in LMICs.

Multimodal Analysis of Transcriptional Regulation in the Dorsolateral Prefrontal Cortex Enhances Alzheimer's Disease Risk Gene Discovery

Kyurhi Kim, Qiang Liu, Aron S. Buchman, Shinya Tasaki, David Alan Bennett — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.29.26366737

Recent transcriptome-wide association studies (TWASs) of Alzheimer's disease (AD) dementia have identified risk genes whose effects are potentially mediated through gene expression, but most do not consider other transcriptomic regulatory modalities that may contribute to AD genetic risk. To address this gap, we profiled transcriptomic quantitative traits across six modalities--gene expression, RNA stability, isoform ratios, splice junction usage, alternative TSS and polyA usage--from dorsolateral prefrontal cortex RNA-seq data tissue (n=1,035). We profiled 396,344 transcriptomic traits, which we leveraged for quantitative trait loci (xQTL) mapping and TWAS analyses of AD. We identified 25,736 xGenes (49.6% of analyzed genes) with at least one conditionally independent xQTL in separate-modality mapping, increasing signal detection by 42% relative to studying expression alone. Cross-modality mapping reduced redundant independent xQTLs by 45% while retaining signals better aligned with biological characteristics of each modality. We colocalized cross-modality xQTLs with 55 of 75 AD GWAS risk loci, including 15 risk loci detected only with non-expression modalities. TWAS analyses identified 161 independent risk genes with 59% detected through non-expression modalities and ~30% in multiple modalities. Follow-up proteome-wide association studies using DLPFC proteomic data provided proteomic support for 26 of these independent xTWAS risk genes. Protein-protein interaction analysis further showed that 46.6% of these independent xTWAS risk genes form an interconnected network through hub genes including MAPT, BIN1, and TREM2. Our study demonstrates that studying transcriptional modalities besides expression substantially enhances the detection of AD-associated genetic signals and elucidates their underlying mechanisms.

Nuclear cGAS governs transcription to control lipid metabolism, tumor growth, and therapy response

Yu Deng, Matthew J. Niederhuber, Zhichuan Zhu, Austin J. Hepperla, Le Yu — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1172/jci205579

Cyclic GMP-AMP synthase (cGAS) is a cytosolic DNA sensor critical for host defense against microbial infections. To prevent aberrant activation, nuclear cGAS is restrained by nucleosome tethering or by barrier-to-autointegration factor–mediated restriction of its access to DNA. However, its physiological roles beyond enzymatic suppression in the nucleus remain poorly understood. Here, we present a genome-wide map of cGAS chromatin localization in human cells and unexpectedly find its binding beyond nucleosome tethering, where cGAS is recruited to chromatin by AP-1 transcription factors via a potentially previously uncharacterized transactivation domain. Integrated CUT & RUN , RNA-seq, and metabolomics analyses reveal that cGAS transcriptionally regulates cholesterol homeostasis. Mechanistically, cGAS promotes LGALS3 transcription, which inhibits ACAA2 activity to suppress fatty acid oxidation and acetyl-CoA production, thereby lowering intracellular cholesterol levels. Reduced cholesterol lowers plasma membrane phosphatidylinositol [3,4,5] triphosphate [PI(3,4,5)P 3 ] levels and decreases mTOR complex 2 (mTORC2) activation. In contrast, cGAS depletion results in cholesterol accumulation and elevated PI(3,4,5)P 3 , driving mTORC2/Akt signaling, promoting xenografted tumor growth, and conferring resistance to DNA-damaging agents. In vivo, the elevated hepatic cholesterol levels observed in cGas –/– mice were largely reversed by inhibition of Akt but not STING, whereas cGAS deficiency had minimal effects on lung physiology. Collectively, our findings uncover an unexpected role for nuclear cGAS in orchestrating a transcriptional program that links lipid metabolism to cell proliferation, stress response, and cholesterol homeostasis in liver.

Twenty-five new microsporidian genomes: Insights into ploidy, reproduction, and the group's pangenome

Amjad Khalaf, Diégo Ayala, Nathan W. Bailey, Lia Bote, Ore Francis — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.30.762710

Microsporidia are single-celled, obligately intracellular eukaryotic parasites of growing importance. Many aspects of their biology remain enigmatic, partly because of difficulties in sampling, culturing, and experimentally manipulating them. Here, we continue efforts to generate microsporidian genomes from serendipitously infected organisms sequenced for biodiversity genomics initiatives, such as the Darwin Tree of Life Project. We present 25 new microsporidian genomes: 14 biologically complete, including several at (near-)chromosome level, and 11 partial. Using RNA-seq data for three published genomes we identify Prokka as a suitable ab initio annotation approach for Microsporidia. Consistently annotating all available genomes, we investigate gene-content diversity across microsporidian clades. We find that the microsporidian pangenome exhibits extreme diversity, and that current genomic data substantially undersample the group's proteome space. Neither GC content nor genome span consistently explains orthogroup number across lineages, suggesting different evolutionary pressures. We find few one-to-one orthogroups in Microsporidia, as well as few orthogroups that are both lineage-specific and conserved across most species within a given lineage. Finally, Hi-C data confirm that tetraploid Amblyosporida microsporidia occur as two equivalent diploid units. In contrast, one Enterocytozoonida tetraploid genome suggests that all four genome copies occur within a single nucleus (a 'monokaryon'), confirming a previous proposal.

The gene ivory:mir-193 controls scale type differentiation in Heliconius butterflies

Joseph J. Hanly, Leo T. Camino, Julia C. Holder, Paola Calderon-Oñate, Pushkar Ravindra Wagh — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-79315-w

The ivory:mir-193 locus is a genetic hotspot underlying melanic wing pattern variation across Lepidoptera, acting as a master regulator of melanic scale fate. This includes the genus Heliconius, where aposematic mimicry is driven by three scale cell types: light Type I, dark-melanic Type II, and red Type III scales. We test functions of ivory:mir-193 in Heliconius using CRISPR-induced somatic mosaic knockouts of ivory and mir-193 across multiple pattern morphs. Knockouts convert Type II scales to Type I scales. Effects on Type III scales vary among and within individuals, indicating that ivory:mir-193 is permissive for Type III development. Using single-nucleus RNA-seq, we profile the transcriptional landscape of H. melpomene Δ78k mutants lacking mir-193. Loss of mir-193 produces readthrough transcription, consistent with a model where mir-193 acts as a co-transcriptional terminator. These data show that miR-193, a miRNA under the transcriptional control of the ivory host gene, mediate the diversification of wing patterns across Lepidoptera by modulating scale cell identity during pupal wing development. Butterfly wing scales are diverse cell types that generate the colours and structures used in communication and adaptation. This study shows that the non-coding RNA ‘ivory’ controls alternative scale-cell states, linking regulatory evolution to phenotypic diversity.

miRNA cargo from pleural mesothelioma-derived extracellular vesicles reprograms fibroblasts toward an inflammatory CAF-like phenotype

Vivek Dharwal, Winston Lay, Richard Zelei, Ali Azimi, Ellen Donohoe — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41420-026-03401-7

Abstract Pleural mesothelioma (PM) is an aggressive cancer with a low survival rate. A better understanding of disease pathogenesis can aid the development of therapies. This study profiles the miRNA cargo of PM-derived extracellular vesicles (EVs) and examines their effects on fibroblasts, the predominant cell type in the PM tumour microenvironment (TME). High-throughput profiling revealed that EVs from epithelioid and non-epithelioid PM have distinct miRNA cargoes differing from each other and from non-malignant controls. Our data suggest that oncogenic miRNAs were upregulated and tumour-suppressive miRNAs were downregulated in PM-derived EVs. Further, we identified miRs that are exclusively present in either epithelioid or non-epithelioid PM subtypes. These miRs could serve as biomarkers to distinguish PM subtypes and aid in treatment decisions. To analyse the EV-mediated fibroblast response, fibroblasts were treated with isolated EVs, followed by RNA-seq and molecular analyses. Our miRNA–mRNA integrative analysis predicts that differential miRNA cargo from PM-derived EVs modulates multiple pathways in treated fibroblasts. GSEA analysis on KEGG_2021_Human and oncogenic signatures predicts upregulation of pathways including Cell Cycle, Neutrophils and Extracellular Trap Formation, and EZH2-Regulated pathways in treated fibroblasts. Through molecular analysis, we report that a predominant inflammatory CAF-like phenotype was observed post-EV treatment, as reflected by increased levels of markers like IL-6/8, MMP-1/3, ICAM1, ENG, and RRAGD. This was also accompanied by enhanced oncogenic signalling reflected by elevated Osteopontin, thrombospondin, Cystatin C, autotaxin, AXL, and ERBB2 levels. Additionally, our data show that PM-EVs modulate epigenetic regulation by altering EZH2 phosphorylation at Thr345: epithelioid EVs increased p-EZH2 levels, and non-epithelioid EVs decreased p-EZH2 levels. Further, our data show that PM-EV-mediated fibroblast reprogramming can increase PM cell migration and proliferation, thus promoting a tumour-supportive microenvironment. Collectively, this study highlights the regulatory roles of PM-EVs. The predicted EV-driven signalling pathways in PM-TME may represent potential therapeutic avenues for future therapeutic research.

Gene Expression Analysis for Modelling Immune Trajectory and Endotype Transition During Pediatric Septic Shock

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Shani Shameem, Claire Dunican, Elisabeth J. Robinson, Yael Feinstein, Joshua Hageman — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11541283/v1

Abstract Differential gene expression can describe groups of patients (endotypes) with differential outcome in sepsis based on immune (dys)function. However, limited mechanistic understanding of immune trajectory in sepsis hampers potential modulation of immune dysfunction. We aimed to characterise the immune trajectory of 19 children with septic shock from first interaction with pediatric critical care teams (time point, TP, 1) to 48 hours afterwards (TP2). We used differential gene expression, including weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA), digital cytometry and RNA velocity from bulk RNA-Seq data to compared modelled future gene expression with measured change in gene expression. We discovered key gene modules (WGCNAs, named by “hub” gene) associated with myeloid cell expansion between at TP1 ( CMTM6 ) and poor outcome in previously described BASIC Endotype 1 patients ( RAB2B ), and modules associated with adaptive immune function at TP2 ( BCL2 ) among others. RNA velocity analysis showed that immune trajectory was predictable from TP1 samples, including the ability to identify patients that subsequently transitioned from BASIC Endotype 1 (poor outcome) to BASIC Endotype 2 (better outcome). These data provide proof of concept for the identification of immune trajectory (including potentially treatable traits) from a single early sample in pediatric septic shock.

Night Jasmine Blooming Optimization (NJBO): A Novel Nature-Inspired Algorithm for Electrical Engineering Applications

Hussein Mohammed Hathal, Riyadh A. Abdulhussein, Takele Ferede Agajie, Alfian Ma’arif — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.31272/jeasd.3886

Optimization algorithms have become vital to the solution of complex optimization problems that are difficult to solve using conventional methods. In this paper, we introduce a novel nature-inspired metaheuristic algorithm called the Night Jasmine Blooming Optimization (NJBO) algorithm, which is inspired by the blooming of the night jasmine plant (Cestrum nocturnum). The suggested algorithm combines adaptive hormonal regulation, Gibberellin-based disturbance, scent-guided exploitation, elite preservation, and Nelder–Mead local refinement, which allows us to have a balanced exploration–exploitation process. It was tested with thirteen classical benchmark functions and the CEC2017 benchmark suites and benchmarked with several well-known and recent metaheuristic methods. As a practical application, NJBO was used for identification of an IIR system and optimal design of a five-stage Cockcroft–Walton voltage multiplier circuit. Additionally, the scalability analysis, runtime evaluation, component contribution analysis, parameter sensitivity analysis, and statistical validation with the Friedman ranking test and Wilcoxon signed-rank test were performed to assess the system completely. The results show that NJBO is a very effective general-purpose optimization algorithm with competitive convergence performance, good optimization accuracy, and stability for the benchmark and engineering optimization problems that were investigated.

Single-Cell Genomic Profiling Identifies an H3F3B–SEPTIN6 Axis Associated with Malignant Epithelial Phenotypes in Lung Adenocarcinoma

Xianghua Zhou, Liqin Wu, Fanfan Yang, Chengbin Hou, Jiaqi Wang — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10963935/v1

Abstract Background Lung adenocarcinoma is an important cause of cancer morbidity and mortality among women, yet sex-specific molecular features of malignant epithelial populations remain incompletely understood. This study investigated the expression and functional significance of H3F3B and its association with SEPTIN6 in LUAD. Methods Single-cell RNA sequencing data from three publicly available lung datasets were integrated to characterize epithelial cell populations and identify genes associated with malignant phenotypes. Differential expression, pathway enrichment, protein–protein interaction, and gene co-expression analyses were performed to characterize H3F3B-associated transcriptional programs. H3F3B expression was further evaluated in three paired LUAD and adjacent tissue specimens by reverse transcription quantitative polymerase chain reaction, Western blotting, and immunohistochemistry. Functional effects of H3F3B were investigated in A549 cells using overexpression and siRNA-mediated knockdown, followed by proliferation, migration, and invasion assays. SEPTIN6 expression was assessed following H3F3B perturbation. Results Integration of 665,761 epithelial cells from 878 samples identified distinct epithelial populations, including a prominent Secretory Club/AT2-like malignant population. H3F3B was significantly upregulated in malignant epithelial cells and showed increased expression across LUAD stages. H3F3B expression was positively correlated with SEPTIN6 expression in the integrated dataset. In paired patient tissues, H3F3B expression was higher in LUAD tissues than in adjacent tissues at both the mRNA and protein levels. H3F3B overexpression increased A549 cell proliferation, migration, and invasion, whereas H3F3B knockdown produced the opposite effects. SEPTIN6 expression increased following H3F3B overexpression and decreased following H3F3B knockdown. Conclusion H3F3B is associated with malignant epithelial states in LUAD and promotes proliferative, migratory, and invasive phenotypes in A549 cells. Its positive association with SEPTIN6 and the concordant regulation of SEPTIN6 following H3F3B perturbation suggest that the H3F3B–SEPTIN6 pathway may contribute to LUAD progression. Further studies are required to define the molecular mechanism underlying this regulatory relationship and to establish its clinical relevance.

Abstract Background Erenumab, the first calcitonin gene-related peptide (CGRP) receptor-targeting monoclonal antibody approved for migraine prevention in China, has shown substantial clinical benefit in Chinese patients with migraine.
