Atherosclerosis and Diabetic Foot Share a Common Molecular Signature of Cellular Senescence: A Multi-Omics and Machine Learning Study

Fuguang Zhang, Heng Wang, Yuelu Yang, Changhong Li, Xuejun Weng — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11347381/v1

Abstract Atherosclerosis (AS) and diabetic foot ulcer (DFU) are two major vascular complications of diabetes that frequently co-exist, yet whether they share a common molecular basis remains unclear. Here, we tested the hypothesis that AS and DFU share a common senescence-related molecular signature. We integrated transcriptomic datasets GSE134431 (DFU) and GSE163154 (AS), identified differentially expressed genes common to both diseases, and intersected them with the CellAge senescence-associated gene set to obtain senescence-related DEGs (SR-DEGs). Three machine learning algorithms—LASSO, random forest, and SVM-RFE—were applied to prioritize core feature genes, which were subsequently validated at the single-cell (GSE245703), tissue, and in vitro levels. We identified 72 SR-DEGs shared by AS and DFU, from which three core feature genes—CALCOCO1, CCND2, and FUCA1—were selected by cross-algorithm consensus. A multi-gene nomogram incorporating these genes achieved excellent discriminative performance in both diseases (AUC: 0.96 for DFU, 0.90 for AS). Single-cell analysis revealed cell type–restricted expression patterns of these genes, with fibroblasts emerging as a key senescent cell type in the DFU microenvironment. Clinical validation in an independent cohort of 22 patients (11 DFU vs. 11 non-diabetic foot ulcer) confirmed significant accumulation of senescent cells and dysregulated feature gene expression in DFU tissues. Moreover, metformin treatment effectively attenuated hydrogen peroxide- and high-glucose/high-lipid-induced fibroblast senescence in vitro, partially reversing the expression changes of the three feature genes. These findings demonstrate that AS and DFU share a common senescence-associated molecular signature, identify CALCOCO1, CCND2, and FUCA1 as potential diagnostic and therapeutic targets, and suggest that metformin may exert protective effects through modulation of this senescence-related gene network.

Differential Expression of miR-335-5p, miR-424-5p, miR-181a-5p, and hsa-miR-27a-3p and Their Association with Semen Quality Among Infertile Men

Zahra Keshtkar, Fatemeh Ghasemian, Farhad Mashayekhi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.18502/jri.v27i2.23144

Background: Semen analysis alone may not accurately diagnose male infertility. This study investigated four semen miRNAs, miR-335-5p, miR-424-5p, miR-181a-5p, and hsa-miR-27a-3p-as potential non-invasive biomarkers and examined their associations with infertility and sperm quality. Methods: Semen samples (n=54) were collected from couples who attended an IVF center for male-factor infertility between October 2022 and May 2025. Samples were classified into groups of normozoospermia (Control group; n=8), asthenozoospermia (n=12), oligozoospermia (n=11), teratozoospermia (n=12), and oligoasthenoteratozoospermia (OAT, n=11). Expression levels of selected miRNAs and the genes BCL2, BAX, Caspase-3, PRM1, and PRM2 were assessed using quantitative PCR (qRT-PCR). Sperm quality was evaluated through chromatin integrity, chromatin condensation, viability, acrosome status, and membrane integrity staining assays. One-way ANOVA with Tukey’s post hoc test was performed using Prism 8, with p< 0.05 considered statistically significant. Results: miR-424-5p, miR-335-5p, and miR-181a-5p expression levels were significantly reduced in infertility groups (p<0.01, p=0.0003, and p=0.001, respectively), whereas hsa-miR-27a-3p expression was increased (p=0.0003). The OAT group exhibited the poorest sperm chromatin quality and highest acrosomal damage (p<0.001). Conclusion: These miRNAs are significantly associated with male infertility and sperm quality and may serve as potential non-invasive biomarkers for differential diagnosis of male infertility.

Impact of Packaging Attributes on Brand Love: Evidence from the Luxury Fashion Industry in Egypt

Mai Elbeheri, Talaat Abdel-Hamid — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.32479/irmm.24750

This study examines the influence of Packaging Attributes on Brand Love in the luxury fashion industry in Egypt, building upon literature on the effect of packaging attributes as a multidimensional concept that extends beyond its protective function to include visual, functional, environmental, and experiential attributes on brand love towards luxury fashion brands. This study is theoretically grounded in the combination of the Cue Utilization Theory, Signaling Theory, and Attachment Theory to examine how packaging attributes shape consumers' emotional responses and strengthen brand love. This research employed a quantitative approach, using an online questionnaire to collect data from 440 luxury fashion consumers via River sampling, and the relationships were analyzed using IBM SPSS Statistics software using Multiple linear regression. Results demonstrated that packaging attributes have significant impact on brand love, with each packaging attribute contributing differently to brand love; thereby, visual, functional, and experiential packaging attributes positively impact brand love, whereas environmental packaging attributes were not a statistically significant predictor of brand love. Findings contribute evidence from the Egyptian market, offering practical implications for luxury fashion brands and marketers, highlighting packaging as a strategic tool to strengthen consumers’ emotional attachment to the brand, and supporting competitiveness in the luxury fashion market.

Identification of Non-Invasive Tumor-specific Biomarkers for Hepatocellular Carcinoma through Multi-transcriptomics Data Integration

Zahra Golshan, Hanieh Fadakar, Saeedeh Salehi, Yasaman Najafi, Sanaz Akbari — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10946987/v1

Abstract Background Hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) is the most prevalent primary liver cancer and a leading cause of cancer-related mortality worldwide. Early detection is critical for improving patient outcomes; however, current surveillance modalities exhibit insufficient sensitivity for early-stage disease. While tissue biopsy remains the diagnostic gold standard, its invasive nature limits its routine application. This study seeks to identify non-invasive blood-based biomarkers for HCC through transcriptomic analysis. Methods We analyzed transcriptomic datasets from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) and the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO), focusing on HCC tissues, plasma, and peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs). Weighted Gene Co-expression Network Analysis (WGCNA) and differential expression profiling were employed to identify candidate biomarkers, with key genes prioritized using STRING network analysis. Additionally, exosomal miRNA profiles were integrated to capture systemic regulatory signatures. Pathway enrichment analysis was conducted via Reactome 2024 to identify consistently dysregulated "tissue fundamental pathways" across liver datasets, which were then compared with blood-derived pathways. Candidate genes within these pathways were analyzed to define potential blood-based biomarker panels. Results We identified 11 plasma-derived genes (e.g., RPS27A, CCND1, HSP90AB1, POLR2F, H2BC13, H2BC14, POLR2C, POLR2L, NPM1, FEN1, XRCC6), 6 PBMC-derived genes (e.g., JUN, POLR2H, CUL1, DICER1, AURKA, CCNA1), and 9 exosomal miRNAs (e.g., hsa-miR-671-5p, hsa-miR-184, hsa-miR-425-5p, hsa-miR-193a-5p, hsa-miR-103a-3p, hsa-miR-371a-5p, hsa-miR-874-3p, hsa-miR-663a, hsa-miR-18a-5p) significantly correlated with HCC. Many identified biomarkers align with established cancer hallmark pathways, underscoring their mechanistic relevance. Notably, several of these genes (e.g., JUN, POLR2C) and miRNAs (e.g., miR-874-3p) have not been previously reported in association with HCC, highlighting their potential as novel biomarker candidates. Conclusion This integrative multi-transcriptomic analysis has unveiled novel, clinically relevant blood-based biomarkers for HCC, providing a promising foundation for sensitive, non-invasive detection strategies. Prospective validation in clinical cohorts is crucial for advancing the translation of these findings into early diagnostic protocols.

Computing the Riemann Hypothesis, Volume II: Cauchy Deconvolution and Certified Computation

Nicholas G. Polson, Vadim Sokolov — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23861975

Volume II of Computing the Riemann Hypothesis holds the computations of the Thorin programme. It asks what computation can and cannot decide about the zeros of the zeta function, evaluates Riemann's Xi function in ball arithmetic, and computes the Thorin measure at finite resolution, the de Bruijn Newman flow from the Pólya density, the barrier method in Thorin form and the Thorin phase diagram. Its second part treats Cauchy deconvolution: the deconvolution operator, three families of generalised gamma convolutions and the deconvolution obstruction, deconvolution as a damped Weil criterion, the level one measure and the backward Cauchy flow with its complexity and acceleration, the explicit formula as a deconvolution, particle filters and nested sampling from level one to the critical line, and the Balazard, Saias and Yor criterion by Monte Carlo. Later parts cover Weil positivity in the windows of Yoshida and Zhu, truncations of the Biane Pitman Yor and beta lattices, friable and ferromagnetic approximants of Xi with certified real zeros, the Comtet Majumdar family, approximating clocks, and conditional results under a quasi Riemann hypothesis at 7/8, which rests on preprints that are not refereed. Results computed in ball arithmetic are certified; floating point results are stated as computed. No proof of the hypothesis is claimed. Volume I, Representations of Xi, Thorin Measures and the Reciprocal Xi Function, develops the theory.

Integrated single-cell transcriptomics reveal E2F1-driven multidrug resistance in medulloblastoma and identify Palbociclib as a therapeutic candidate

Yueliang Yao, Junying Zhang, Ping Liang, Hang Ji — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11043118/v1

Abstract Purpose Medulloblastoma (MB) is a malignant pediatric brain tumor where tumor recurrence driven by acquired multidrug resistance remains a paramount clinical challenge. Resolving the intratumoral heterogeneity underlying this resistance is urgently required to identify novel therapeutic targets. Methods Single-cell RNA sequencing data and large-scale bulk transcriptomes were integrated and comprehensively analyzed. Multidrug-resistant subpopulations were identified by mapping ridge regression-based drug sensitivity predictions to single-cell profiles. Developmental trajectories and core regulatory networks were reconstructed, and essential transcription factors were functionally evaluated by computational vector field perturbations. The prognostic value of specific cellular states was assessed, and potential therapeutic antagonists were screened using computational databases and spatial molecular docking. Results Two distinct multidrug-resistant reservoirs were identified, an E2F1 -driven DNA repair state exhibiting cross-resistance to genotoxic agents, and a ZIC5 -regulated neuronal differentiation state associated with Hedgehog signaling. Trajectory inference and in silico perturbations demonstrated that the continuous accumulation of E2F1 is critical for maintaining the chemoresistant DNA repair phenotype. Clinical deconvolution revealed that elevated E2F1 expression acts as a robust indicator of poor overall survival. Subsequently, computational pharmacogenomic screening and molecular docking identified palbociclib as a highly potent inhibitor capable of precisely binding and targeting the CDK4/6-E2F1 axis. Conclusion An E2F1 -driven DNA repair program and a ZIC5 -associated differentiation state directly govern distinct multidrug resistance mechanisms in MB. Targeting the CDK4/6-E2F1 axis with palbociclib represents a promising precision therapeutic strategy to overcome chemoresistance in high-risk patients.

IN SILICO TOXICITY SCREENING: FROM CONVENTIONAL TO DIGITAL TOXICOLOGY

Saira Bhanu Shaik*1, M. Vamsi Vara Prasad2, M. A. V. D. S. Prajna3, P. Sai Srinu4, A. Vara Lakshmi5, Ch. Siva Durga6, G. Akhila Bhanu Prasanna7, Ch. Janush8 — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23763947

Toxicity remains a major consideration in drug discovery because a compound must combine therapeutic activity with an acceptable safety profile. Conventional toxicity testing provides essential biological evidence but can be resource-intensive and may be difficult to apply to large chemical libraries at an early stage. In silico toxicity screening provides a complementary, data-driven approach for identifying potential safety liabilities before extensive experimental testing. This review summarises the transition from conventional toxicology to digital toxicology and discusses quantitative structure–activity relationship (QSAR) modelling, read-across, molecular docking, toxicokinetic modelling, machine learning, deep learning and artificial intelligence (AI) approaches. Major toxicity endpoints, including cardiotoxicity, hepatotoxicity, nephrotoxicity, neurotoxicity, genotoxicity, carcinogenicity, reproductive and developmental toxicity, immunotoxicity and respiratory toxicity, are considered from a computational perspective. The article also examines how computational methods can support ADMET profiling, lead optimisation, candidate prioritisation and early toxicity-based deprioritisation. Although digital methods can improve speed, scalability and consistency, their reliability depends on data quality, model validation, applicability domain, interpretability and experimental confirmation. Future toxicology is expected to increasingly integrate AI, multi-omics, human-relevant in vitro systems, organ-on-chip models and New Approach Methodologies (NAMs). Overall, computational toxicity assessment is best viewed as a complementary component of an integrated safety strategy rather than a replacement for experimental toxicology.

ETHNO PHARMACOLOGY AND THE SCIENTIFIC INTEGRATION OF TRADITIONAL MEDICINE: ADVANCING HOLISTIC AND EVIDENCE-BASED HEALTHCARE

S. Poojitha, P. Renuka, K. V. Ratnamala* — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23799765

Traditional medicine systems continue to serve as primary healthcare resources for a substantial proportion of the global population, particularly in low- and middle-income countries where access to modern medical infrastructure remains limited. Ethnopharmacology has emerged as a critical interdisciplinary field that systematically bridges indigenous knowledge systems with contemporary biomedical science, enabling scientific validation, standardization, and integration of traditional therapeutic practices. This comprehensive review examines the evolving role of ethnopharmacology in advancing holistic treatment paradigms through methodological innovations such as network pharmacology, systems biology, metabolomics, artificial intelligence, and multi-omics technologies. The paper critically analyzes multimodal therapeutic strategies, constitutional typing frameworks, and mind–body integration approaches embedded within traditional medicine systems. Furthermore, it evaluates current challenges related to clinical validation, quality control, regulatory harmonization, safety assessment, and ethical issues including intellectual property rights and equitable benefit-sharing. Emerging evidence suggests that traditional medicine offers promising complementary strategies for managing complex chronic and multifactorial diseases; however, rigorous interdisciplinary research, robust clinical methodologies, and culturally sensitive policy frameworks are essential for its sustainable integration into global healthcare systems. Strengthening collaborations between traditional knowledge holders and scientific communities will be pivotal in developing equitable, evidence-based, and patient-centered holistic healthcare models.

AI DRIVEN DIGITAL TWINS FOR DRUG REPURPOSING: CURRENT EVIDENCES, APPLICATIONS, TRANSLATIONAL CHALLENGES AND FUTURE DIRECTIONS

Conventional drug discovery and development is associated with substantial financial costs, lengthy development timelines, and high attrition rates during preclinical and clinical evaluation. Drug repurposing provides an alternative strategy by identifying new therapeutic applications for existing drugs with previously characterized pharmacological and safety profiles. Recent advances in artificial intelligence, multimodal biomedical data integration, mechanistic modelling, and digital twin technology have created new opportunities to improve computational drug repurposing and patient-specific treatment prediction. This review examines the emerging integration of AI-driven digital twins with drug repurposing, focusing on digital twin architectures, artificial intelligence methodologies multi modal data integration, drug target prediction, pharmacokinetic or pharmacodynamic modelling, treatment-response simulation, and personalized therapeutic decision-making. Converging Mechanisms of Atrial Cardiomyopathy

Wei Su — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5463/thesis.1969

Atrial fibrillation (AF) is the most common sustained cardiac arrhythmia and is increasingly recognized as a manifestation of an underlying atrial cardiomyopathy. This thesis investigates molecular and cellular mechanisms through which genetic, environmental, and protein-misfolding-related disturbances promote atrial remodeling and AF, with particular emphasis on the cardiomyocyte cytoskeletal and nucleoskeletal network. The first part of this thesis examines genetic defects affecting structural proteins. Pathogenic variants in desmin (DES) were shown to induce variant-specific desmin aggregation, disruption of the linker of nucleoskeleton and cytoskeleton (LINC) complex, abnormal lamin A/C organization, nuclear-envelope rupture, and cytosolic DNA exposure. These structural abnormalities were accompanied by altered calcium handling and action-potential remodeling. Pharmacological induction of heat-shock proteins by geranylgeranylacetone partially restored structural and functional abnormalities, supporting proteostasis as a potential therapeutic target. Proteomic profiling of human atrial tissue from patients carrying PKP2 variants further demonstrated bilateral atrial remodeling involving extracellular matrix, cytoskeletal, desmosomal, mitochondrial, and redox-related pathways. In parallel, LMNA variants produced variant-specific alterations in nuclear viscoelasticity and abnormal calcium rhythmicity, linking nuclear mechanical dysfunction to atrial cardiomyocyte impairment. The thesis subsequently extends these concepts beyond genetic disease. Chronic exposure to commonly used pesticides induced conserved arrhythmogenic remodeling across experimental models, characterized by calcium dysregulation, oxidative stress, DNA damage, and mitochondrial alterations. Finally, localized transthyretin (TTR) amyloid deposition was identified in atrial tissue from a subset of patients with paroxysmal AF. Multi-omics and mechanistic studies indicated that fibrillar TTR contributes to calcium-handling abnormalities, electrophysiological remodeling, and increased arrhythmia susceptibility. Together, these studies identify cytoskeletal disruption, nuclear instability, impaired proteostasis, mitochondrial dysfunction, oxidative stress, and calcium dysregulation as converging mechanisms underlying diverse forms of atrial cardiomyopathy. The findings provide a mechanistic framework linking genetic, environmental, and amyloid-related triggers to AF and highlight potential opportunities for mechanism-based and precision therapeutic strategies.

Recent advances in the genetic dissection and breeding of salt-alkaline tolerance in wheat

Lecheng Li, Kun Niu, Minghang Song, Shanyi Sun, Shubing Liu — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44412-026-00022-w

Abstract Saline-alkaline soils severely constrain wheat production and have become a major threat to food security, especially in regions experiencing secondary salinization and climate change. As a glycophytic crop, wheat is highly sensitive to excessive salt and alkaline stresses, which disrupt ion balance, osmotic homeostasis, nutrient uptake, photosynthesis, and reactive oxygen species (ROS) metabolism, ultimately reducing plant growth, yield, and grain quality. Over the past decade, rapid advances in genome-wide association studies, quantitative trait locus mapping, and multi-omics technologies have greatly improved our understanding of the genetic and molecular basis of salt tolerance in wheat. Research on alkaline tolerance has received increasing attention and has provided initial insights into alkaline stress adaptation in wheat, although its genetic basis remains less well characterized than that of salt tolerance. Meanwhile, modern breeding approaches such as marker-assisted selection, genomic selection, and CRISPR/Cas-mediated genome editing provide promising tools for improving wheat salt-alkaline tolerance. Here, we summarize recent progress in the functional genes, molecular mechanisms, and breeding strategies associated with salt and alkaline tolerance in wheat, and further discuss current limitations and emerging research directions, including the integration of multi-omics technologies, artificial intelligence-assisted phenotyping, and precision breeding. Overall, this review provides a comprehensive overview of salt-alkaline stress adaptation and offers valuable insights into improving wheat productivity on saline-alkaline soils.

Polystyrene microplastic exposure induced a pronounced bone-loss phenotype in C57BL/6 mice, characterized by reduced bone density and trabecular bone volume together with impaired osteogenic differentiation. Transcriptomic and proteomic datasets of hydroxy-α-sanshool ameliorating hepatic steatosis in MASLD mice

Yan Sun, Yun Li, xiongzhong ruan — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17632/5m6nbjhctj

Research Hypothesis: This study hypothesized that Hydroxy-α-sanshool (HAS), a bioactive compound from Sichuan pepper, could ameliorate Metabolic Dysfunction-Associated Steatotic Liver Disease (MASLD) in mice by regulating hepatic lipid metabolism, specifically through suppressing de novo lipogenesis and enhancing lipid clearance. Data Description: This dataset comprises the raw transcriptomic and proteomic data derived from the liver tissues of C57BL/6J mice. The mice were divided into three groups: the Normal Control Diet (NCD) group, the High-Fat High-Fructose High-Cholesterol (HFFC) diet-induced MASLD model group, and the HFFC diet group treated with Hydroxy-α-sanshool (HAS, 8 mg/kg) for 6 weeks. Notable Findings: The data reveals that HAS intervention significantly reverses hepatic lipid accumulation. Multi-omics integration highlights that HAS exerts its effects via a dual mechanism: downregulating the SREBP1-FASN-SCD1 axis (inhibiting fatty acid synthesis) and modulating the PCSK9/LDLR axis (promoting LDL-C clearance). Interpretation & Usage: This dataset provides a comprehensive molecular landscape of the therapeutic effects of HAS on MASLD. Researchers can use this data to explore the regulatory networks of lipid metabolism, compare metabolic reprogramming under different dietary or pharmacological interventions, and identify potential biomarkers or novel therapeutic targets for steatotic liver diseases.

Multi-omics in the All Of Us Research Program expands mapping genetic mechanisms in health and disease

Evin M. Padhi, Elizabeth A. Kiernan, J. Nguyen, Marianne K. DeGorter, Victoria Silva Rosa — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.29.26366507

The All of Us (AoU) Research Program multi-omics cohort was developed to expand discovery of the molecular mechanisms underlying health and disease. Here, we report on the AoU multi-omics cohort comprising proteomic (N = 10,043) and transcriptomic (N = 9,043) sequencing data generated from ancestrally diverse AoU participants. We provide comprehensive expression, splicing, and protein quantitative trait locus (e/s/pQTL) analyses and detected 182,246 QTLs, of which 54,255 finemapped to rare variants and 59.4% (32,235) resolve to a single causal variant. We provide multiple insights into rare variant effects and demonstrate that they are more likely to be ancestrally-restricted, replicate across molecular layers and impact known Mendelian disease genes (OR = 1.4) and approved drug targets (OR = 1.3). Rare variant QTLs from AoU further provided evidence for 5,186 variants, many absent from ClinVar, affecting established disease genes. Combined, the availability of multi-omics in AoU provides a key resource to identify the genetic and molecular mechanisms impacting health and disease across multiple ancestry groups in the United States.

Single-cell transcriptome-wide association analysis reveals epithelial cell-specific genetic susceptibility to colorectal cancer

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.29.26366877

Bulk-tissue transcriptome-wide association studies (TWAS) have identified colorectal cancer (CRC) susceptibility genes, but cell-type-specific regulatory mechanisms remain poorly understood. Here, we performed single-cell TWAS integrating matched germline genotypes and single-cell transcriptomes from normal colorectal mucosa (381 donors) and polyps (84 donors), cell-type-specific chromatin accessibility, and GWAS from 132,261 cases and 1,752,398 controls. A deacetylase governs thermotolerance and pathogenicity by regulating host amino-acid utilization in Cryptococcus neoformans

Yue Ni, Yang Meng, Yue Qiao, Chao Li, Hailong Li — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.micres.2026.128807

Thermotolerance is critical for fungal survival in vivo , yet how metabolic remodeling enables pathogenic fungi such as Cryptococcus neoformans to withstand heat stress remains unclear. Here, we identify the protein deacetylase Dac6 as a thermotolerance regulator in C. neoformans functioning independently of the yeast deacetylase Rpd3 and mirroring the heat-resistant phenotype of clinical isolates. Integrated multi-omics analyses reveal that Dac6 remodels histone acetylation and gene expression to reprogram amino acid metabolism under heat stress, thereby enhancing lysine and threonine utilization. Lysine and threonine are also among the most markedly altered metabolites in the host during cryptococcal infection, and Dac6 deletion impairs fungal utilization of these amino acids under heat stress in vitro and in vivo . Consequently, Dac6 deficiency leads to reduced lung and brain colonization and attenuated virulence in mice. Thus, Dac6 couples nutrient utilization to thermal adaptation, revealing a chromatin-guided metabolic strategy that supports fungal survival in the host.

Prioritizing Osteoporosis-Associated Candidates Through Multi-Omics Mendelian Randomization, Transcriptomic Modeling, and in vitro Validation

Lixuan Sun, Shiqi Ni, Kangjia Yang, Xingyu Zhu, Jiabo Tang — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2147/ijgm.s630355

Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) is characterized by persistent airflow limitation, chronic airway inflammation, and immune dysregulation, and currently available therapies remain insufficient to effectively halt disease progression. In this study, we used an integrative, hypothesis-generating strategy to investigate the potential mechanisms of baicalein against COPD by combining multi-dataset transcriptomic analysis, single-cell transcriptomics, machine learning-based feature selection, Mendelian randomization (MR), molecular simulation, virtual knockout analysis, and in vitro validation. Putative targets of baicalein were predicted using CTD, SEA, and SwissTargetPrediction, and were intersected with COPD-related genes collected from GeneCards and OMIM. Four GEO datasets (GSE20257, GSE42057, GSE76925, and GSE130928) were integrated after batch-effect correction, yielding a combined cohort of 260 control samples and 250 COPD samples. Candidate genes were prioritized by intersecting the results of LASSO regression, random forest, and support vector machine. Immune-cell infiltration was estimated using CIBERSORT, and single-cell transcriptomic data were used to define the cellular localization of prioritized genes. Formal protein-level MR analysis was conducted for CD163 using deCODE plasma protein pQTL/GWAS summary statistics as the exposure dataset and the IEU OpenGWAS COPD dataset (ebi-a-GCST90018807) as the outcome dataset. Molecular docking, molecular dynamics simulation, and virtual knockout analysis were further used to provide structural and network-level supportive evidence. Finally, LPS-stimulated BEAS-2B cells were used as an epithelial inflammatory model to evaluate the effects of baicalein by CCK-8 assay, wound-healing assay, ELISA, and RT-qPCR. Five core genes were prioritized, namely ABCC1, CD163, CYP1B1, IKBKB, and PIK3CA. Immune infiltration and single-cell analyses suggested that macrophage-associated immune regulation may represent an important mechanistic direction. MR analysis provided supportive genetic evidence for prioritizing CD163 in COPD. Molecular simulation offered preliminary structural support for several target-compound interactions. In LPS-stimulated BEAS-2B cells, baicalein reduced inflammatory cytokine release and modulated the expression of IKBKB, PIK3CA, IL1B, IL6, and IL10, thereby providing epithelial-level support for the predicted network. Taken together, these findings suggest that baicalein may exert anti-inflammatory effects in COPD through a multi-target, immune-associated mechanism, with macrophage-related regulation and CD163 emerging as noteworthy candidate directions for further investigation. This study provides an integrative framework for target prioritization and mechanistic exploration, while the predicted macrophage-centered mechanisms still require dedicated validation in immune-cell and in vivo models.

Omics-informed vascular digital twins: protocol for a scoping review and framework linking molecular data to mechanistic and hybrid models of large arteries

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Kardokh Kakabra, Muhamad Albadawi, Elfatih A. Hasabo, Canberk Yıldırım, Osama I.I. Soliman — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23866596

Objective. To map how multi-gene molecular data have been linked to mechanistic, multiscale and hybrid models of large arteries, and what limits each link. Introduction. Decisions on aneurysm surveillance, restenosis risk and dialysis access depend on how the arterial wall will remodel, which is driven by cellular and molecular processes. Arterial digital twins therefore need molecular data to enter, constrain or test mechanistic models. Existing reviews describe digital-twin architectures, circulatory digital models, vascular ageing or agent-based frameworks; none of those examined reports a systematic search or classifies how molecular data and mechanistic models are linked. Eligibility criteria. Population: large arteries and their cells and molecular data, any species. Concept: a link between molecular-assay data on 3 or more analytes and a mechanistic or hybrid model run in the study, in one of three tiers: data-to-model, co-prediction (mechanistic outputs and molecular data entered jointly in one fitted predictor), and model-guided sampling (model output determines where tissue is sampled). Context: named large-artery beds, generic arteries, grafts, stents and fistulae. Methods. The review will follow JBI methodology and the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic reviews and Meta-Analyses extension for Scoping Reviews (PRISMA-ScR). After deposit, Europe PMC, PubMed, OpenAlex, arXiv and Semantic Scholar will be searched from 2000, with reference harvesting and citation chasing; recall will be validated on known eligible studies not used to develop the search. Screening and charting will be performed by natural language processing (NLP) software in two independent screener runs with frozen instruction files, calibrated before use against the blind decisions of two independent human auditors and monitored with a control set during use. The auditors will decide every forwarded record and every conflict, audit a random sample of exclusions sized to bound screening sensitivity, and verify every field that feeds a deliverable. Outputs will be a coupling taxonomy, an evidence map, a readiness matrix per omics layer, a resource table with sex and ancestry coverage, and candidate reporting items.

From Molecular Profiling to Prognosis: Biomarker-Defined Subtypes of Adult Sporadic Supratentorial Intracranial Meningioma and Their Prognostic Utility—A Scoping Review Protocol

Md. Moshiur Rahman, Amit Agrawal, Luis Rafael Moscote‐Salazar — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/n6vt8

Traditional World Health Organization (WHO) histopathological grading of meningiomas relies on morphologic features (mitotic index, brain invasion, necrosis, sheeting), yet clinical behavior frequently deviates from assigned grades. Up to 20% of WHO grade 1 meningiomas recur despite gross total resection (GTR), while certain grade 2 tumors run indolent clinical courses. The 2021 CNS WHO classification (CNS5) incorporated objective molecular criteria—specifically TERT promoter (TERTp) mutations and homozygous CDKN2A/B deletions—as standalone criteria for WHO grade 3 designation. Beyond focal genomic alterations, high-throughput DNA methylation profiling, multi-omic clustering, and integrated molecular-morphologic schemes demonstrate superior prognostic risk stratification compared to histopathology alone. However, two critical gaps exist in the current literature: Anatomical Moderation: The prognostic impact of driver mutations (e.g., NF2 alterations) varies by anatomical location (supratentorial vs. infratentorial/spinal). Pooling diverse anatomical compartments masks location-specific prognostic signals. Assay Accessibility vs. Prognostic Utility: High-throughput methylation and multi-omic platforms are costly and unavailable in resource-limited settings. Low-cost immunohistochemical (IHC) surrogates (e.g., p16/MTAP for CDKN2A/B loss, H3K27me3 loss, FOXM1) require systematic evaluation regarding assay concordance and predictive validity. This scoping review will systematically map biomarker-defined subtypes specifically within adult sporadic supratentorial intracranial meningiomas, examining both measurement validity and clinical prognostic performance.

Early‐Life Polyethylene Microplastic Exposure Exacerbates ADHD‐Like Behaviors in Mice via PI3K/AKT1/Nrf2 Signaling: Evidence From the Pilot Cohort and Multi‐Omics Analyses

Yufan Luo, Ruijin Xie, Hua Xu, Xue Xiao, Wei Xiao — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/advs.78594

Microplastic pollution may influence neurodevelopment, yet its relationship with attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) remains unclear. We integrated a pediatric cohort, multi-omics analyses, network toxicology, and animal experiments to investigate polyethylene (PE) microplastic exposure in ADHD vulnerability. Among 90 children, PE was the predominant polymer detected in blood and was more abundant in children with ADHD. Higher PE burden was associated with greater symptom severity, increased IL-6, and reduced antioxidant capacity. Multi-omics and network analyses converged on PI3K/AKT1/Nrf2 signaling. In a perinatal nicotine mouse model, developmental PE co-exposure exacerbated ADHD-like behaviors, dopaminergic dysfunction, and oxidative injury. Melatonin partially alleviated these abnormalities by restoring PI3K/AKT1/GSK3β and Nrf2 signaling, whereas AAV-mediated PI3K knockdown weakened its protective effects. Collectively, the human findings identify circulating PE burden as an environmental correlate of ADHD-related neurodevelopmental vulnerability, while the animal experiments provide evidence that developmental PE co-exposure can aggravate ADHD-like abnormalities in a susceptible background.

Autentikasi Pilot Berbasis Pengenalan Wajah Menggunakan Convolutional Neural Network Kustom dengan Deployment TFLite

Wirpan Atmaja Putra, Heri Setiawan, Yohanes Dwi Cahyono — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.35870/jtik.v11i1.7689

Most commercial UAV platforms still lack a mandatory pre-flight identity check, leaving ground control stations exposed to impersonation and unauthorized takeover. This paper proposes a facial recognition-based pilot authentication system using a custom four-block Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) to classify three identity classes: Pilot 1, Pilot 2, and Stranger. Trained on 2,125 facial images with data augmentation, Batch Normalization, MaxPooling, and Dropout regularization, the model achieves a test accuracy of 99.07% with a loss of 0.0613. The model is further converted into TensorFlow Lite (TFLite) format for efficient deployment on resource-constrained embedded devices, offering a computationally light alternative to conventional authentication methods as an embedded pre-flight security checkpoint for UAV ground control stations.

Bridging the Artificial Intelligence Divide in Higher Education: Digital Literacy, Social Influence, and Ethical AI Adoption among University Students

Wuryan Andayani, Rika Nur Widiastutik, Erwin Saraswati, Virginia Nur Rahmawati, Silvi Asna Prestianawati — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.32479/irmm.24617

This study examines the determinants of ethical artificial intelligence (AI) adoption among university students by integrating the Unified Theory of Acceptance and Use of Technology (UTAUT) and Digital Divide Theory. Specifically, this study investigates the effects of digital literacy, social influence, and digital divide on ethical AI adoption, as well as the moderating role of digital divide in the relationships between digital literacy, social influence, and ethical AI adoption. A quantitative survey was conducted involving 408 university students in Indonesia. The data were analyzed using regression analysis, supported by reliability and validity tests, correlation analysis, Gaussian copula endogeneity check, Harman’s single-factor test, non-response bias test, and simple slope analysis. The findings show that digital literacy has a positive and significant effect on ethical AI adoption, indicating that students with stronger digital literacy are more likely to use AI critically, responsibly, and in accordance with academic integrity. Social influence also has a positive and significant effect, suggesting that lecturers, peers, academic norms, and institutional guidance play an important role in encouraging responsible AI use. Digital divide-related conditions are also significantly associated with ethical AI adoption, highlighting the importance of access, capability, and digital support in AI-supported learning. However, the moderating effects of digital divide are not statistically supported. These findings suggest that digital divide functions more as a direct contextual factor than as a moderating variable. This study contributes to the literature on AI adoption in higher education by emphasizing that ethical AI adoption is shaped by individual digital competence, social-academic support, and inclusive digital conditions. The findings also imply that universities need to strengthen digital literacy, develop clear AI-use guidelines, and reduce digital access gaps to promote ethical and inclusive AI adoption.

Parallel Dataset for Natural Language Processing and Machine Learning

Judah Sleibi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17632/5gf48dt23t

## Description This dataset is intended to support research in Arabic Natural Language Processing (NLP), with a particular focus on Arabizi, an informal writing system in which Arabic is represented using Latin characters and numerals. The dataset provides text resources for investigating the relationship between Arabizi and Arabic script, supporting the development and evaluation of computational approaches for processing informal Arabic-language content. ### Potential Research Applications - **Arabizi-to-Arabic Transliteration and Conversion:** Developing models that transform Arabizi text into Arabic script. - **Machine Translation:** Investigating translation and text conversion approaches involving informal Arabic representations. - **Text Normalization:** Standardizing informal, non-standardized Arabic expressions. - **Machine Learning and Deep Learning:** Training and evaluating NLP models on Arabizi and Arabic textual data. - **Cross-Script Language Processing:** Studying linguistic patterns and computational challenges associated with Arabic written in Latin characters. ### Data Availability and Formats The dataset is distributed in CSV and Parquet formats to facilitate accessibility, reproducibility, and efficient data processing. The CSV format supports broad compatibility with research tools, while the Parquet format provides efficient storage and preserves optimized categorical data types. This resource is intended for researchers and developers working on Arabic NLP, low-resource language processing, multilingual text analysis, and computational linguistics. ### Contributions, Feedback, and Collaboration Contributions, suggestions, and constructive feedback are highly encouraged. Researchers, developers, and NLP enthusiasts interested in improving the dataset, identifying inconsistencies, exploring new applications, or collaborating on related research are welcome to connect. Suggestions for dataset improvements, preprocessing methods, linguistic annotations, and experimental evaluations are particularly appreciated. For collaboration opportunities, technical discussions, or research proposals, please reach out through the following platforms: **GitHub:** https://github.com/judahsleibi34 **LinkedIn:** https://www.linkedin.com/in/judah-sleibi-b8578b321/ Community contributions and academic collaboration are welcome to support the continued development and improvement of this resource.

Design and implementation of an industrial software cloud platform integrating knowledge graphs and large language models

Hailong Yang, Hao Liu, Yu Bai, Lei Yang, Mingxue Li — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-78651-7

Industrial software is evolving from standalone tools toward collaborative services, increasing the need for heterogeneous resource coordination and industrial knowledge reuse. Integrating knowledge-based planning with shop-floor execution remains a challenge in complex manufacturing workflows. To address this challenge, this study presents the design and engineering implementation of an industrial software cloud platform integrating knowledge graphs and large language models (LLMs). Deployed in a private cloud, the platform comprises resource, enabling, driving, and application layers within a cloud-fog-edge-endpoint framework. A knowledge-model-data-resource operational loop connects data governance, knowledge retrieval, task planning, verification, execution, and feedback. Knowledge graphs provide domain evidence and constraints, LLMs generate candidate plans, and mechanistic models and deterministic software services support verification and execution. Agents coordinate service invocation and feedback across these stages. Two engineering applications demonstrate the platform’s implementation in five-axis blisk computer-aided manufacturing (CAM) and CNC machine-tool prognostics and health management (PHM). Existing CAM records show reductions of 40.27% in aggregate programming and preparation time and 12.29% in single-piece machining time. The PHM application demonstrates the functional integration of data acquisition, fault reasoning, maintenance planning, and knowledge updating. These cases demonstrate the platform’s engineering implementation in manufacturing and maintenance workflows. The current evaluation is based on case-specific CAM records and PHM functional demonstrations, with broader performance and cross-enterprise validation remaining outstanding.

A multiphysics model for triboelectric nanogenerator design with explicit surface roughness representation

MD Tanzib Ehsan Sanglap, Jack Perris, R. Mukherjee, Charchit Kumar, Daniel M. Mulvihill — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.nanoen.2026.112314

The design of triboelectric nanogenerators (TENGs) for efficient energy harvesting requires predictive models that capture the interplay between surface roughness, real contact area, and electrostatic behaviour across diverse tribolayer materials and roughness levels. To address this demand, this paper presents a multiphysics finite element framework that couples mechanical contact analysis with electrostatic simulations, considering exact surface roughness representations rather than idealised statistical approximations. Compared with optical interference microscopy measurements, the framework predicts the real contact area ratio more accurately than analytical models. The proposed approach captures the electrostatic behaviour by scaling the TENG surface charge density with the real contact area ratio between the rough tribolayers, computed for a given mechanical load. This method improves agreement with experiments for open-circuit voltage and capacitance relative to approximate analytical models. To represent the TENG circuit, a time-dependent ordinary differential equation is integrated, enabling evaluation of electrical responses under varying load conditions and elucidating the roles of surface roughness, mechanical load, contact-separation frequency, and resistive load. The framework provides a robust, scalable tool for performance optimisation across dielectric materials, mechanical behaviours, and operating conditions and is readily extendable to other surface-dependent energy-harvesting devices.

Closed-Form Discrete Mechanics for Non-Holonomic Inversion: Analytical Formulation and Benchmark of the Asymmetric Tippe Top via the FL-Protocol

Ameen Ullah Khan — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23866608

Classical non-holonomic mechanics presents significant analytical challenges when systems combine asymmetric rigid-body rotation, dry Coulomb friction, and geometric contact transitions. Conventionally, such systems—exemplified by the inverting eccentric top (the Tippe Top)—are treated through continuous differential formulations requiring numerical ordinary differential equation (ODE) integration, which encounters severe step-size stiffening and floating-point drift at contact boundaries. This technical report presents an alternative discrete mechanics framework (the FL-Protocol) that resolves the dynamic trajectory, critical bifurcation thresholds, and terminal spin velocity analytically in closed form. Operating natively within rational algebraic space, the discrete formulation evaluates deterministically in 2.44 nanoseconds on standard silicon execution units, maintaining exact First-Law mechanical energy conservation with zero numerical drift. GitHub Repository, Code & Telemetry: https://github.com/SHAP-STUDIO/Research

Individualized Homoeopathy Added to Standard Pharmacological Care for Migraine: A Pragmatic Randomized Open-Label Blinded-Endpoint Controlled Trial.

Shilpa Prasad, Ratnasabapathy E., Venkatesan H., Sitharthan R. — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.7417/ct.2026.2139

Background: Migraine is the second leading cause of years lived with disability worldwide and the leading cause among young women; daily functioning, rather than attack frequency alone, is now a priority outcome. Individualized homoeopathy is widely used as an adjunct, yet adjunctive pragmatic evidence in tertiary public-sector Indian care is scarce. Methods: In a pragmatic, two-arm, parallel-group, 1:1 randomized, open-label, blinded-endpoint (PROBE) trial, 120 adults (18-60 years) with ICHD-3 migraine and baseline HIT-6 ≥ 50 were allocated to individualized classical homoeopathy plus standard pharmacological care (n = 60) or standard pharmacological care alone (n = 60). The co-primary endpoints were change in HIT-6 and change in monthly migraine days from baseline to 6 months, analysed by intention-to-treat with baseline-adjusted ANCOVA under hierarchical (fixed-sequence) testing. Responder (≥6-point HIT-6 reduction, a prespecified threshold derived from chronic-migraine evidence), Patient Global Impression of Change (PGIC), medication use, safety, and exploratory subgroup and 12-month analyses were secondary. Results: At 6 months, all 120 randomized participants were included in the intention-to-treat primary analysis (60 per arm). Because both co-primary endpoints were specified in advance and tested in a fixed sequence, HIT-6 was tested first and monthly migraine days second. The homoeopathy arm showed a greater reduction in HIT-6 (16.8 vs 9.1 points; baseline-adjusted difference 7.86, 95% CI 5.59-10.12; p < 0.001; Cohen's d = 1.24) and monthly migraine days (9.1 vs 4.8 days; adjusted difference 3.64, 95% CI 3.02-4.26; p < 0.001; d = 1.14). Acute-medication-day reduction was also larger (adjusted difference 4.54, 95% CI 3.92-5.16; p < 0.001). MCID response was 98.3% vs 66.7% (risk difference 31.7 percentage points; number-needed-to-treat ≈ 3), and 80.0% vs 28.3% reported being much/very much improved (PGIC ≥ 6). At 6 months, 53.3% of the homoeopathy arm had no current prophylactic medication recorded versus none of the standard-care arm. Any adverse event occurred in 23.3% vs 33.3%; no statistically clear between-group difference in overall adverse-event frequency was demonstrated. Initial aggravation was recorded in 71.7% of the homoeopathy arm; its clinical definition and characteristics require verification from the source records. Conclusions: Adjunctive individualized homoeopathy was associated with clinically meaningful improvement in migraine-related daily functioning and headache-day burden at 6 months, with concordant medication-use findings. Because the design was open-label and the intervention involved additional clinician contact, the findings estimate the effect of the adjunctive individualized-homoeopathy care package rather than isolated specific-remedy efficacy. Overall adverse-event frequency did not differ clearly between groups, but the sample size does not establish broad safety.

Comparative assessment of carbonation-ingress modelling in reinforced concrete structures with application to a cooling tower

Martina Šomodíková, Lenganji Simwanda, Miroslav Sýkora, David Lehký — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/15732479.2026.2751141

Among the primary durability threats for reinforced concrete structures is carbonation-induced corrosion of reinforcement. Despite significant research efforts in the last decades, models for carbonation ingress are associated with large case-specific model uncertainties. Therefore, this study compares selected predictive models for carbonation of reinforced concrete. Two representative frameworks are examined: a widely accepted resistance-based approach of the fib Bulletin 34, and a detailed semi-mechanistic model, which makes it possible to reflect mixture of the concrete. Their performance is assessed in a case study on an existing cooling tower, which provides field data for calibration and validation. Deterministic analyses demonstrate that the different models yield consistent predictions, while probabilistic evaluation highlights the dominant influence of concrete cover and the significant roles of diffusion parameters and model uncertainty. The comparative results confirm that reliability-based approaches provide a more robust basis for durability design than purely deterministic evaluations, with case-specific updating of key parameters proving essential for accurate service-life assessment. On average, both the resistance and semi-mechanistic models seem to reproduce field carbonation depths with reasonable accuracy (about 10 mm versus a measured mean of 15 mm). Probabilistic reliability analysis then indicates that the durability target of βt=1.5 is reached after about 20 years of exposure.

Trajectory-free identifiability and stability of expected hitting times in controlled absorbing Markov processes

Elysa Marie Alfredine RAZAFINDRAFARA, Mahefa Abel Razafinirina, Jean Berky Nguala, Solym Manou-Abi, Angelo Raherinirina — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.rico.2026.100870

Trajectory-level data is widely considered indispensable for recovering global dynamical quantities in stochastic control and reinforcement learning. This paper challenges that assumption by studying controlled absorbing Markov processes under an extreme observational regime where each trajectory is reduced to a single transition, with no temporal links connecting observations. Our contribution is a non-sequential sampling framework: under full support of the predecessor distribution, independent one-step pairs identify the transition submatrix Q row by row through conditional frequencies. The Neumann series is then evaluated algebraically, not term by term. This reframing clarifies that identifiability does not require trajectory-level information, but does require labeled transitions. Despite this severe information constraint, we prove that the expected hitting time remains identifiable from aggregated one-step observations under suitable coverage and persistence conditions. Our main contribution is a unified theoretical framework establishing four interlocking results: identifiability from first-order statistics alone; Lipschitz stability with explicit constants depending only on estimable quantities; structural bounds showing how persistence and drift jointly control the global dynamics; and a concentration phenomenon whereby hitting times are dominated by high-persistence states. The framework extends to absorption probabilities and discounted value functions. These results demonstrate that certain global dynamical quantities remain recoverable under substantially weaker temporal information than traditionally assumed. We also connect the framework to limit theorems for additive functionals of Markov chains and to inference under partial observations, and we outline semi-Markovian and spatially dependent extensions. The identifiability results are established under a fixed stationary policy, with observations of the form ( X pre , X post ) between labeled states. Extending the framework to control requires observing the actions jointly with the state transitions.

The Consciousness–Atma Hypothesis

Vivek Srivastava, Roshi Srivastava — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23853051

THE CONSCIOUSNESS–ATMA HYPOTHESIS A Proposed Unified Framework of Biological Energy Utilization and Regulatory Direction A Conceptual, Mathematical and Experimentally Testable Research Thesis Researcher: Vivek SrivastavaORCID iD: 0009-0001-1940-9389Researcher Handle: BikkusethvivekResearch Project: Consciousness–Atma Research Project Version: 0.3 — Foundational Integrated ThesisDate: November 2026 ABSTRACT This thesis presents the Consciousness–Atma Hypothesis, a proposed framework for investigating the relationship between biological energy utilization and the regulatory direction of organized living systems. The central proposition distinguishes two interconnected dimensions of biological organization. The first is Consciousness (Cₑ), operationally defined in this research as the level or capacity at which a biological system acquires, converts, distributes, and utilizes energy from its environment to sustain organized activity. The second is Atma (A), proposed as the directive and integrative dimension that coordinates and gives direction to the activities associated with Consciousness. The hypothesis suggests that biological organization cannot be adequately understood by examining energy acquisition or utilization in isolation. The regulatory mechanisms that coordinate energy use, genetic expression, cellular signalling, bioelectric activity, and adaptive responses must also be investigated. The thesis proposes a preliminary mathematical framework involving energy balance, regulatory dynamics, and measurable biological variables. It identifies cellular systems, particularly Saccharomyces cerevisiae, as suitable initial experimental models because of their well-characterized energy-responsive regulatory pathways. Existing research on Snf1-dependent regulation, cellular energy homeostasis, bioelectricity, metabolic organization, and biological information processing provides relevant foundations. However, none of these findings independently establishes the complete Consciousness–Atma Hypothesis. The principal research objective is therefore to determine whether an integrated regulatory representation provides additional explanatory or predictive value beyond established energy-metabolism and regulatory-pathway models. The distinction between operational biological Consciousness (Cₑ) and phenomenal or subjective consciousness (Cₚ) is maintained throughout the thesis. This work establishes a foundational research programme rather than claiming experimental proof. Its scientific development will depend on operational definitions, mathematical identifiability, controlled experiments, independent replication, and clearly specified falsification criteria. Keywords: Consciousness, Atma, Biological Energy Utilization, Energy Homeostasis, Cellular Regulation, Bioelectricity, Gene Regulation, Metabolic Integration, Systems Biology, Mathematical Modelling. CHAPTER 1: INTRODUCTION 1.1 Background Living systems continuously interact with their environments. They acquire energy and matter, transform available resources, maintain internal organization, respond to changing conditions, and coordinate multiple biological processes. These activities involve interconnected mechanisms such as: Energy acquisition and conversion. Metabolic pathways. Genetic information. Gene expression and regulation. Protein activity. Cellular signalling. Membrane potentials and bioelectricity. Feedback and adaptive regulation. Although these processes are studied extensively in biology, an important conceptual question remains: What is the relationship between the utilization of environmental energy by a living system and the mechanisms that direct and coordinate that utilization? The Consciousness–Atma Hypothesis approaches this question by distinguishing an energy-utilization dimension from a proposed directive and integrative dimension. It seeks to establish whether this distinction can be represented through measurable biological variables and experimentally testable relationships. 1.2 Central Research Proposition The central proposition of this thesis is: A living system possesses an organized capacity to acquire and utilize environmental energy. The activities associated with this capacity are coordinated through regulatory mechanisms that direct, integrate, and modify biological responses. For this research, these two dimensions are designated: Consciousness (Cₑ). Atma (A). The hypothesis proposes that these dimensions are distinguishable but interconnected. 1.3 Research Philosophy The research begins with the conceptual proposition that energy utilization and biological direction represent fundamental aspects of living organization. However, the scientific investigation must determine: Whether the proposed distinction is measurable. Whether it provides explanatory value. Whether it predicts biological behaviour. Whether it applies across different levels of biological organization. Whether its predictions differ from those of existing scientific models. The hypothesis must remain open to experimental refinement. CHAPTER 2: THE CONCEPT OF CONSCIOUSNESS 2.1 Operational Definition Within this thesis, Consciousness is initially defined as: The level or capacity at which a biological system acquires, converts, distributes, and utilizes energy from its environment to maintain organized biological activity. This definition is designated Cₑ to distinguish it from phenomenal consciousness. The definition focuses on observable biological functions rather than assuming that every energy-utilizing system possesses subjective experience. 2.2 Components of Cₑ The operational assessment of Cₑ will involve six principal dimensions. A. Energy Acquisition The capacity of a biological system to obtain usable energy from its environment. Examples include: Nutrient uptake. Photosynthetic energy capture. Chemical energy acquisition. Utilization of available environmental substrates. B. Energy Conversion The transformation of acquired energy into biologically usable forms. Examples include: ATP synthesis. Oxidative phosphorylation. Glycolysis. Photosynthetic energy conversion. Other metabolic energy-transfer processes. C. Energy Allocation The distribution of available energy among biological functions. These may include: Maintenance. Growth. Repair. Transport. Biosynthesis. Movement. Reproduction. D. Biological Maintenance The expenditure of energy required to preserve internal organization and sustain biological activity. E. Regulatory Response The capacity to modify biological activity in response to changes in energy availability and environmental conditions. F. Stability and Continuity The ability to maintain organized activity over time despite changing external conditions. 2.3 Proposed Measurement Framework The following variables will be investigated independently: Variable Meaning J_in Usable energy input rate J_ATP ATP production rate, where applicable η Energy conversion efficiency J_maint Energy expenditure for maintenance τ_homeo Duration of maintained homeostasis R_env Environmental response characteristics These variables should not initially be combined into a single arbitrary score. A validated operational index may be developed only after appropriate measurement scales, normalization methods, and biological criteria have been established. 2.4 Biological Organization as a Necessary Condition Energy conversion alone is not sufficient to establish biological Consciousness under this operational definition. For example: A battery converts and releases stored energy. A flame involves energy conversion. An isolated chemical reaction may release energy. These systems do not automatically qualify as biological Consciousness. The proposed operational framework therefore requires biological organization, regulation, and sustained functional activity. 2.5 Oxygen and Biological Energy Utilization Oxygen must not be treated as a universal prerequisite for energy utilization. Different biological systems employ different metabolic strategies, including: Aerobic respiration. Anaerobic respiration. Fermentation. Photosynthetic energy capture. Water, major biological elements, trace elements, ions, and molecular cofactors contribute to biological organization in different ways. The framework must accommodate this diversity rather than impose one metabolic pathway as universal. CHAPTER 3: THE CONCEPT OF ATMA 3.1 Proposed Definition Within the Consciousness–Atma Hypothesis: Atma is proposed as the directive and integrative dimension that gives direction to the activities associated with Consciousness. The concept addresses how a living system coordinates its activities rather than merely obtaining and utilizing energy. Atma is therefore investigated through the mechanisms that organize, regulate, and direct biological activity. 3.2 Proposed Biological Components The proposed biological correlates of Atma include the following. A. Genetic Information Genetic information provides instructions for the synthesis of biological molecules and contributes to the inherited organization of living systems. B. Gene Regulation Gene-regulatory mechanisms determine when, where, and to what extent particular genes are expressed. C. Protein Activity Proteins perform structural, catalytic, transport, signalling, and regulatory functions. D. Cellular Signalling Signalling systems coordinate cellular responses to internal and external changes. E. Bioelectricity Membrane potentials, ion gradients, and electrical interactions contribute to cellular communication and coordination. F. Feedback Mechanisms Feedback mechanisms allow biological systems to modify their activities in response to the consequences of previous activities. G. Integrated Regulation The i

41/60 NANOROBOT-ASSISTED PRECISION 3D BIOPRINTING AT THE LIMIT NANOROBOT-ASSISTED PRECISION 3D BIOPRINTING AT THE LIMIT is a 777-page English-language Research Design Working Edition in the STARLI Micro/Nanorobot Fabrication and Regenerative Biomanufacturing Sixty-Flagship series (Flagship 41/60). It investigates print defect correction without survivor selection. The research coupling is 3D bioprinting x microscale post-print correction x cell placement. All proposed work is restricted to nonclinical in-vitro, organ-on-chip, synthetic phantom or authorized ex-vivo environments. No experiments were executed in preparing this edition; no human treatment, clinical efficacy or regulatory clearance is claimed. Its falsifiable candidate is: Under full-build accounting, a frozen microrobotic correction policy will increase independently qualified constructs over both no-rework and skilled manual rework. The Atlas minimum demonstrator is: Blindly introduce printed defects, then compare no repair, human micro-needle repair and locked robotic repair on all builds. Strong comparators include established bioprinting, expert micro-needle rework and the no-rework arm. A central rival account is exclusion of irreparable constructs from the final denominator. The nonnegotiable stop condition is: unselected qualified yield does not improve or repair-related biological damage rises. Each failed, aborted, hidden-fault and incomplete run is retained in the experimental denominator, and observation of a finished form is never substituted for independently measured function. Thirty mathematical plates address domain-specific metrics and established model families across transport, measurement, elasticity, time-dependent dynamics, causal attribution, calibration and uncertainty. Eighty-two chapters and 410 separately paginated scientific investigations organize six research arcs. Two hundred Research Gates have been revised with independent observables, one of fifteen blinded causal intervention families, orthogonal controls and noncompensatory refusal criteria. Thirty mathematical plates now carry model-specific physical derivation commentary, explicit applicability limits and distinct flagship-level blind checks. Thirty-three Phoenix Answer Embryos propose unbuilt future instruments and succession artifacts, each requiring its own bench validation. This version is a research design and not a report of validated physical findings. It draws scientific method and conceptual boundaries from the user-supplied Atlas and research mother volumes; especially the Atlas-supplied ancestry DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23046134 and 10.5281/zenodo.23486020. Full external bibliographic DOI checking and scientific peer review have not been completed. Domain-specific sensitivity, causal identifiability and independent replication require qualified laboratory evaluation before any scientific superiority claim. The Atlas minimum demonstrator is: Have two laboratories reproduce a liver-gut chip and exchange masked test samples across sites. Strong comparators include commercial organ-on-chip products and expert manual cell seeding. A central rival account is shared critical reagents or centralized calibration mistaken for reproducible autonomy. The nonnegotiable stop condition is: a hidden central supplier is required or barrier function cannot be rebuilt. Each failed, aborted, hidden-fault and incomplete run is retained in the experimental denominator, and observation of a finished form is never substituted for independently measured function. Thirty mathematical plates address domain-specific metrics and established model families across transport, measurement, elasticity, time-dependent dynamics, causal attribution, calibration and uncertainty.
