Computational Non-Identifiability: A Design-Time Screen for Parameter Visibility in Embedded Motor Drives

Yankui Yang, Zihuan Xu — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23857939

A self-commissioning routine can report a parameter its own arithmetic has deleted. This dataset and code accompany a manuscript that formalises that failure as computational non-identifiability (IDZ): a direction the plant carries and the model implies, but which the deployed source never forms as a column. The contribution is fourfold. (i) A symbolic criterion that separates IDZ from four look-alikes (numerical collapse NCL, below-floor BLF, biased BIAS, non-independent NAT). (ii) A design number that prices the verdict before data are acquired, reducing to a Gramian of the deployed regressor. (iii) A demonstration on a brushed DC gear motor sampled at two rates, where the same source returns L̂ = −26.3 mH at 10 ms and 4.15–4.23 mH at 100 µs. (iv) A firmware audit of 33 trees in which five production projects carry a confirmed IDZ: LinuxCNC (Coulomb friction column), SimpleFOC (q-axis inductance), VESC (injection voltage), openinverter (dq cross-coupling) and FoxBMS (battery internal resistance). The package contains the manuscript, all figure sources, the authors' own bench measurements (approximately 75 MB), the analysis scripts, and a per-instance firmware evidence dossier with quoted code and line numbers. No third-party dataset is redistributed; external archives are cited by DOI. Known limitations are stated in README section 7, including one flagship number that is not reconstructable from this package and one parameter taken from handbook practice rather than measurement.

Probability, Context and Differentiation: The Layers Steps 1–4c: the distribution monad, the provenance comonad, the tangent layer, and orders of transitions

Vinay Awasthi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23849566

Abstract The three companions [1, 2, 3] use three structures side by side: probability laws and kernels, provenance (which source a record came from), and differentiation at the boundary of the simplex. This paper studies how they fit together, one layer at a time. Every ingredient of Step 1 is standard in categorical probability; what this step contributes is a set of exact statements about where the ingredients fail to commute, which the later steps build on. The finite distribution monad Dand the provenance comonad W= L×(−) are related by a distributive law λ: WD⇒DW, (l,p) →δl⊗p. All four axioms are written out with their types and proved. Its two-sided Kleisli category is the category of Markov kernels indexed by a source, with one source held throughout a composite. Marginalisation is total and affine. Event conditioning is partial and nonlinear. It is undefined exactly where the event has probability zero, it cannot be extended continuously to that wall, and its derivative grows like 1/p(A) near it. Conditioning commutes with marginalisation only for events that are cylinders over the kept coordinates. Contextual Bayes decomposition. Conditioning a pooled law equals pooling the conditioned sources with Bayes-updated weights. In the worked example, source weights 1 3 4 , 4 become 8 15 23 , 23 , and keeping the old weights gives the wrong answer ( 63 160 instead of 9 23 ). Forgetting provenance is marginalisation over the sources. It commutes with every window, but it is not injective: two families of sources can share a pooled law and every pooled table while differing in every labelled table. Step 2, the tangent layer. The cone tangent structure of the tangent companion commutes with the provenance comonad. Each source is a face of the labelled simplex, and a feasible direction leaves that face exactly when it injects whole records under another label; after forgetting provenance, an injected source and a drifting source have the same direction. Marginalisation and conditioning map feasible cones onto feasible cones, with explicit lifts; conditioning’s lift shrinks like p(A) near its wall. The cone TkHof the half-line is generated by elementary tensors, so it is closed under products, and pooling is a morphism at every order: the pooling paradox lives in the decision functional, not in pooling. Differentiating the contextual Bayes decomposition splits every sensitivity into a reweighting term and a within-source term; in the worked example the answer 9 16 23 moves at rate− 529 per unit of small-trial share. Strata and jumps. The sources that survive conditioning are exactly those with πlpl(A) > 0. Conditioning maps fibres of forgetting onto fibres: it keeps provenance ambiguity on the event, discards it off the event, and can destroy identifiability but never create it. Inside 1its domain, conditioning moves at rate at most 2/P(A), so nothing jumps and a sudden change needs a rare event. At the wall the limit is the conditional of the path’s leading jet; analytic passages are removable, and jumps need a kink, a non-analytic path, or a jump of the law. Step 3, orders of transitions. On non-negative germs in a Hardy field, order is a semiring homomorphism to (min,+), so every layer operation has an exact order calculus. Each source reaches an event at its own order. The sources of least order survive conditioning, with weights given by leading coefficients; the others vanish at their extinction orders. The two sides of a transition carry independent order data, analytic passages are removable but smooth ones need not be, and flat approaches have limits inside a Hardy field but not outside. A transition of order mis invisible to the tangent tower below level m. At a fixed gap, a higher-order approach switches more gradually. Step 4a–4b, several parameters. When several rates vanish together, orders form a fan: the normal fan of a Newton polyhedron, one cone per bounded face, each with its own surviving sources. The limit set is computed cone by cone. A limit exists when the exponents have a componentwise minimum, and for monomial cells only then. Lines through the limit point are not enough, and degenerate directions hide whole intervals. Iterated limits are the corners of the fan, and they are exactly what the tangent tower computes: the lexicographic lemma reads off one corner, and the canonical flip moves to the other. Five small examples show each case. Step 4c, parameters as an object. Parameters get their own 2-category, the Para con- struction over source-indexed kernels, whose 2-cells are reparametrisations; arcs are 2-cells from the germ line, and the real Puiseux field holds all arcs at once. Every layer operation commutes with reparametrisation. Decisions live on the order fan: along every arc the act is eventually constant, ties at the limit are broken at higher order, and the decision fan transports along monomial reparametrisations. In the worked example, two study designs are two 2-cells, each filling one cone, so each has a limit where the raw race has none. Step 4d, the decorated fan and Theorem J. The order fan carries a decoration, a facewise presheaf of initial forms, and only the pair (a probability-decorated order fan) determines the limits. Pooling’s whole curvature is its exact cross term. With the sample size as a parameter, the lab’s Theorem J becomes an inequality of orders: the band is the leading coefficient of the cross term on the one ray where exposure stays of order one, since exposure sees the share at half weight and impact at full weight. The same fan marks where Gaussian bands are valid, with a Poisson ray and an empirical wall beyond it. On the Poisson ray an exact discrete band replaces J’s, and beyond it a bounded adversary owns the rare event. Step 4e, graded classes. The classes Mk of maps that keep feasibility at order k form a strictly descending chain of subcategories. Every subtraction-free map lies in all of them, so every layer operation does, conditioning included, at every order. Only subtraction (contrasts, as in estimators and decision gaps) breaks feasibility. Step 4f, degenerate directions. A direction needs refining only when the event’s total mass P(A) has a degenerate initial form, because non-negative cells cannot cancel. The resolution is Newton–Puiseux for P(A): finite except along exact common roots, which lie in the wall. Two examples show a limit two refinements deep, and an infinite root cut by a competitor.

What does a bundle of measures really achieve? Causal identifiability of heating optimisation and heat recovery in an anonymised nonresidential building analysis

Süleyman Tiryaki — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23856750

The label given to a renovation package often suggests a single technical cause for its energy saving. When the package changes several systems at once, however, this attribution is not supported by the package comparison. This paper examines an anonymised, 69-page energy consultancy report using structured intervention coding and a formal identifiability test. In the transition studied, hydraulic balancing, heating-circuit temperatures, control and heat recovery are changed together. The documented final energy demand falls by 31.7 per cent relative to the immediately preceding envelope state. This contrast is observable within the report; the individual effects of the heating and ventilation measures are not. A saturated two-factor model has four parameters, while only two distinct treatment combinations are available. The missing combinations are not replaced by assumed saving shares. Instead, a complete simulation design is formulated that would make main effects and interaction identifiable under constant boundary conditions. A supplementary mechanism check shows that the reported reduction in ventilation losses of about 87 per cent must not be equated with the stated heat recovery efficiency of 80 per cent. The result is a verifiable demonstration of limited attributability, not a new estimate of real savings. The paper links the diagnosis to a measurement and documentation plan for later technical verification.

constraint-gap-measurements: measurement suite for the realizable set in Wi-Fi sensing attacks (WCNC 2027 submission state)

Egemen Tunçarslan, Andrew H. Bond — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23861446

The measurement repository of the paper The Realizable Set in Wi-Fi Sensing Attacks: Measurement, Cost and Identifiability (Egemen Tunçarslan, Andrew H. Bond), submitted to IEEE WCNC 2027 (EDAS 1571352231), archived as it stood at the upload of 4 October 2026: commit ec4a9f898d653638a72ed968381a87829a2be7d9, tag wcnc2027-submission. It contains the attacker-channel reconstruction and loaders, the grammar audit, the alignment and null measurements, the search over physical parameters, the identifiability inversion, the defence and detector-gate experiments, the l-infinity measurement, and two preregistrations, of 24 and 27 August 2026, that fix seven measurements in advance. Erratum. Four things found or recorded after the upload change what some of the paper's numbers mean. (1) The release-format factor 1.40 is a single pilot draw; remeasured over thirty draws it reads COLLAPSED under both linearisations (medians 1.108 and 0.954), so it is not a factor and the 1.40 × 1.24 × 1.13 split of the 1.95 does not stand. (2) The 13.1% defence figure is not significant once the overlap of the twenty splits is counted (Nadeau-Bengio corrected t = 1.200, p = 0.245). (3) The defence measurements split WiMANS by window; split by recording, the victim's balanced accuracy is 0.216 (chance 0.250). (4) The attacker channel depends on a synthetic pilot draw, and keeping the measured phase gives a profile-matched ratio of 0.865 at n = 450, against 1.528. The full text is ERRATUM.md, a file of this record and at the root of the zip. Neither dataset is redistributed: nine arrays derived from WiMANS and NTU-Fi HAR are left out, and ARCHIVE.md lists every difference from the commit. Both datasets are obtained from their own releases; loaders are included. The machine-checked algebra is archived separately, as constraint-gap-lean.

What Counts as a Philosophical Position? Thesis, Commitment, Attribution, and the Limits of Doctrinal Labels

Syed Raheel Shahzad — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23854113

What counts as a philosophical position, and when is a researcher justified in attributing it to a thinker, text, school, or intellectual tradition? Philosophical reference works frequently identify thinkers through doctrinal labels such as realism, empiricism, skepticism, compatibilism, nihilism, and virtue ethics. Yet these labels may conceal important distinctions between a philosophical thesis, a methodological commitment, an argumentative hypothesis, a practical orientation, an interpretive reconstruction, and the suspension of judgment. This paper develops the Attributed Position Account (APA-10), an original ten-field analytical framework for identifying, reconstructing, comparing, and responsibly attributing philosophical positions. It argues that a position cannot be adequately represented by its doctrinal label alone. Responsible attribution requires attention to the precise claim or orientation, its conceptual domain, the stance taken toward it, its argumentative grounds, its logical commitments, possible objections, and the historical evidence supporting the attribution. The APA-10 framework introduces ten research fields: question anchor; thesis or orientation; domain and scope; stance or force; attribution target; grounds or rationale; commitments and relations; defeaters and objections; source and locator; and attribution judgment. The study further introduces the Position Attribution Test (PAT), a five-gate assessment procedure examining identifiability, force, scope, evidence, and coherence under challenge. These gates are noncompensatory: an apparently plausible classification cannot overcome missing evidence, an ambiguous assertion, or an unsupported historical attribution. The procedure recognizes withholding judgment as a legitimate scholarly outcome. Eight analytical case studies examine important problems in contemporary and historical philosophy: ontological commitment, constructive empiricism and scientific realism, the analysis of knowledge, logical pluralism, ethical outlooks, Pyrrhonian skepticism, Madhyamaka emptiness, and culturally situated philosophical traditions. Particular attention is given to two negative controls: the distinction between Pyrrhonian suspension and denial, and the distinction between Madhyamaka emptiness and unrestricted nihilism. These examples demonstrate why superficially similar doctrines cannot automatically be classified as equivalent positions. The paper engages philosophical arguments and scholarship associated with W. V. O. Quine, Bas van Fraassen, Edmund Gettier, J. C. Beall and Greg Restall, Sextus Empiricus, Buddhist philosophical traditions, and contemporary work on epistemology, ethics, philosophical logic, and intellectual history. Its central methodological argument is that a philosophical position is better understood as an issue-relative, source-grounded, force-sensitive, and revisable commitment profile than as an isolated doctrinal label. The research contributes an analytical framework, a formal diagnostic model, eight constructed case records, six original research figures, a controlled attribution codebook, a Position Attribution Test, a machine-readable JSON schema, and a proposed procedure for independent reliability testing. The examples are constructed analytical illustrations rather than empirical observations. The framework has not yet undergone independent inter-annotator reliability testing or external specialist peer review. Its classifications and methodological proposals are presented for criticism, refinement, and independent assessment. RELATIONSHIP TO THE COORDINATES OF THOUGHT The research builds upon the original distinction between philosophical questions, positions, and traditions developed in Syed Raheel Shahzad's book: The Coordinates of Thought — Philosophy: Every Question, Answer, Thinker and Tradition. Author: Syed Raheel Shahzad Publisher: The Syed Group Year: 2026 Book reference:https://coordinatesofthought.com/book/the-coordinates-of-thought/ The companion digital reference platform, Coordinates of Thought, organizes the author's 131-question philosophical architecture and connects philosophical questions with thinkers, positions, traditions, books, sources, and educational material. Philosophy reference platform:https://coordinatesofthought.com/ The Coordinates — 131 philosophical questions:https://coordinatesofthought.com/map/ Author profile:https://coordinatesofthought.com/author/syedraheelshahzad/ These resources provide the original intellectual architecture and its associated digital implementation. They are cited as author-related source materials, not as independent scholarly verification of the present research. THE PHILOSOPHY MAPPING AND DISAGREEMENT SERIES This is Paper 04 of The Philosophy Mapping and Disagreement Series, an ongoing philosophical research program investigating the structure, comparison, attribution, and representation of philosophical questions, positions, disagreements, thinkers, and traditions. Paper 01 — Mapping Philosophical Disagreement A Question–Position–Thinker–Tradition Framework for Comparative Philosophy and Knowledge Organization. Introduced a structured method for representing philosophical disagreements through questions, positions, thinkers, traditions, sources, and consequences. DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23721230 Paper 02 — When Are Two Philosophical Questions the Same? Question Identity, Reformulation, Translation, and Presupposition in Comparative Inquiry. Developed a framework for distinguishing question identity, reformulation, refinement, overlap, and analogical comparison. DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23770454 Paper 03 — The Logic of a Philosophical Question Answer Spaces, Hidden Assumptions, and Conditions of Disagreement. Developed the Inquiry-State Account and the IL-8 Inquiry Ledger to analyze possible answers, presuppositions, argumentative responsibilities, evidential warrant, and provisional closure. DOI: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23831850 Paper 04 extends these preceding investigations by addressing a distinct methodological problem: what constitutes a philosophical position, how its content and commitment should be represented, and when its attribution to a thinker or tradition is warranted. The four papers form a connected research progression from the mapping of philosophical disagreement, through the identity and logic of philosophical questions, to the evidence-based attribution of philosophical positions. RESEARCH MATERIALS AND SUPPLEMENTARY FILES The supplementary research package includes: APA-10 controlled research codebook. Blank position-attribution coding worksheet. Eight constructed analytical case records in CSV format. Eight corresponding structured case records in JSON format. Controlled philosophical relation vocabulary. JSON Schema for machine-readable validation. Research validation report. Series DOI crosswalk identifying Papers 01–03. The publication archive also retains the editable manuscript, research figures, source verification ledger, and publication metadata. The technical validation confirms structural consistency of the supplied research files; it does not establish empirical reliability, complete historical verification, or the philosophical correctness of the proposed framework. It investigates distributed multirobot scaling versus throughput-induced injury. The research coupling is swarm cooperation x cell transport x decentralized decision making. No experiments were executed in preparing this edition; no human treatment, clinical efficacy or regulatory clearance is claimed. Its falsifiable candidate is: From one to twenty agents, a frozen decentralized policy will improve qualified live-cell throughput without increasing cell damage or per-cell resource burden. The Atlas minimum demonstrator is: Scale one to twenty microagents to construct a one-hundred-cell heterogeneous spatial pattern. Strong comparators include single-arm micromanipulation and centralized multi-arm coordination. A central rival account is faster operation purchased through damage, added energy or unreported human rescue. The nonnegotiable stop condition is: unselected cell viability declines as agent number increases. Each failed, aborted, hidden-fault and incomplete run is retained in the experimental denominator, and observation of a finished form is never substituted for independently measured function. BIT-EMIR Stage 1: Energy Accounting, Detectability and Calibration Limits for a JWST Single-Line Emitter

Quang Trịnh Bùi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23852775

DESCRIPTION (ENGLISH)This independent research report closes Stage 1 of BIT-CH3-EMIR-001, an exploratory branch within BIT Stage 3 motivated by interactions between electromagnetic domains and infrared line emission. It consolidates EMIR-R1 through R4 and a bounded calibration-input recheck. The release contains conditional bounds for positive photometric response and complete energy accounting, scoped nonidentifiability results, a frozen-throughput line-to-image conversion, independent aperture photometry on a public JADES F150W DR2 cutout, and an audit of the calibration inputs needed to compare the image and spectrum. The fixed 0.15-arcsec-radius aperture gives -0.759 nJy with a robust blank-aperture scale of 0.720 nJy. This is a background-subtracted estimate, not negative physical flux. The stage is closed with scope. The scientific inference gate remains on hold pending an identified local image-response calibration and source-specific spectral-to-image registration. The release does not establish source variability, 88-day periodicity, line identity, electromagnetic heating, dark matter or a new physical law. Core methods overlap established photometry, conservation and identifiability; international novelty of the synthesis is unverified. Five open questions specify required inputs and acceptance criteria for further work. The source archive preserves the original R1-R4 ancestry, the gate recheck, negative results, claim ledger and audit records. Five offline replay tasks passed and ten selected result files reproduced byte-for-byte. Fresh STPSF model generation and remote data acquisition were not repeated at closure. The main report is in English with a Vietnamese synthesis. ChatGPT/OpenAI assisted with drafting, code and checking; no independent peer review is claimed. This EMIR Stage 1 is distinct from BIT ontology Stages 1-2 and from the earlier R1-R20 control programme. MÔ TẢ TIẾNG VIỆT Báo cáo đóng Chặng 1 của nhánh BIT-CH3-EMIR-001 thuộc BIT Chặng 3: từ trực quan tương tác miền điện từ đến cận năng lượng, điều kiện đối chiếu ảnh - phổ, phép đo ảnh độc lập và giới hạn hiệu chuẩn. Hợp nhất R1-R4 cùng lượt kiểm cổng dữ liệu; giữ nguyên nguồn, kết quả âm và phản ví dụ. Phép đo cố định trên JADES F150W DR2 cho -0,759 nJy với thang nhiễu nền robust 0,720 nJy. Chặng nghiên cứu được đóng có phạm vi; kết luận về nguồn thật vẫn chờ hiệu chuẩn. Chưa xác nhận nguồn biến thiên, chu kỳ 88 ngày, nhận dạng vạch hoặc cơ chế điện từ; chưa có tuyên bố định luật vật lý mới hay tính mới quốc tế đã kiểm. Năm câu hỏi mở được bàn giao cùng đầu vào tối thiểu, tiêu chí kiểm, mã và hồ sơ tái lập. Báo cáo chính bằng tiếng Anh, có phần tổng kết tiếng Việt. Hồ sơ được xây với hỗ trợ ChatGPT/OpenAI và chưa qua phản biện độc lập.

THE CROSS-COMPONENT VALIDATION PROJECTION BIAS (CCVPB)

Shamiul Hoque Shan — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23866486

This manuscript proposes the Cross-Component Validation Projection Bias (CCVPB), atheoretical framework concerning the transfer of confidence from one component of a complextask to another component that has not been independently validated to a comparablestandard. The primary case is the Linguistic-Mathematical Validation Projection Case(LMVPC): an individual evaluates a linguistic explanation as correct and nearly flawless,but, without independently auditing the associated advanced mathematical analysis, extendsconfidence in the linguistic component to the mathematical component.The framework distinguishes objective component accuracy, subjective confidence, per-ceived component relatedness, evidential warrant, independent target verification, and thetransfer of confidence. This separation is intended to prevent a valid inference from being mis-classified as a bias merely because information has moved across components. The proposedformal architecture includes a signed projection-weight matrix, an operational definition ofprojection excess relative to a justified benchmark, an N-component directed graph, and aconditional dynamical model of repeated confidence transfer.Twenty interconnected theoretical layers organize the construct, its terminology, formal-ization, measurement, competing explanations, testable predictions, experimental design,statistical analysis, simulation, boundary conditions, and research translation. The manuscriptproposes six initial hypotheses and a staged research programme beginning with the linguisticmathematical primary case.The framework is conceptual rather than empirically established. Its originality, constructvalidity, parameter identifiability, predictive performance, and incremental explanatory valueover established accounts of evaluative spillover and generalization bias remain open researchquestions. The principal contribution claimed here is therefore a structured, falsifiableproposal, not a discovered universal psychological law.Keywords: cross-component confidence transfer; validation; projection bias; evidential warrant;confidence calibration; metacognition; generalization; directed networks; mathematical modeling;falsifiability.

Memory and Spatial Coupling in Regional Population Dynamics. A Fractional-Order Geosimulation of Regions

Călin-Adrian Comes — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23852137

Regional population trajectories depend on the memory of past regimes and on interaction between neighbours. This study proposes a geosimulation in which the annual population of each NUTS-3 region follows a fractional-order Verhulst–Lotka–Volterra system on the regional graph. Each region has its own growth rate, carrying capacity and Caputo derivative order between 0.3 and 1.95, so that memory becomes a mappable parameter, and a gravity-type coupling lets neighbouring populations affect growth. The model is calibrated on Eurostat series for the 42 regions of Romania (2000–2025) as a coupled system, without coupling for 1,070 regions of the European Union and EFTA (2003–2023), and as coupled systems for Bulgaria and Lithuania, which with Latvia complete Europe’s four steepest-declining countries. Nested integer-order and uncoupled variants are compared with information criteria, identifiability analysis and rolling-origin forecasts. Freeing the order reduces the Romanian residual error 7.9-fold, and the fractional model is preferred for 79% of European regions. Orders are mostly inertial and strongly clustered in Europe (Moran’s I = 0.38) but not in Romania (I = −0.16). Coupling improves the fit in 36 of 42 Romanian regions, but a placebo test shows that the distance-decay form of the weights is not identified. Bulgaria, Lithuania and Latvia need memory as much as Romania, whereas coupling is spatially identified only in Romania, weak in Bulgaria and absent in Lithuania. Memory-based forecasts match extrapolation but not a five-year drift. The 2035 baseline projects a 3.9% national decline, growth around the capital and decline in the south.

Qiang Cui — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.34875624

code and data for Exact Projection for Coupled Integer Factorization: Identifiability, Structured Algorithms, and Certificates

Extending the Theory of Planned Behavior in Life Insurance Purchase Intention: Fatalism, Learned Helplessness, and Financial Moral Obligation in Indonesia

Arief Eko Winarto, Annisa Ayura, Nely Sulastri, Evi Rinawati Simanjuntak — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.32479/irmm.24972

This study examines how multidimensional fatalism, learned helplessness, and financial moral obligation extend the Theory of Planned Behavior in explaining life-insurance purchase intention among Indonesian adults living in metropolitan areas. Using survey data from 710 adults, the study applies partial least squares structural equation modeling to estimate a reflective-reflective higher-order fatalism construct represented by religious and traditional fatalism and to examine direct, indirect, and interaction effects. Multidimensional fatalism was positively associated with attitude and purchase intention and negatively associated with learned helplessness. Attitude, subjective norms, and perceived behavioral control were positively associated with purchase intention, whereas learned helplessness and financial moral obligation were negatively associated with intention. Financial moral obligation also had statistically significant but modest negative interaction effects on the learned helplessness-intention and attitude-intention relationships. The cross-sectional, non-probability metropolitan-area sample limits causal and population-wide inference. The findings distinguish fatalistic belief from learned helplessness and suggest that internalized family duty may operate differently from external social norms, while the added constructs complement rather than replace the core TPB predictors.

50/60 MISSION RELIABILITY IN AUTONOMOUS NANOROBOTIC REGENERATIVE MANUFACTURING AT THE LIMIT MISSION RELIABILITY IN AUTONOMOUS NANOROBOTIC REGENERATIVE MANUFACTURING AT THE LIMIT is a 777-page English-language Research Design Working Edition in the STARLI Micro/Nanorobot Fabrication and Regenerative Biomanufacturing Sixty-Flagship series (Flagship 50/60). It investigates safe failure recovery with preserved productive service. The research coupling is autonomous manufacturing mission x regenerative specimen integrity x physically verified fault recovery. All proposed work is restricted to nonclinical in-vitro, organ-on-chip, synthetic phantom or authorized ex-vivo environments. No experiments were executed in preparing this edition; no human treatment, clinical efficacy or regulatory clearance is claimed. Its falsifiable candidate is: A frozen safety and recovery protocol will improve completed qualified work at equal fault burden while meeting independent stop and specimen integrity gates. The Atlas minimum demonstrator is: Blind-inject four fault classes into complete cell assembly missions with matched useful work targets. Strong comparators include expert human operation, fixed automation scripts and conventional redundant hardware. A central rival account is apparent safety caused by aborting early and completing less useful work. The nonnegotiable stop condition is: no advantage at equal effective work or an unknown fault fails to stop the apparatus. Each failed, aborted, hidden-fault and incomplete run is retained in the experimental denominator, and observation of a finished form is never substituted for independently measured function. Thirty mathematical plates address domain-specific metrics and established model families across transport, measurement, elasticity, time-dependent dynamics, causal attribution, calibration and uncertainty. Eighty-two chapters and 410 separately paginated scientific investigations organize six research arcs. Two hundred Research Gates have been revised with independent observables, one of fifteen blinded causal intervention families, orthogonal controls and noncompensatory refusal criteria. Thirty mathematical plates now carry model-specific physical derivation commentary, explicit applicability limits and distinct flagship-level blind checks. Thirty-three Phoenix Answer Embryos propose unbuilt future instruments and succession artifacts, each requiring its own bench validation. This version is a research design and not a report of validated physical findings. Teachers’ Perceptions of Inclusive Leadership Practices in Elementary Schools: Community Inclusion and Public–Private Sector Differences

Munwar Bagum, Naseem Bibi, Najma Ashiq, Muhammad akram Malik, Muhammad Jawad Tahseen — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.63056/jllsa.2.11.2026.427

This article examines elementary teachers’ perceptions of inclusive leadership in Khanewal District, Punjab, Pakistan. It selects one descriptive construct—“the school as an inclusive community”—and two inferential grouping variables, school sector (public/private) and gender (male/female), from the thesis. The source thesis reports a quantitative questionnaire survey of 824 teachers and a 12-item, five-point Likert scale for the selected construct. The reported overall mean perception was 4.17 (SD = 0.90), indicating generally favourable responses. The thesis reports no statistically significant sector comparison, t(822) = −0.12, p = .54; reported summed-scale means were 50.05 for public-school teachers and 50.10 for private-school teachers. It reports a gender difference, t = 2.86, p written as .00, with male teachers scoring 50.63 (SD = 5.07) and female teachers 49.52 (SD = 6.05). The findings require caution because the thesis contains inconsistent labels, apparent transcription errors, and incomplete statistical output. This article reproduces the thesis-reported values rather than independently analysing participant-level data. Expanded descriptive tables clarify what the reported means can and cannot show. Interpretations are limited to the sample, and no causal conclusions are drawn. Recommendations focus on strengthening community engagement, improving reporting transparency, and verifying the reported group comparisons before policy use.

Understanding What Reduces Academic Misconduct Intention Among College Students in the AI Era

Nguyễn Phong Luân Phan — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.52714/dthu.ajes.23.2051

The rapid development of artificial intelligence (AI) has created new challenges for academic integrity in higher education. Although previous studies have primarily examined academic misconduct among university students, less attention has been paid to college students and the factors that may mitigate their intentions to engage in such misconduct. Accordingly, this study examines the relationships between four factors and college students’ intentions to violate academic integrity in the context of AI use. A survey was conducted with 358 college students in Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam. The data were analyzed using Cronbach’s alpha, exploratory factor analysis (EFA), Pearson’s correlation analysis, and multiple linear regression. The results indicate that perceived risk, perceived behavioral control, and attitudes toward academic misconduct are significantly and negatively associated with misconduct intentions, whereas subjective norms are not statistically significant. Perceived risk exhibits the strongest negative association with misconduct intentions (β = −0.514). This study contributes empirical evidence from a college student population and extends the application of the theory of planned behavior (TPB) by incorporating perceived risk alongside its original components. The findings suggest that educational institutions should establish clearer academic integrity policies, communicate the consequences of misconduct, and provide students with practical guidance on the acceptable use of AI.

From Competence to Coexistence: A Version-by-Version Roadmap for the Entry of Home Humanoid Robots into Everyday Life

Akbarzadeh Alireza — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23861768

This paper critiques the prevailing dichotomy in the philosophy of robotics—robots as existential threat versus robots as legal persons—and proposes a conceptual framework for the gradual entry of humanoid robots into the family, culture, memory, responsibility, and daily life. The central argument is that a home robot should not enter the household with full capabilities at once. Instead, it must advance through a version-by-version path of practical competence. Each version carries an ethical core and operational scope appropriate to its stage. The paper formulates a set of normative principles concerning human control, erasable personal memory, a family constitution for the robot, responsible disobedience, protection of children's personality formation, retirement, cultural citizenship, public identifiability, insurance, and the avoidance of emotional exploitation. The result is a gradual path toward coexistence rather than replacement.

Strengthening capacity in high-burden malaria-endemic countries: a strategic analysis of training gaps and blended learning solutions in national malaria control programs

Moses Alikali, Graham V. Brown, Samira Hamid Abdelrahman, Dyann F. Wirth — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12936-026-06215-1

Abstract Background Malaria remains a significant public health challenge, with approximately 70% of the disease burden concentrated in 11 High-Burden to High-Impact (HBHI) countries identified by the World Health Organization. Despite substantial investments in interventions including insecticide-treated nets, rapid diagnostic tests, artemisinin-based combination therapy, and vaccines, persistent human resource capacity deficiencies undermine the effectiveness and sustainability of malaria control. Training gaps affect community health workers, district officials, and national program managers across various areas, including diagnostics, case management, surveillance, and data analytics. Methods This mixed-methods analysis integrated peer-reviewed literature, WHO and donor agency reports, and case studies from HBHI countries. Data from national health information systems and malaria program reports were evaluated using an Impact-Feasibility Matrix, with systemic relationships mapped across community, district, and national levels. Recommendations were validated through consultations with technical experts, Malaria Policy Advisory Group members, National Malaria Control Program managers, and partner organizations. Results Significant training and workforce shortages were identified at all health system levels. Community-level deficiencies in health worker training and supervision correlated with misdiagnosis, treatment delays, and poor community engagement. District challenges included suboptimal supply chain management, insufficient surveillance capacity, and limited managerial skills. National-level constraints involved inadequate technical expertise in epidemiology, data analysis, and policy adaptation. A blended learning strategy combining self-paced digital modules, virtual mentoring, and outreach training with supportive supervision emerged as a high-priority intervention. Implementation of standardized evaluation frameworks was recommended to assess training effectiveness across multiple outcome domains. Conclusions Enhancing workforce capacity through blended learning methodologies can effectively address critical human resource gaps in HBHI countries. Context-specific training, digital resources, and sustainable mentorship frameworks can significantly improve operational efficiency, service delivery, and strategic planning. Local capacity strengthening for best practice implementation at all health system levels is essential for achieving malaria elimination targets.

COMBO ‐ RATE : An Experimentally Validated Bioinformatic Tool to Identify Promiscuous HLA Restrictions

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Jessica Nevarez‐Mejia, Raphael Trevizani, Adam Abawi, Kendall Kearns, Emil Johansson — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1111/tan.71060

ABSTRACT Defining HLA restriction of T cell epitopes is essential for understanding immune responses in infectious disease, autoimmunity and vaccine design. Current bioinformatic programmes, including the IEDB RATE tool, enable inference of single‐HLA restrictions from immune response data of HLA‐typed donors. However, T cell epitopes are frequently presented by multiple HLA alleles, a phenomenon termed promiscuous restriction, limiting the utility of single‐allele approaches. To address this limitation, we developed COMBO‐RATE, an extension of RATE that systematically evaluates combinations of HLA alleles to identify multi‐allelic restriction patterns. Analysis of three independent datasets spanning distinct antigen systems and different epitope discovery strategies revealed that promiscuous restriction is a near‐universal feature of immunodominant epitopes. Focusing on the 43 immunodominant CD4 + T cell epitopes identified in a B. pertussis genome‐wide screen, COMBO‐RATE outperformed conventional RATE, identifying restrictions for 35 of 43 epitopes, compared to 24 by RATE alone, and uncovered 64 additional allele restrictions, including 29 unique alleles. Experimental validation using single‐HLA transfected cell lines and antigen presentation assays confirmed COMBO‐RATE‐inferred restrictions, demonstrating that a single epitope can be independently presented by distinct HLA alleles. Overall, COMBO‐RATE provides a robust and scalable framework for defining complete HLA restriction profiles from existing population response data, with important implications for the design of vaccines requiring broad HLA coverage across genetically diverse populations. This pipeline is available as both a Python package and a user‐friendly web application.

Key stakeholders’ perspectives on introducing a new TB vaccine for adolescents and adults in Mozambique: a qualitative study

Agostinho Viana Lima, Doyoon Kim, Sozinho Acácio, Quinhas Fernandes, Benedita José — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12879-026-14746-w

ABSTRACT Tuberculosis (TB) remains a major global health challenge, particularly in low- and middle-income countries such as Mozambique. To address this burden, promising new preventive TB vaccines targeting adolescents and adults are currently in phase III efficacy trials. This study aimed to assess stakeholders’ perspectives on priority high-risk groups, the challenges in reaching them, and potential strategies for delivering a TB vaccine. We conducted a qualitative study using semi-structured interviews with members of the National TB Program, the National Immunization Program, and the National Immunization Technical Advisory Group. Data were collected between March and July 2024. Our findings suggest that a TB vaccine program in Mozambique should prioritize individuals with comorbidities, especially those living with HIV or diabetes, and close contacts of TB patients, followed by healthcare workers, miners, and incarcerated populations. Although uptake is expected to vary across groups, relatively high coverage was anticipated among people living with HIV, TB contacts, and older adults, as well as healthcare workers, incarcerated individuals, formal miners, and in-school adolescents. To improve uptake, campaign-based strategies using mobile brigades were considered promising approaches to expand coverage. Stakeholder perspectives highlight the importance of prioritizing high-risk groups and adopting context-specific delivery strategies to support the effective introduction of a TB vaccine in Mozambique. Clinical trial number not applicable.

Association of histological chorioamnionitis and fetal inflammatory response with pathogen distribution in neonatal umbilical cord blood cultures

Tong Dang, Lixin Ma, Binqing Ding, Rong Tao, Yingying Bao — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12884-026-10165-6

Abstract Objective To investigate the association between different stages of histological chorioamnionitis (HCA), complicated fetal inflammatory response syndrome (FIRS), and the distribution characteristics of pathogens in neonatal umbilical cord blood cultures(UCBC), so as to provide a basis for the early clinical identification of intrauterine infection. Methods A retrospective case-control study was conducted, enrolling 243 newborns delivered at Women's Hospital School of Medicine Zhejiang University from January 2021 to December 2024. According to the results of UCBC, the newborns were divided into a positive group (81 cases) and a negative group (162 cases, matched at a ratio of 1:2). Baseline data such as HCA and FIRS were compared between the two groups, and the high-risk factors affecting positive UCBC results were analyzed. Multivariate logistic regression was used to screen independent risk factors for positive UCBC. Furthermore, the distribution characteristics of pathogens in UCBC under different stages of HCA and FIRS were analyzed. Results The incidence rates of moderate to severe HCA, FIRS, placental abruption, acute chorioamnionitis, maternal prenatal temperature ≥ 38.0℃, maternal prenatal pathogenic infection, and maternal pregnancy complicated by cervical incompetence in the UCBC positive group were significantly higher than in the UCBC negative group (P < 0.05), and the maternal age was older (P < 0.05). Multivariate logistic regression analysis identified that FIRS (OR = 6.38, 95%CI: 2.793–14.573, P < 0.001) and prenatal pathogenic infection (OR = 6.049, 95%CI: 2.946–12.418, P < 0.001) were independent risk factors for positive UCBC. A total of 86 pathogenic strains were isolated from 81 positive UCBC specimens (5 cases of mixed infection), including 51 Gram-positive strains (59.3%), mainly Enterococcus faecalis (14.0%), group B Streptococcus (7.0%) and Streptococcus mitis (7.0%); 35 Gram-negative strains (40.7%), mainly Escherichia coli (29.1%). Among children with positive UCBC results, the rate of moderate or severe HCA was higher than that in the normal or mild HCA group (55.6% vs 44.4%), but the difference was not statistically significant (P > 0.05). The difference between the group with FIRS and the group without FIRS was statistically significant (61.7% vs 38.3%, P < 0.05). Escherichia coli(E. coli) detection was significantly higher in HCA complicated with FIRS than in those without FIRS (68.0% vs 32.0%, P < 0.05). All Listeria monocytogenes infections were accompanied by moderate or severe HCA and FIRS. Conclusion FIRS and maternal prenatal infectious pathogens are independent predictors of positive UCBC.For HCA complicated with FIRS, Gram-negative pathogens such as E. coli should be prioritized in empirical antibiotic coverage.

Comparative inactivation of Tulane virus on fresh iceberg lettuce using oxidizing sanitizers and pulsed light

Teresa Paniconi, Éric Jubinville, Valérie Goulet-Beaulieu, Lee‐Ann Jaykus, Julie Jean — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.foodcont.2026.112711

Human norovirus (HuNoV) is the leading viral cause of acute gastroenteritis worldwide and is frequently associated with foodborne outbreaks linked to fresh produce, particularly to leafy greens. This study provides a comparative evaluation of the efficacy of two oxidizing sanitizers widely used in the fresh produce industry, peracetic acid (PAA), sodium hypochlorite and the non-thermal physical treatment pulsed light (PL), for the inactivation of viruses on iceberg lettuce. Lettuce samples were spot-inoculated with Tulane virus (TuV), a cultivable HuNoV surrogate, and exposed to chemical sanitizers for 90 s at different concentrations (chlorine: 50, 100, and 200 ppm; PAA: 40, 80, and 160 ppm) or to PL at fluences ranging from 1.44 to 11.52 J/cm 2 . The effect of these treatments on lettuce color, weight, and texture was also assessed. For chemical treatments, no statistically significant differences in viral reduction were observed among concentrations (p > 0.05), with average reductions of 1.7 log 10 for PAA and 2.2 log 10 for chlorine. In contrast, PL treatment achieved substantially higher levels of viral inactivation (∼ 5 log 10 reduction) across all fluences tested. Sequential application of PL (1.44 J/cm 2 ) followed by either sodium hypochlorite (50 ppm) or PAA (40 ppm) did not result in additional viral reduction, indicating a limited benefit of combined treatments under the experimental conditions used in this study. Overall, these findings demonstrate that PL is effective at inactivating surface-associated TuV on fresh-cut iceberg lettuce under controlled conditions, while chemical sanitizers provide more limited reductions, however, confirmation using infectious HuNoV systems would be required. These results provide practical insights for fresh produce sanitation strategies, while highlighting important limitations related to surface accessibility, matrix effects, and the use of a surrogate virus.

Toponet: a topology-guided dual-branch framework for medical image classification

Jin Sun, Nan Gao, Wenqi Tao, Zaifang Zhang, Chenghao Shao — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s00138-026-01950-6

Abstract Volumetric medical image classification remains challenging due to limited robustness and interpretability, largely because modern models based on CNNs and Transformers primarily rely on appearance-driven cues and struggle to explicitly capture global anatomical topology, such as connectivity, loops, and cavities. To address this limitation, we propose TopoNet, a topology-guided dual-branch framework that integrates multiscale topological structures into volumetric representation learning. Specifically, TopoNet computes cubical persistent homology to obtain persistence diagrams and distills robust topological signals from background noise via statistical confidence bands, enabling adaptive and noise-robust filtering without relying on rigid thresholds. Crucially, to overcome the spatial information loss inherent in traditional vectorization methods, the selected persistence features are mapped back to the voxel domain. This process restores spatial context to generate spatially registered topological structures, forming an explicit topology modality aligned with the input images and providing spatially grounded structural cues that improve interpretability. The image and topology modalities are encoded in parallel and fused via stage-wise cross-branch attention to facilitate complementary feature interactions across scales. We validate TopoNet on two datasets, where it consistently outperforms strong baselines across key metrics, achieving \((0.8197 \pm 0.0587)\) in Accuracy and \((0.8123 \pm 0.1127)\) in AUC on the MSD Task07 Pancreas dataset, and \((0.9333 \pm 0.0079)\) in Accuracy and \((0.9267 \pm 0.0437)\) in AUC on the BraTS 2018 dataset. Overall, these results indicate that topology-guided multimodal fusion provides a promising avenue for improving performance, robustness, and clinical interpretability in volumetric medical image classification. The code is available at https://github.com/hbqdcq/TopoNet.

A Review of FITS Data Structures and Scientific Metadata for Astronomical Machine Learning

Dargham Fadhil Awwad, Zahraa Ch. Oleiwi, Salwa Shakir Baawi — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.66157/jisca.1.2.37

The Flexible Image Transport System (FITS) is a widely adopted standard for storing, exchanging, and analysing astronomical data. Developed in 1979, formally described in 1981, and endorsed by the International Astronomical Union in 1982, FITS combines multidimensional numerical arrays and tabular data with structured metadata that support scientific interpretation. This narrative review examines the historical development and internal architecture of FITS, including Header/Data Units, data types, and header keywords relevant to photometric calibration, coordinate transformations, and observational provenance. It compares FITS with consumer and general-purpose raster formats, distinguishing the preservation of numerical values from the retention of astronomical metadata and calibration context. Representative applications in major space missions and observatory pipelines illustrate the role of FITS in managing images, spectra, and time-series data. The review also discusses the preparation of FITS-based data for artificial intelligence and machine learning, including calibration, quality filtering, normalization, and metadata integration. Particular attention is given to the distinction between archival data containers and the numerical arrays or tensors used by computational models. FITS provides a well-established foundation for reproducible astronomical analysis, although scientific validity and model performance depend on data quality, calibration, preprocessing, and methodological choices rather than file format alone. The review offers an educational foundation for students and researchers developing computational methods for astronomical classification and physical parameter estimation.

Aging alters the distribution, stability, and transcriptional signature of engram cells

Miguel Fuentes-Ramos, Juan Paraíso‐Luna, Patricia Torres-Raves, Isabel Bustos-Martínez, Marta Alaiz-Noya — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-79049-9

ABSTRACT While aging impairs memory precision, its effects on engram dynamics and gene expression remain poorly understood. To address this, we used TRAP2 activity-reporter mice, nuclear tagging, and FOS-based activity mapping to track neurons activated during contextual fear memory encoding and reactivated during recall in young and aged mice. Across 378 brain regions, we quantified engram size, spatial distribution, and reactivation stability. We further applied fluorescence-activated nuclear sorting (FANS) combined with single-nucleus RNA sequencing (snRNA-seq) to characterize gene expression changes associated with memory encoding and recall across diverse cell types. In addition, we compared the transcriptional profiles of first-time versus second-time neuronal responder cells in the dentate gyrus. Aged brains exhibited altered engram allocation, reduced reactivation stability, and distinct gene expression patterns during memory retrieval. These findings reveal age-related changes in the organization and molecular identity of memory traces, providing mechanistic insight into cognitive decline and highlighting potential targets for intervention.

Specification of bone marrow sinusoids requires TIE2-mediated positive feedback involving COUP-TFII and VEGFR3

Xiao Li, Xiwen Jia, Zhiliang Sun, Taotao Li, Beibei Xu — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-11244-2

Abstract The bone marrow (BM) vascular network plays crucial roles in driving bone development and supporting hematopoiesis, yet the mechanisms governing its specialized architecture, particularly sinusoidal morphogenesis, remain inadequately characterized. We show in this study that TIE2 ( Tek ) was highly expressed by BM sinusoidal endothelial cells (SEC) and the endothelial Tek excision led to BM sinusoidal capillarization. Particularly, the BM sinusoids displayed thinner vessel diameter with the aberrant mural cell coverage in the Tek mutants. Mechanistically, TIE2 insufficiency led to a dramatic decrease of VEGFR3 in BM-SECs while its expression in hepatic sinusoids was not obviously altered. The RNA-seq analysis showed that GO terms enriched for the downregulated genes were related to the biological processes including sinusoidal development while pathways related to arterial ECs and angiogenesis were upregulated in the bone marrow of Tek mutants. The alteration of sinusoidal VEGFR3 expression occurred within 48 h after the induced endothelial deletion of Tek . Consistently, the defective BM sinusoidal formation was validated with the induced Tek deletion in VEGFR3 + SECs. The insufficiency of TIE2 ligand ANGPT1 also led to reduced sinusoidal VEGFR3, accompanied by similar BM sinusoidal defects. Furthermore, disruption of sinusoidal morphogenesis was observed in mutant mice with the endothelial excision of Nr2f2 (COUP-TFII), displaying a decreased expression of BM sinusoidal TIE2 and VEGFR3. These findings suggest that ANGPT1/TIE2 and COUP-TFII form a reciprocal regulatory loop to coordinate BM sinusoidal specification via regulating VEGFR3.

FGFR4 Enhances Extracellular Vesicle-Associated Output and Promotes STAT1-Dependent LGALS3BP Enrichment in Colorectal Cancer

Dae-Hwan Kim, Jina Choi, Jun Eul Hwang, HYUN JEONG SHIM, Hyun Jin Bang — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21873/cgp.20622

BACKGROUND/AIM: Fibroblast growth factor receptor 4 (FGFR4) contributes to colorectal cancer (CRC) progression, but its effects on extracellular vesicle (EV)-associated proteins remain unclear. Transcriptomic profiling of A549 cells following acrylonitrile exposure

李文静(Wenjing Li) — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26036/cnp0010302

This project aims to investigate transcriptomic alterations associated with acrylonitrile (AN) exposure in human A549 lung adenocarcinoma cells. Acrylonitrile is a widely used industrial chemical and environmental pollutant with potential toxicological effects on the respiratory system. Understanding the transcriptional responses of pulmonary cells to acrylonitrile exposure is important for investigating its potential molecular toxicity and biological effects. Human A549 lung adenocarcinoma cells were used as the experimental model and divided into two groups: an untreated control group (BL) and an acrylonitrile-treated group (AN). Cells in the treatment group were exposed to 100 uM acrylonitrile for 24 hours, while cells in the control group remained untreated. Each group consisted of three samples, resulting in a total of six samples for RNA sequencing. RNA sequencing (RNA-seq) was performed to characterize gene expression profiles in both experimental groups. The sequencing data were generated to enable comparisons of transcriptional patterns between untreated and acrylonitrile-exposed cells. Subsequent bioinformatics analyses included data preprocessing, gene expression quantification, differential expression analysis, and functional enrichment analysis to investigate biological processes and molecular pathways potentially associated with acrylonitrile exposure. This dataset contains paired-end RNA sequencing data from six A549 cell samples, including three control samples and three acrylonitrile-treated samples. The deposited raw sequencing data provide a resource for reproducible transcriptomic analysis, comparative gene expression studies, and further investigation of cellular responses to acrylonitrile exposure. These data may support future research in environmental toxicology, chemical exposure assessment, and pulmonary cell biology.

Threadfin: clone-level analysis of B-cell states from paired single-cell RNA-seq and BCR-seq

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Chen Satoshi — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23824521

Threadfin infers relationships between B-cell clones from paired single-cell RNA and B-cell receptor sequencing. Each clone defined by its receptor is represented by the distribution of transcriptional states among its sampled cells, estimated relative to cells from the same individual or sample and shrunk according to the number of cells sampled. Clones are compared in a clone embedding, whose layout is chosen for each dataset so that continuous populations of clones remain connected, in clonal programmes, and with permutation tests that respect the sampling design.

Exploring the role and mechanism of methionine metabolism in chronic rhinosinusitis with nasal polyps based on bioinformatics analysis

Dan Lin — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bjorl.2026.101917

OBJECTIVE: Chronic Rhinosinusitis with Nasal Polyps (CRSwNP) affects 1%-4% of the global population with high recurrence rates. While methionine metabolism abnormalities contribute to various inflammatory diseases, their role in CRSwNP pathogenesis remains unclear. This study aimed to systematically investigate the role of methionine metabolism in CRSwNP and identify potential biomarkers and therapeutic targets through integrating multi-omics data and interpretable machine learning approaches. METHODS: CRSwNP transcriptomic datasets (GSE136825, GSE179265) and single-cell data (GSE276503) were obtained from GEO database. Single-cell RNA sequencing analysis, differential expression analysis using limma, and Weighted Gene co-expression Network Analysis (WGCNA) were performed to identify methionine metabolism-related candidate genes. Three machine learning algorithms (LASSO, SVM-RFE, XGBoost) with SHAP interpretation screened core genes. Functions and mechanisms were explored through single-gene GSEA, ceRNA network construction, transcription factor prediction, immune infiltration analysis, and pseudotime trajectory analysis. Potential therapeutic drugs were identified via molecular docking. RESULTS: Among 14 candidate genes, CSTB, IGFBP3, and TCN1 were identified as core genes with excellent diagnostic performance (AUC > 0.85). SHAP analysis revealed CSTB and IGFBP3 as disease risk-promoting factors, while TCN1 showed protective effects. Single-cell analysis demonstrated all three genes were predominantly expressed in epithelial cells with significant differential expression patterns. They were enriched in primary immunodeficiency pathways and significantly correlated with immune cell infiltration, particularly M2 macrophages and activated dendritic cells. Molecular docking identified five potential therapeutic drugs including ambroxol, carbamazepine, and curcumin with favorable binding energies. CONCLUSION: Methionine metabolism participates in CRSwNP pathogenesis by regulating CSTB, IGFBP3, and TCN1, affecting epithelial cell function and immune microenvironment. These genes represent promising biomarkers for diagnosis and potential therapeutic targets, providing theoretical basis for developing metabolic-immune intervention strategies in CRSwNP management.

Multi-layered immune regulatory networks in cotton against Verticillium wilt

Jiarui Li, Bingjian Yuan, Mengyao Wei, Chaofan Li, Jiawang Qin — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2026.124701

Cotton ( Gossypium spp.), a globally important economic crop, is severely threatened by Verticillium wilt caused by Verticillium dahliae ( V. dahliae ). This disease affects more than 40% of cotton fields in China and causes annual economic losses of 2.5-3.1 billion US dollars. Therefore, disease resistance breeding must take into account the inherent fitness trade-offs between disease resistance and fibre yield-quality performance. This review proposes that transcriptional regulation driven by cotton endogenous factors represents the central tier of the immune network underlying cotton resistance to Verticillium wilt. On this basis, epigenetic modifications and post-translational modifications (PTMs), which are co-orchestrated by the cotton host and pathogen effectors, act synergistically to shape a multi-layered immune regulatory architecture. At the transcriptional level, we systematically elaborate cotton endogenous core defense pathways mediated by Ca²⁺ signaling, reactive oxygen species (ROS) homeostasis, accumulation of antifungal metabolites, pathogenesis-related (PR) proteins, and their intricate crosstalk among phytohormone signaling pathways that coordinate defense responses. At the epigenetic level, we highlight the regulatory functions of DNA methylation, histone modification, chromatin remodeling, and ncRNAs during the interaction between cotton plants and V. dahliae . In particular, histone acetylation remodels chromatin status to regulate the transcriptional levels of TFs. At the PTM level, we summarize how phosphorylation, ubiquitination and SUMOylation dynamically regulate protein activity and stability to fine-tune the interaction between cotton and V. dahliae . Finally, we discuss emerging strategies for enhancing cotton resistance to Verticillium wilt, including AI-assisted multi-omics integration, CRISPR-based genome editing, structure-guided protein engineering, rhizosphere microbiome manipulation, and bionanotechnology applications. This review provides a framework for understanding cotton- V. dahliae interactions and offers valuable insight for the molecular breeding of disease-resistant cotton cultivars.

Spatial multi-omics of mouse incisor mesenchyme reveals metabolic remodeling during the MSC-to-TAC transition

Yuling Wang, Zexi Chen, Tingting Chen, Yue Zhao, Xiaoqian Yu — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41368-026-00469-w

The continuously growing mouse incisor provides a useful model for investigating stem cell niche organization and the MSC-to-TAC transition because it contains spatially distinct mesenchymal stem/stromal cell (MSC)-enriched and transit-amplifying cell (TAC)-enriched compartments and abundant TAC populations. However, the metabolic programs associated with MSCs and their transition to TACs within the native tissue environment remain poorly understood. Here, we integrated region-resolved transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics with single-cell RNA sequencing to characterize metabolic remodeling associated with the MSC-to-TAC transition in the mouse incisor pulp. We found that the MSC-enriched apical pulp was enriched for molecular features associated with fatty acid β-oxidation and ketone body metabolism, whereas the TAC-enriched mitotic pulp showed molecular signatures associated with the tricarboxylic acid cycle, oxidative phosphorylation, and nucleotide biosynthesis. Integrated transcriptomic and proteomic analyses revealed lower mRNA-protein concordance in the MSC-enriched apical pulp, consistent with a contribution of post-transcriptional regulation, whereas the TAC-enriched mitotic pulp showed tighter mRNA-protein coupling. Metabolite profiling further showed higher abundance of acyl-carnitines and ketone bodies in the MSC-enriched apical pulp and higher abundance of glutamine, aspartate, and purine intermediates in the TAC-enriched mitotic pulp. Pseudotime analysis suggested coordinated changes in transcriptional programs related to fatty acid oxidation, mitochondrial oxidative metabolism, and nucleotide synthesis along an MSC-to-TAC trajectory. Collectively, our study establishes a region-resolved multi-omics atlas of metabolic remodeling associated with the MSC-to-TAC transition in the mouse incisor and provides insights into the metabolic features associated with stem cell activation, progenitor expansion, and tissue homeostasis in the native dental stem cell niche.

Integrative multi-omics analysis and experimental validation identify MARK3 as a key ubiquitination-related regulator of cardiac fibroblast activation in heart failure via the TGF-β1/Smad3 pathway

Fangting Xu, Jintian Zhang, Zihan Yu, Huayi Tan, Shibiao Zhang — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13019-026-04695-5

Heart failure (HF) is a complex cardiovascular disease with high heterogeneity. The ubiquitin pathway plays a crucial role in various pathological processes, but its specific mechanisms in HF development and its relationship with the immune microenvironment remain unclear. Bulk RNA-seq datasets (GSE141910, GSE116250) and a single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) dataset (GSE222144) were obtained from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database. Differentially expressed genes (DEGs) analysis, weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA), and ubiquitination-related gene sets (URGs) were integrated to identify candidate genes. Machine learning algorithms selected key genes and constructed a nomogram prediction model. Immune infiltration (CIBERSORTx), competing endogenous RNA (ceRNA)/transcription factor (TF) regulatory networks, scRNA-seq characterisation, and Mendelian randomisation (MR) analysis were performed. For experimental validation, primary neonatal mouse cardiac fibroblasts (NMCFs) were isolated and treated with 10 µM isoproterenol (ISO) for 24 h. Cell viability (CCK-8), reactive oxygen species (ROS), inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α, IL-1β), fibrotic markers (α-SMA, Collagen I), and expression of the four key genes were assessed by quantitative PCR (qPCR) and Western blot. MARK3 was overexpressed via pcDNA3.1-MARK3 transfection, and a rescue arm with recombinant TGF-β1 (10 ng/mL) was included to interrogate the TGF-β1/Smad3 signalling axis. Twenty ubiquitination-related candidate genes were identified, from which four key genes—CADPS2, DTX4, USP51 and MARK3—were selected by machine learning. The nomogram showed good predictive performance (AUC > 0.7). scRNA-seq revealed that fibroblasts chiefly expressed these genes and exhibited enhanced intercellular communication in HF. MR analysis indicated that low CADPS2 and MARK3 expression and high DTX4 expression may be associated with increased HF risk, whereas the estimate for USP51 was directionally consistent but did not reach statistical significance. Experimentally, ISO significantly impaired NMCF viability, elevated ROS, upregulated TNF-α, IL-1β, α-SMA and Collagen I, downregulated CADPS2 and MARK3, and upregulated DTX4 and USP51, consistent with bioinformatic predictions. MARK3 overexpression markedly attenuated ISO-induced oxidative stress, inflammation and fibrotic trans-differentiation while suppressing TGF-β1 and Smad3 phosphorylation. Exogenous TGF-β1 reversed these protective effects, confirming pathway dependence. This study identifies MARK3 as a protective regulator of cardiac fibroblast activation that functions at least partially through inhibition of the TGF-β1/Smad3 axis, providing a mechanistic basis and candidate therapeutic target for ubiquitination-directed anti-fibrotic strategies in HF.

Exploratory analysis of relevant circRNA-mediated ceRNA networks in left ventricular cardiac tissue and liquid biopsy of heart failure individuals

Camilo Rebolledo, Montserrat Hevia, Juan Pablo Silva, Sebastián Urquiza-Zurich, Francisca Villagran — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13062-026-00896-z

Heart failure (HF) is a complex cardiovascular syndrome characterized by extensive molecular remodeling involving coding and non-coding RNAs. Among these, circular RNAs (circRNAs) have been proposed as potential regulators of gene expression, through competing endogenous RNA (ceRNA)-like mechanisms. In this study, we performed an integrative transcriptomic analysis using publicly available cardiac tissue RNA-seq datasets to identify differentially expressed circRNAs, miRNAs, and mRNAs associated with HF. Predicted RNA-RNA interactions were used to assemble putative circRNA-mediated ceRNA networks, followed by functional enrichment analyses to highlight biologically relevant pathways and molecular axes. PRMT6–SIRT5–HADHA signaling safeguards osteoblasts from R-loop-induced DNA damage during age-related osteoporosis

Xinman Song, Shanshan Huang, Xiaoxuan Guo, Chunna Gao, 岳广娜 — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41420-026-03354-x

Abstract Age-related osteoporosis is characterized by impaired bone formation and progressive deterioration of trabecular microarchitecture. Declining osteoblast abundance and function, together with the accumulation of cellular senescence, are recognized as key contributors to skeletal aging. During aging, increased genomic instability and persistent DNA damage responses further impair osteogenic capacity. Aberrant accumulation of R-loops (RNA–DNA hybrids) can induce transcription–replication conflicts and DNA damage; however, the regulatory mechanisms maintaining R-loop homeostasis during osteoblast aging remain largely unknown. SIRT5, a key mitochondrial desuccinylase that regulates metabolic adaptation through protein lysine desuccinylation, has been implicated in cellular stress responses, but whether SIRT5 controls R-loop homeostasis and bone aging through succinylation remodeling remains unclear. Public mouse bone marrow single-cell RNA-seq datasets from GEO were integrated to delineate age-dependent changes in bone marrow cellular composition and the osteoblast lineage. Naturally aged mice as well as Sirt5 −/− and Prmt6 −/− models were analyzed, and skeletal phenotypes were assessed by micro-computed tomography and histological examination. In vitro, primary osteoblasts were subjected to replicative senescence and H₂O₂-induced oxidative stress. RNA-seq, lysine succinylation proteomics, S9.6-based R-loop CUT&Tag, Western blotting, and immunofluorescence were employed to characterize molecular alterations and phenotypic outcomes. Causality was further tested using AAV5-mediated Sirt5 overexpression in vivo and in vitro as rescue approaches. Single-cell analysis revealed a reduction in the proportion of osteoblasts during aging accompanied by decreased Sirt5 expression. Compared with wild-type controls, Sirt5 −/− mice exhibited more pronounced trabecular bone loss and impaired osteogenesis, with reduced Runx2, ALPL, and OCN and elevated p16/p21, decreased ALP activity, and increased SA-β-gal-positive cells. Transcriptomic profiling indicated activation of stress-related programs and suppression of osteogenic and metabolic pathways. Mechanistically, Sirt5 deficiency caused global succinylation accumulation and reshaped the succinylation landscape of metabolic proteins, leading to elevated multi-site succinylation of HADHA and reduced HADHA protein abundance. Concurrently, R-loops were broadly increased around transcription start site (TSS) regions, accompanied by elevated S9.6 and γH2AX signals and enhanced DNA damage accumulation. Prmt6 deficiency downregulated Sirt5 and phenocopied Sirt5 loss, including trabecular deterioration and increased R-loop signals. AAV5-mediated Sirt5 overexpression restored HADHA expression, attenuated R-loop accumulation and senescence phenotypes induced by oxidative stress or Prmt6 loss, and alleviated trabecular bone loss. Together, these findings identify the PRMT6–SIRT5 axis as a critical regulator of osteoblast aging. Disruption of this pathway promotes succinylation remodeling, compromises R-loop homeostasis, and enhances DNA damage accumulation, thereby accelerating osteoblast senescence and age-related bone loss. Targeting the PRMT6–SIRT5 signaling pathway may represent a potential therapeutic strategy for preventing osteoporosis associated with aging.

Fusion evidence supports computational attribution of neoplastic and microenvironmental states in tenosynovial giant cell tumor

Shiyou Ren, Ya Ding, Dan Li, Xilong Cui, Haiyang Yu — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s12672-026-06234-z

Tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT) contains rare neoplastic cells with colony-stimulating factor 1 (CSF1) rearrangements within a macrophage-rich lesion, complicating attribution of overlapping tumor and microenvironmental expression programs. We reconstructed patient-specific fusion junctions from public long-read RNA sequences from three TGCT patients, requiring concordant short-read alignment, exact junction k-mers, barcodes, and unique molecular identifiers. Fusion-supported, state-concordant cells provided operational training anchors. Analyses included leave-one-patient-out (LOPO) program transfer, external cohort projection, and a conditional linear profile-mixture simulation. A separate 19-library cohort underwent caller-guided, source-restricted sequence auditing. Across three biologically independent patients, 590 cross-platform consensus junction events identified 165 fusion-supported cells, including 111 state-concordant training anchors. Descriptive LOPO areas under the curve were 0.683, 0.803, and 0.939, not independent validation estimates. The 30-gene program had higher point estimates in fibroblast lineages but lower estimates in whole-synovium cohorts; none of five projection contrasts remained significant after joint correction. The CSF1 receptor (CSF1R) myeloid core was directionally elevated in all three external cohorts, with Benjamini-Hochberg-significant support only in GSE3698. The matrix axis remained provisional. Joint non-negative least-squares estimation improved recovery under the simulation assumptions. Fusion evidence supports computational cell-state attribution in TGCT, but biological replication remains limited to three patients. The program is not established as a whole-tissue biomarker, and the simulation does not establish clinical deconvolution. Independent tissue and clinical validation are necessary.

Integrative network toxicology and single-nucleus transcriptomics reveal molecular overlap between predicted 6PPD-quinone targets and diabetic kidney disease

Siyuan Song, Qiling Zhang, Xizi Li, Peipei Zhou, Ying Tan — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12882-026-05473-0

Abstract Purpose The renal implications of the tire-derived contaminant 6PPD-quinone (6PPD-Q) are not well defined. We conducted a hypothesis-generating computational study to identify molecular overlap between predicted 6PPD-Q activity and diabetic kidney disease (DKD). Methods Predicted human targets of 6PPD-Q were integrated with DKD-associated differentially expressed genes and co-expression modules. Functional enrichment, training-restricted machine learning with SHAP interpretation, immune deconvolution, WGCNA sensitivity analysis, virtual knockout, and single-nucleus transcriptomic analyses were performed. The final model was locked and evaluated once in two external datasets (GSE30528 and GSE104948). Single-nucleus disease-group comparisons were conducted at the donor level, with donors rather than individual nuclei treated as biological replicates. Results Intersection of 1,313 predicted 6PPD-Q targets, 2,326 DKD differentially expressed genes, and 936 genes from positively DKD-associated co-expression modules identified 44 candidate genes enriched in mitochondrial, metabolic, xenobiotic-processing, and inflammatory pathways.
