Blending With Insulating Polymers Enables Stable Anti‐Ambipolar Characteristics in n‐Type Organic Mixed‐Conductors for Heterogeneous Spiking Neurons

Zachary Laswick, Ariel Lifer, Catherine Beaumont, Liad Hayoun, Priscila Cavassin — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/adma.75538

Mammalian neurons exhibit diverse structural and physiological properties: a heterogeneity not present in current neuromorphic systems. Recent advancements have led to bio-realistic spiking systems which are based on the integration and long-term operation of organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) with anti-ambipolar characteristics. However, the library of materials enabling anti-ambipolarity (AA) is restricted by the lack of stable, fast, and scalable organic mixed ionic and electronic conductors (OMIECs) exhibiting this specific OFF-ON-OFF behavior. This complicates mimicking specialized neuron types. Blending semiconducting with insulating polymers is a classic organic and OMIEC electronics strategy to improve device performance and enable tunability, yet unexplored for improving anti-ambipolar stability in non-ladder-type anti-ambipolar materials. Here, the n-type OMIEC, P(gNDI-gT2), is blended with the insulating polymer, PMMA, yielding stable anti-ambipolar characteristics. The miscibility in solution forms a mechanically stabilizing and ion-permeable framework in the solid state that considerably reduces swelling-induced structural damage, thus preventing dramatic failure after the first operation cycles without compromising the conductive polymer's electronic pathways. Modulating the polymer blend ratio enables low-power, fast-spiking Hodgkin-Huxley neurons with tunable and programmable dynamics, without optimized device geometries, demonstrating frequency deconvolution and reservoir computing. These two computing primitive building blocks are essential for designing efficient spiking neural networks.

Chronic exercise induces a pathway-dependent electrophysiological signature in the mouse hippocampus

Pablo Muela, Julia Makarova, Ricardo Muñoz-Arnaiz, Juan B. Aballay, Elisa Cintado — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-11267-9

Chronic physical exercise enhances hippocampal function, yet its impact on network activity remains poorly defined. We explored whether treadmill running leaves durable imprints detectable through electrophysiological techniques and spatial transcriptomics. Field potentials (FPs) were recorded in anesthetized male C57BL/6 J mice after a chronic exercise protocol. Using machine learning to analyze FP generators, we were able to reliably classify animals into their correct group by Lateral Perforant Path (LPP) activity alone. Classification rested on a multifeatured signature rather than any single feature. Schaffer Collateral and Lacunosum-Moleculare generators showed changes that did not achieve statistical significance. Exploratory spatial transcriptomics identified a coordinated enrichment of calcium signaling and long-term potentiation programs in the dentate gyrus. These findings demonstrate that exercise-induced plasticity is not a global shift but a localized remodeling of specific synaptic inputs, with changes detectable at both circuit and molecular levels. These durable, pathway-specific imprints persist in the absence of active task engagement, suggesting a reorganization of the brain’s memory circuitry.

Hydroxysafflor yellow A ameliorates autism-like phenotypes and attenuates ADAM10-associated neuroimmune alterations: an integrative multi-omics and in vivo study

Lei Wu, Mengzhen Wei, Qiong Zhang, Lu Chen, Lei Jia — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12967-026-08999-x

Autism spectrum disorder (ASD) is a heterogeneous neurodevelopmental condition characterized by synaptic dysfunction and neuroimmune imbalance. Current pharmacological treatments for ASD remain largely focused on managing associated symptoms rather than modifying core disease mechanisms, whereas the molecular regulators linking neuroimmune dysregulation to neural circuit abnormalities remain incompletely characterized. Identifying such regulators and exploring whether they are associated with pharmacologically responsive alterations may provide new insights into ASD pathophysiology and potential intervention strategies. We integrated Mendelian randomization, transcriptomic analysis, machine learning, and single-cell RNA sequencing to identify and prioritize ASD-associated candidate genes. Pseudotime, cell–cell communication, and functional enrichment analyses were performed to characterize the regulatory context of the prioritized candidates. Molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations were used to assess the structural plausibility of the interaction between hydroxysafflor yellow A(HSYA) and A disintegrin and metalloproteinase 10 (ADAM10), followed by in vivo evaluation of HSYA treatment in a valproic acid (VPA)-induced ASD mouse model through behavioral, histopathological, and molecular analyses. Fourteen candidate genes were identified through the integration of Mendelian randomization and transcriptomic analyses. Among these candidates, ADAM10 was further prioritized based on its diagnostic performance and feature contribution in machine-learning analyses. Single-cell analysis showed that microglia were among the cell populations with relatively high proportions of ADAMA10 -expressing cells and revealed dynamic ADAM10 expression across microglial states. Functional enrichment analyses implicated inflammatory signaling, protein processing, and cell adhesion pathways. Cell–cell communication analysis indicated increased microglia–neuron communication in the high- ADAM10 -expression group. Molecular simulations supported a potential interaction between HSYA and ADAM10. In the VPA-induced ASD model, HSYA improved ASD-like behaviors, reduced neuronal damage and glial activation, and reduced ADAM10 protein expression. By integrating genetic, transcriptomic, machine-learning, cell-type-resolved, structural simulation, and in vivo evidence, this study prioritized ADAM10 as an ASD-associated regulatory candidate and further linked ADAM10 expression to microglial states, neuroimmune regulation, and intercellular communication. HSYA ameliorated ASD-like phenotypes and was associated with reduced ADAM10 protein expression in vivo, supporting further investigation of ADAM10-related neuroimmune alterations as potential targets of pharmacological modulation in ASD. These findings extend current evidence implicating ADAM10-related alterations in ASD by providing complementary multi-level evidence. However, the specific contribution of ADAM10 to HSYA-associated effects requires further mechanistic clarification.

"BIOMARKERS IN PERSONALIZED DRUG THERAPY: CURRENT ADVANCES, CLINICAL TRANSLATION, RESEARCH GAPS, AND FUTURE PERSPECTIVES"

Sakshi N. Rajwade, Amol Poapat Darwade, Rothe Pragati, Arati B. Salunke, Ashwini N. Sangale — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23834990

Personalized drug therapy aims to improve treatment efficacy and safety by considering individual genetic, molecular, physiological, and clinical characteristics. Biomarkers support patient stratification, treatment selection, dose optimization, response monitoring, and prediction of adverse drug reactions. This critical narrative review evaluates current advances, clinical applications, translational challenges, research gaps, and future perspectives related to biomarkers in personalized drug therapy. Pharmacogenomic biomarkers help explain interindividual differences in drug pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, efficacy, and toxicity. In addition, multi-omics, immune, imaging, liquid-biopsy, and digital biomarkers are expanding the scope of individualized pharmacotherapy. These approaches have shown important clinical utility in oncology and are increasingly being explored across cardiovascular, neurological, immunemediated, metabolic, and other therapeutic areas. However, clinical translation remains constrained by inadequate analytical and clinical validation, assay variability, limited representation of diverse populations, difficulties in interpreting rare genetic variants, uncertain clinical utility, high testing costs, ethical concerns, and limited integration into routine healthcare. Biomarkers can support a shift from empirical prescribing toward more precise and individualized drug therapy. Their successful implementation will require prospective clinical validation, standardized testing, diverse population datasets, regulatory harmonization, integration of multiomics and clinical information, and evidence of real-world clinical and economic benefit. A combined approach incorporating pharmacogenomics, therapeutic drug monitoring, modelinformed precision dosing, and artificial intelligence may provide a more comprehensive foundation for individualized drug therapy.

The Integrative Mechanism of Xiaoyaosan Through Liver-Brain Crosstalk in the Treatment of Depression Combined with MASLD

Yunhang Chu, Ming Yang, Jiahui Liang, Xinri Zhang, Yan Leng — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.2147/dddt.s650753

Abstract: Depression and metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD) frequently co-occur, and their bidirectional pathological interactions complicate clinical management. Liver-brain crosstalk provides a systematic framework for understanding these multi-organ connections. Xiaoyaosan (XYS), a classical formula for soothing the liver and relieving depression, has been investigated for its therapeutic potential in both conditions; however, most available evidence remains preclinical. We systematically reviewed 158 original studies graded by evidence level and synthesized the pharmacological mechanisms of XYS. The available evidence suggests that XYS may restore liver-brain communication through vagal, humoral, and gut microbiota pathways. At the vagal pathway level, XYS has been reported to inhibit aberrant afferent transmission of intestinal pro-inflammatory signals to the central nervous system while concurrently enhancing cholinergic anti-inflammatory efferent output, thereby potentially restoring the afferent-efferent balance of autonomic innervation. At the humoral pathway level, XYS may restore the negative feedback function of the hypothalamic-pituitary-adrenal (HPA) axis, redirect tryptophan metabolic flux away from excessive diversion toward the kynurenine pathway, and alleviate neuroinflammation through upregulation of monoamine neurotransmitters and inhibition of microglial M1 polarization. At the gut microbiota pathway level, XYS may reshape microbial community structure and its metabolic profile, restore the microecological barrier and intestinal mucosal permeability by increasing the abundance of short-chain fatty acid-producing bacteria and reducing enterogenous endotoxin load. Two integrative nodes, namely the serotonergic redirection of tryptophan flux and the systematic restoration of gut microbiota, are proposed to underlie the dual efficacy. These nodes may form a bidirectional closed loop that links peripheral metabolic signals with central neuroplasticity. Current evidence has notable limitations, including poor clinical relevance of disease models, insufficient causal inference, questionable dose extrapolation, and lack of temporal dynamics. Future studies should prioritize randomized controlled trials in comorbid populations, multi-omics biomarker discovery, and temporal causal network analysis to facilitate clinical translation. Keywords: Xiaoyaosan, depression, metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease, liver-brain crosstalk, homotherapy for heteropathy

Multi-omics and functional analyses link tubular ZDHHC12-mediated SMAD3 palmitoylation to renal fibrosis after ischemia–reperfusion injury

Haoxun Zhang, Xinyu Liu, Yaxuan Wang, Guoling Zhang, Xuran Ji — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13062-026-00986-y

Renal ischemia–reperfusion injury (IRI) causes acute tubular damage and can progress to renal fibrosis, but the molecular regulators linking early injury to fibrotic remodeling remain unclear. Here, we integrated mouse renal RNA sequencing, GSVA, four public GEO datasets, machine-learning-based feature selection, single-cell RNA sequencing, spatial transcriptomics, human kidney specimens, and functional validation to investigate palmitoylation in IRI-associated fibrosis. Transcriptomic analysis showed time-dependent changes after IRI, with early metabolic disruption followed by inflammatory and extracellular matrix-related responses. GSVA revealed increased palmitoylation scores at days 1 and 3 after reperfusion. Integrated bioinformatic analysis prioritized Zdhhc12 as a candidate gene, and validation experiments showed sustained ZDHHC12 upregulation from day 1 to day 14 after IRI. Single-cell and spatial analyses identified proximal tubular cells as a major site of increased palmitoylation activity and ZDHHC12 expression. ZDHHC12 was also elevated in human post-ischemia/reperfusion renal tissues and TGF-β1-stimulated renal tubular epithelial cells. Functionally, ZDHHC12 knockdown reduced TGF-β1-induced Collagen I and α-SMA expression in vitro, whereas renal tubule-specific ZDHHC12 knockdown alleviated tubular injury, inflammation, renal dysfunction, and fibrosis in vivo. Mechanistically, IRI and profibrotic stimulation were associated with palmitic acid accumulation, increased global S-palmitoylation, and enhanced SMAD3 palmitoylation. ZDHHC12 knockdown suppressed SMAD3 palmitoylation, whereas ZDHHC12 overexpression promoted SMAD3 palmitoylation and profibrotic signaling, which were attenuated by 2-bromopalmitate. Site-directed mutagenesis and in silico analysis further identified Cys421 as a critical palmitoylation site of SMAD3, linking ZDHHC12-mediated palmitoylation to fibrotic activation. These findings identify ZDHHC12-mediated SMAD3 Cys421 palmitoylation as a mechanism linking renal IRI to fibrosis.

Diversity analysis of actinomycetes from the gut of Pheretima aspergillum and bioactive secondary metabolites from an anti-Enterococcus faecalis strain

Wenbin Liu, Xiang Zhang, Ruyu Zhang, Wenna Zheng, Lijun Wang — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12864-026-13527-8

Abstract Background Antimicrobial resistance is one of the most pressing global public health challenges, with vancomycin-resistant Enterococcus (VRE) listed as a high-priority pathogen by the World Health Organization. Animal gut environments harbor actinomycetes with unique biosynthetic potential due to long-term coevolution with hosts. Pheretima aspergillum , a medicinal earthworm used in traditional Chinese medicine, continuously ingests complex organic substrates, creating selective pressures that may drive the evolution of novel secondary metabolic pathways in its gut symbionts. However, the diversity, culturable resources, and anti-VRE potential of gut actinomycetes from P. aspergillum remain largely unexplored. Results This study presents the first systematic characterization of the gut actinomycete diversity of P. aspergillum . High-throughput 16 S ribosomal RNA (16 S rRNA) amplicon sequencing revealed that the gut bacterial community of P. aspergillum is highly diverse, comprising 21 phyla, 75 classes, 141 orders, 263 families, 501 genera, and 608 putative species. Actinomycetota ranked as the third most dominant phylum (10.2% relative abundance). A total of 414 culturable gut-associated bacterial isolates were recovered using eight culture media, of which 332 (80.2%) were actinomycetes; 26 of the 414 isolates (6.3%) were identified as putative novel species. Strain CB1-10-1, identified as Streptomyces chartreusis , exhibited broad-spectrum antimicrobial activity against vancomycin-resistant Enterococcus faecalis (VREfa) and other pathogens. Whole-genome sequencing and genome mining identified 38 biosynthetic gene clusters (BGC). One type II polyketide synthase cluster showed a high-confidence match to the characterized benastatin BGC (BGC0000204.5). Integrated metabolomic analysis further confirmed the production of benastatin analogues. Guided by this multi-omics strategy, benastatin B and benastatin C were isolated and structurally identified, representing the first report of benastatin production by S. chartreusis . Moreover, this study is the first to demonstrate that benastatin B exhibits potent anti-VREfa activity, with a minimum inhibitory concentration of 2 µg/mL and a minimum bactericidal concentration of 4 µg/mL – both superior to vancomycin – and it shows bactericidal properties. Conclusions This study establishes P. aspergillum as a productive source of actinomycete diversity harboring potential novel species. The multi-omics-guided discovery approach led to the identification of benastatin B as a promising bactericidal lead compound against VREfa infections, providing proof-of-concept for the value of animal symbiotic actinomycetes in antimicrobial drug discovery and a foundation for future mechanistic and preclinical studies.

Sustained delivery of 3D-bioprinted stem cell-derived small extracellular vesicles promotes neural regeneration following traumatic brain injury via the CREB1/PGC-1α/FNDC5 axis

Ying Ai, Qian Zhang, Tiange Chen, Lin Lin, Ziyang Chen — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bioactmat.2026.10.030

Background Clinical translation of stem cell-derived small extracellular vesicles (sEVs) for traumatic brain injury (TBI) is restricted by low yield in 2D culture and rapid in vivo clearance. Objective To develop an in situ sustained delivery system (3D-sEVs@Gel) encapsulating 3D-bioprinted adipose stem cell (ADSC)-derived sEVs (3D-ADSC-sEVs) within GelMA (Gelatin methacryloyl) hydrogel for long-term functional repair after TBI. Methods SEVs derived from coaxially bioprinted 3D-ADSCs were isolated, and a GelMA hydrogel-based delivery system was subsequently constructed. Following orthotopic transplantation of the 3D-sEVs@Gel system into the lesion site after TBI, functional recovery, neural stem cell (NSC) proliferation and lineage-associated responses, and the underlying molecular mechanisms were systematically evaluated through behavioral tests, immunofluorescence, multi-omics, and pharmacological inhibition. Results SEVs generated using the coaxial alginate-microfiber culture system exhibited an approximately eightfold higher yields and protein loading compared with those obtained from 2D culture. In vivo , 3D-sEVs@Gel prolonged local retention, reduced tissue loss, and significantly improved long-term sensorimotor coordination and spatial memory following TBI. In addition, treatment increased the numbers of proliferating cells co-expressing neural progenitor-, neuronal-, and oligodendroglial-lineage-associated markers within the examined neurogenic regions. Mechanistically, 3D-sEV treatment increased nuclear CREB1 levels in NSCs, promoting PGC-1α transcription and subsequent FNDC5 expression, thereby supporting endogenous neurogenesis and functional neural repair. Conclusion The 3D-sEVs@Gel system promotes chronic neurological reconstruction post-TBI by modulating NSC proliferation and neural lineage-associated responses through the CREB1/PGC-1α/FNDC5 pathway, offering a promising cell-free strategy for neural repair and tissue engineering.

Multi-omic cancer subtype clustering with random-walk-refined cross-view contrastive learning

Liting Zhang, Zefeng Li, Xian Mallory — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-77559-6

Cancer heterogeneity complicates precision oncology, and robust multi-omic subtyping remains challenging. Existing contrastive integration methods often force heterogeneous omics into a single shared latent space and treat all non-matching samples as negatives, which can suppress modality-specific structure and separate biologically similar patients. We present Subtype-DIVIDE, a self-supervised multi-view contrastive framework for integrative cancer subtyping. The model encodes each omic modality separately and performs target-conditioned cross-view alignment, using the target modality’s affinity structure to guide inter-view consistency. To reduce false negatives, Subtype-DIVIDE further refines contrastive supervision with per-omic random-walk soft-positive masks constructed from modality-specific affinity graphs. Across nine TCGA cohorts spanning copy number, DNA methylation, mRNA, and miRNA data, Subtype-DIVIDE achieved statistically significant survival separation in all datasets and the highest mean number of clinically enriched variables among the compared methods. Cox models, leave-one-cluster-out analyses, and technical covariate tests supported the clinical relevance and robustness of the inferred subtypes. Ablation studies showed that cross-view alignment is essential, while diffusion-based soft positives improved robustness. A KIRC case study identified coherent subtype programs involving metabolism, vascular and inflammatory signaling, extracellular-matrix remodeling, and epithelial transport. External validation on two independent cohorts, performed without hyperparameter retuning, supported the generalizability of the inferred subtypes. In 222 CPTAC-3 clear-cell renal carcinoma cases, the subtypes were significantly associated with pathologic stage and T category across all training repeats and recapitulated the KIRC subtype programs. In 1,980 METABRIC breast tumors, they consistently showed significant survival separation and enrichment for age, nodal status, and stage across all repeats.

Salivary Multi-Omics Biomarkers Using Artificial Intelligence for Early Oral Cancer Detection and Risk Classification

Sharmin Akter Sabah Bakht, Musomi Khandaker Shamsunnahar Chadni, Amena Begum Runa Ramesh Khatri, Asif Hassan Malik Farhana Yeasmin Rita, Fahima Akter Nila Pritom Das — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.66838/j.carcinog.25.1.703-719

Background: Oral cancer continues to be a significant health issue worldwide, and a late diagnosis is highly correlated with poorer treatment outcomes and shorter life expectancy. While these two steps (examining a patient and obtaining tissue cultures) are essential to this diagnosis (previously routine), they do not help to detect the patient in time at the population level. A minimally invasive, biological fluid such as saliva has many molecular components that may mirror pathological changes occurring in the oral cavity. Salivary omics domains could thereby yield a better assessment, through a combination of the domains, of the risk of oral cancer at an early stage. Artificial intelligence (AI) may further enhance the analysis of complex molecular patterns by integrating information from multiple salivary biomarker domains. Such approaches may support the identification of potential early indicators of oral cancer and improve the classification of individuals according to their estimated disease risk. Objective: This study aimed to evaluate the potential of multi-omics salivary biomarkers (genomic/transcriptomic, proteomic, metabolomic, and microbiome) for the early detection and risk classification of oral cancer. The study also highlights the potential application of AI-based analytical approaches to integrate multi-omics biomarker information and clinical characteristics for improved oral cancer risk classification. Methods: The approach applied was quantitative cross-sectional and analytical, and involved a number of respondents of 279 people. Demographic and behavioural risk factors and the perception of being assessed for these risk factors by biomarkers were investigated, along with oral symptoms, clinical findings, and salivary multi-omics classifications. Normality test and Cronbach's Alpha reliability test, Bartlett's Test of Sphericity, and KMO Test, Independent Samples t test, One-Way Analysis of Variance, Kruskal-Wallis, Chi-Square Test of Independence, Pearson correlation and regression analysis were used in statistical analysis. Results: The results showed a good level of reliability with an average Cronbach's Alpha of 0.901 - 0.938. Supporting construct validity is the value of kmo which is excellent (0.959), and the Bartlett's test result shows that the p-value is less than 0.001. Gender difference, differences in age groups, differences in category of areca use and cooked chew (areca/users, non-users), differences in Betel chewing (areca, non-users) were the significant differences. The overall multi-omics risk classification was well correlated with the clinical examination status, χ² (9) = 429.967, p < 0.001. Overall multi-omics risk revealed significant associations between domains of major biomarkers, with the highest between clinical examination and overall multi-omics risk. The regression analysis resulted in a highly significant predictive model of overall risk classification (R = 0.943, R² = 0.889, p < 0.001), which explained a great variance of risk predictors that were included in the model. Conclusion: Results showed that the multi-omics biomarkers of saliva were a prevalent candidate for use as a minimally invasive tool to aid early detection/classification of OCD. Combination of use of molecular biomarker domains with clinical examination may lead to improved identification of patients likely to show symptoms of a clinical disorder that would warrant early diagnosis referral. The disease diagnosis performance needs to be further tested in other populations before the wide-scale application of the system. Future studies should investigate the integration of salivary multi-omics biomarkers using appropriately validated AI models, with particular attention to early cancer detection, risk classification, model interpretability, and external validation across independent populations.

Early Warning Systems for AI Performance Degradation: A Multi Metric Predictive Framework

Shaymaa Kaseb Layus — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.25195/ijci.v52i2.756

The extensive use of artificial intelligence systems in manufacturing settings has posed significant pressure on ensuring the sustainability of performance in the long term. Although the development of models has improved, AI systems are still vulnerable to slow performance decline due to data drift, changes in concepts, and resource variability, which can silently build up until major failures happen. Nevertheless, the current monitoring methods are restricted by the use of a single metric assessment, use of reactive threshold-based warning systems and also being reliant on labeled data, which are inadequate in early and reliable detection of degradation. To overcome these shortcomings, the following paper will suggest a multi-metric predictive early warning framework to detect and predict AI performance degradation at an early stage. The suggested system will combine ten metrics of heterogeneous performances such as predictive accuracy, operational efficiency, resource usage, and statistical drift signals in a single monitoring architecture. More complex feature engineering algorithms are used to detect acute anomalies and long-term degradation trends, including rolling statistics and rate-of-change analysis, spectral decomposition. The framework is a combination of various complementary detection techniques, such as statistical process control, cumulative sum (CUSUM), exponentially weighted moving averages (EWMA), changepoint detection, and multivariate anomaly detection, to produce a composite warning index with graduated alert levels. Moreover, a predictive component predicts system health, and assists in planning interventions proactively. The framework is confirmed by large-scale simulation experiments with realistic degradation curves, periodically varying, and stochastic noise behavior in production AI systems. Findings indicate that the suggested method identifies degradation on average 14.3 days before approaching critical levels. It has a good discriminative performance with AUC = 0.89, whereas at the warning threshold (0.6) it has a true positive rate of 84.7% and a false positive rate of 8.9% and at the critical threshold (0.8), its true positive rate is 91.2% and false positive rate is 12.3% respectively. Moreover, the system has shown good anomaly detection performance with up to 94 percent of the abnormal events detected and can make accurate short-term prediction with a mean absolute error of 0.021 when forecasting one-day-ahead health index. Visual analytics dashboards and action-oriented decision-support outputs support the framework, increasing the interpretability and usefulness of the outputs in operations. On the whole, the suggested framework will provide a solid base of proactive and trustworthy AI performance tracking, allowing shifting towards the proactive and data-driven management of the system in a real-life deployment setting.

Covariant Bitemporal Geometrodynamics (CBG) Foundational Principles, Spectral Geometry, and Exact Symmetry Reductions of the Five-Dimensional Orbifold M3_2

Manuel Jiménez Velasco — 2026-10-31 — openalex

Standard (3+1)-dimensional General Relativity and Kaluza–Klein scalar-tensor extensions confront five foundational mathematical and structural problems: the arbitrariness of the spacetime dimensionality (d_t, d_s, D) and metric signature, the presence of off-diagonal graviphoton ghost modes (g_{μ2}) and closed timelike loops on unprojected S¹ fibers, the ad-hoc introduction of scalar kinetic parameters (ω_BD), matter-coupling constants (α_0), and scalar potentials V(ψ), the breaking of four-dimensional Maxwell conformal invariance in D ≠ 4 bulk geometries, and the occurrence of horizon and interior curvature singularities (K_5 → ∞) for uncoupled scalar fields under Bekenstein's no-hair identity and the Janis–Newman–Winicour (JNW) singularity theorem. Genetic and Physiological Responses of Sunflower to Nitrogen Availability: Implications for Sustainable Agriculture

Ziyad Ahmed Abed, F.F. Alogaidi, Z.K. Alshugeairy, حاتم حسن جاسم — 2026-10-31 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17582/journal.pjar/2026/20260410134545

Sunflower is an important crop for farmers and consumers.A field experiment (RCBD, three biological replicates) at the College of Agriculture -University of Baghdad evaluated two genotypes (Ishaqi1, Ishaqi2) under four nitrogen rates (0, 100, 200, 300 kg N ha-1).Morphological and yield traits were recorded and expression of gene AY941250 at flowering was measured by RT-qPCR using 18S rRNA for normalization.MIQE-compliant validation of the qPCR assay (primer specificity, efficiency, melt curves, and reference gene assessment) is provided in Methods and Supplementary Materials.Results show that higher N was associated with increased plant growth, yield components and higher AY941250 transcript levels in both genotypes; Ishaqi1 displayed the largest fold increase under the highest N.These findings are associative: they document correlation between N supply and AY941250 expression but do not establish causation.Functional confirmation (e.g., gene knockdown/overexpression and independent qPCR validation) is required to determine the gene's role in nitrogen uptake or use efficiency.

CausalWAM: Learning the Causes of Actions in World Action Models

Juntao Cheng, Jinzhou Tang, Fan Feng, Henrik I. Christensen, Yujia Zheng — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

World action models (WAMs) learn latent variables from observations and use these to both predict the evolution of the scene and to generate actions. The prediction has to be of the whole scene, whereas only a portion of the scene causes actions. WAMs fitted to demonstrations do not, in general, guarantee that they will generate actions solely from the latent causes of the demonstrated actions (the action-relevant latent variables), as the model could just as well learn to imitate in this way whilst entangling the action-relevant latent variables with the nuisance variables. We therefore separate out what causes action from what prediction depends on. We prove that under a sparsity constraint the latent variables used to generate actions are an invertible function of the action-relevant latent variables alone and the true structure is identified up to a permutation of the latent variables. This requires neither sufficient temporal dependence nor any assumptions on how the unobserved regimes evolve and holds even when the action-relevant latent variables are statistically dependent on the rest. We therefore propose CWAM (Causal WAM), which incorporates the constraint into a pretrained WAM by adding a sparse routing path to its action head, with the world modeling objective remaining unchanged. Experiments on both synthetic data and real-world robots validate the theory as well as showcase the improved efficiency in closed-loop success.

Variance-adaptive Chernoff bounds: nonasymptotically valid, asymptotically sharp confidence sets for unbounded means

Aaditya Ramdas, Diego Martinez-Taboada — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Consider the classical statistical problem of sub-Gaussian mean estimation under martingale dependence: given $n$ observations with conditional mean $μ$ and a known conditional sub-Gaussian proxy $s>0$, we seek a nonasymptotically valid confidence interval for $μ$. The usual Chernoff interval has half-width $s\sqrt{2\log(2/α)/n}$; it neither adapts to the actual standard deviation $σ\le s$ nor attains the Gaussian quantile $z_{1-α/2}$. We construct an analytically closed-form nonasymptotically-valid interval under martingale dependence whose endpoints, at every iid law with $σ> 0$, agree almost surely to first order with $\bar X_n\pmσz_{1-α/2}/\sqrt n$. The two new building blocks are a sub-Gaussian exponential supermartingale with an \emph{empirical} quadratic variance and a cubic correction term, and a sub-Gaussian continuation supermartingale, the former delivering variance adaptivity ("Empirical-Bernstein'' for unbounded data), the latter also providing the Gaussian quantile ("Empirical-Berry-Esseen'' for unbounded data). The same constructions yield a closed-form time-uniform confidence sequence with asymptotically unimprovable half-width $σ\sqrt{2\log\log n/n}$. We also give closed-form multivariate ellipsoids with the true-covariance chi-square limit, and extend the scalar Gaussian and LIL constructions to handle heavy-tailed settings where one only knows a bound on any absolute central moment of order greater than two. Experiments show substantial width reductions when the true variance is smaller than the implied variance bound, at small loss in the boundary cases. The surprising (to us) takeaway message is: given any more than two moments, one can construct fully data dependent nonasymptotic confidence sets for the mean that are asymptotically efficient.

On Statistical-Computational Gaps in Graph Combinatorial Testing Problems

Ikjun Choi, Pratik Patil, Alessandro Rinaldo — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study high-dimensional graph combinatorial hypothesis testing problems in which a signal is supported on the edge set of an unknown subgraph drawn from a class invariant under vertex relabeling. For Gaussian observations, we characterize sharp statistical and computational thresholds (at polynomial order) for the signal magnitude required for strong detection across various combinatorial graph alternatives. Our analysis is based on bounding the second moments of the full and low-degree likelihood ratios in terms of the overlap of two independent latent structures and comparing the resulting lower bounds with scan, average, and coordinatewise maximum tests. Under an appropriate low-degree conjecture for computational hardness, we obtain planted-clique thresholds matching those known for Gaussian submatrix detection and identify new statistical-computational gaps for multiple planted cliques and hypercliques, thick cycles and thick paths. We also establish a novel transfer principle across natural exponential family observation models: matching the one-coordinate chi-square divergence preserves full likelihood-ratio second moments exactly and makes low-degree second moments equivalent up to constant factors under a degree condition. Under local regularity, we obtain corresponding testing upper bounds in terms of an effective signal strength. When these upper and lower bounds match, they yield universal threshold scales in the local alternative regime. As a consequence, statistical-computational gaps for planted-clique detection in Erdős--Rényi settings can be deduced from the Gaussian model.

Statistical Foundations of Reinforcement Learning: A Non-Asymptotic Perspective

Yuxin Chen, Yuejie Chi, Jianqing Fan, Gen Li, Yuting Wei — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Reinforcement learning (RL) embodies a foundational paradigm for sequential decision-making in the face of uncertainty. The rapid growth of model complexity in emerging applications has significantly amplified the challenges of achieving data-efficient RL, particularly in sample-starved scenarios where data collection may be costly, time-consuming, or even high-stakes. Consequently, understanding and improving the data efficiency of RL algorithms has become a central problem of both fundamental theoretical interest and practical importance. Despite decades of research, the statistical underpinnings of RL remained remarkably underdeveloped until relatively recently, particularly in the finite-sample regimes most relevant to modern practice. Over the past few years, there has been a surge of activity advancing the frontiers of non-asymptotic statistical RL theory, drawing on ideas from high-dimensional statistics and probability, information theory, and statistical learning theory. In this monograph, we present a non-asymptotic treatment for RL based on tabular Markov decision processes, focusing on three canonical sampling mechanisms --- RL with a generative model, online RL, and offline RL --- together with two algorithmic paradigms: model-based and model-free approaches. Centered around non-asymptotic sample complexity analysis, our treatment emphasizes minimax-optimal algorithm design, particularly methods that attain statistical optimality without significant burn-in costs, as well as sharp statistical characterizations of several widely used RL algorithms. Whenever possible, we provide largely self-contained analyses together with intuitive explanations. We also briefly discuss several extensions, such as robust RL, competitive multi-agent RL, federated RL, uncertainty quantification, and RL from human feedback.

Stable and efficient covariance estimation for sample L-moments using Hájek projections

Jihong Park, Palakorn Seenoi, Jeong-Soo Park — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

L-moments are useful summary statistics and are widely used in statistical analysis. The joint covariance matrix of sample L-moments not only describes their sampling uncertainty but is also needed for covariance-weighted distances and related statistical procedures. An exact unbiased covariance estimator is already available. However, in our previous applications, this estimator sometimes produced singular or indefinite covariance matrices. Such matrices cause problems when matrix inversion is required. The present paper addresses this difficulty. We examine standard positive-definite corrections and two covariance estimators based on leave-one-out sample L-moments: the delete-one jackknife estimator and an estimator based on the Hájek projection. Both are positive semidefinite by construction. Simulation results show that the projection estimator generally has smaller estimation error and better numerical stability than the exact unbiased estimator. The jackknife estimator overestimates the variances of higher-order sample L-moments and has larger estimation error than the projection estimator. We also develop fast rank-prefix algorithms for the three covariance estimators considered. These algorithms substantially reduce computation time.

PDE-Residual-Informed Kriging

Yu Tan, Shichang Du, Yongxiang Li — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Physics-informed Gaussian process (GP) methods provide a probabilistic framework for PDE solving. Existing approaches incorporate PDE information in different ways: pointwise conditioning uses the governing equation only at finitely many selected locations, globally constrained GPs restrict the admissible functions to satisfy the PDE exactly, and residual-penalized methods control the PDE mismatch over the whole domain through an additional penalty weight. What is still missing is a GP formulation that directly incorporates global PDE-residual information of the entire domain into its covariance structure, while retaining model flexibility and standard GP conditioning without a separate residual-weight parameter. To solve this issue, this study proposes PDE-residual-informed Kriging (PRIK) based on a PDE-residual kernel. The PDE-residual kernel is constructed by equipping a kernel-section space with an inner product defined by the $L^{2}$ inner product of PDE residuals. This construction makes PRIK yield the posterior mean with minimum integrated squared PDE residual. It also provides direct statistical interpretations: the variance-scale estimate equals the normalized PDE-residual energy (i.e., the integrated squared PDE residual of the posterior mean divided by the number of labeled observations), and the profile likelihood balances this residual energy against model complexity. Under the stated regularity and stability conditions, we establish $H^{2}$-quasi-optimality and convergence rates for the posterior mean, together with a nonasymptotic bound for its PDE-residual norm. We further show that the normalized posterior kernel converges to a variational Green-type kernel and derive a uniform nonasymptotic contraction bound for the posterior variance. Numerical studies provide empirical evidence consistent with the theory and demonstrate the PDE-solving capability of PRIK.

Advancing Specific Local Wine Identification through Integration of ICP-MS Mineral Wine Profiles and Machine Learning: A Case Study on Discrimination Among the Ten Beaujolais Crus

Yohann Clément, Pierre Lantéri, Agnès Hagège, Bertrand Chatelet, Théodore Tillement — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

The Beaujolais vineyard, located in France's Auvergne-Rh{ô}ne-Alpes region, is internationally recognized for its geological diversity and the strong identity of its ten crus. Guaranteeing authenticity of these appellations is essential for producers and regulators, as mislabeling and fraud can undermine both economic value and consumer trust. In this study, we introduce the Mineral Wine Profile (MWP), a high-throughput ICP-MS protocol enabling the semi-quantitative analysis of more than 40 mineral elements. A total of 1288 Gamay wines from the 2022 and 2023 vintages, collected across the ten Beaujolais crus, were analyzed. Multivariate statistical methods and machine learning were applied to assess their discriminatory power. Principal Component Analysis clearly separated vintages, while Random Forest models achieved 98% accuracy for vintage classification. Mixture Discriminant Analysis further differentiated the ten crus with an average accuracy of 92%. Misclassifications mostly occurred between geographically neighboring crus, reflecting overlapping soil structures and terroir continuity. Element-soil correlations (e.g., Fe enriched in clay-rich soils, Mn in sandy soils, Cs in silty soils) provided mechanistic insights into terroir differentiation. These results confirm that wine mineral fingerprints can act as reliable chemical memories of origin. By combining elemental profiling with Machine Learning, this work demonstrates a robust, reproducible, and scalable approach for intra-regional wine authentication. Beyond its scientific contribution, the MWP approach offers practical applications for the valorization of terroirs, protection of Appellations d'Origine Contr{ô}l{é}e (AOC), and fraud prevention in the wine industry.

Truthful and Admissible Principal--Agent Testing with Nuisance Parameters

Aaditya Ramdas — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Statistical contracts reward experimenters according to the evidence produced by a costly study, so the reward rule influences both who participates and what they report. Principal--agent testing theory explains how to prevent ineffective proposals from being profitable. We ask when such contracts can also elicit honest parameter reports if the decision concerns a mean effect but the variance or distributional shape is unknown. Under risk neutrality and a fixed participation cost, we construct menus of e-values --- nonnegative normalized rewards with expected value at most one under every null --- such that (i) agents who know they are null have no incentive to participate, (ii) every alternative type earns positive expected profit and is thus incentivized to participate, (iii) truthful reporting uniquely maximizes expected their reward, (iv) and each contract is admissible: its payouts cannot be pointwise improved while retaining null validity. For Gaussian observations, we treat separately agents who know both mean and variance and agents who know only their mean; in the latter case, truthfulness holds at every variance without requiring a nuisance prior. Multi-sample extensions preserve these incentives and can reward accumulating evidence. Beyond Gaussian models, we show that two independent nonnegative observations suffice to elicit any finite mean above a positive null threshold, without any further distributional knowledge, but one observation cannot combine strict mean elicitation with individual admissibility on this class. Finally, we distinguish the existence of useful tests from the identification needed for truthful contracts. Boundedness is not required, and finitely many null constraints do not by themselves guarantee admissible elicitation.

Statistical inference for quantile correlation regression with bivariate outcomes

Feipeng Zhang, Heng Peng — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

It is increasing scientifically important to recognize, quantify and visualize the dependence structure between the bivariate outcomes. In this paper, we investigate conditional dependence using a new dependence measure, the quantile correlation coefficient, which is defined by the Pearson correlation of two quantile-hits of bivariate outcomes. Furthermore, we propose a new regression model for covariate effects directly on the quantile correlation coefficient via a generalized linear regression model. The proposed model has intuitive interpretation and provides a comprehensive view of covariate effects across quantiles of bivariate outcomes. The proposed estimator enjoys desirable asymptotic properties including uniform consistency and weak convergence as a process in the quantile index. Additionally, we develop the general hypothesis testing for the regression coefficients, with applications to summarize covariate effects across the quantile ranges. Simulation studies demonstrate the good finite sample performance of the proposed estimation and inference procedures. Two empirical applications of the proposed method reveal interesting insights in premature twins data for measuring association and ChIP-seq data for measuring reproducibility.

Overcoming the Representational Limitations of Amplitude Encoding for Quantum Neural Networks

Vincent Gurgul, David Prinz — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Quantum Neural Networks (QNNs) offer a framework for learning functions from classical data, but their expressive power depends critically on how inputs are encoded into quantum states. Angle encoding can require resources that scale with the number of features, whereas amplitude encoding represents $d$ features using only $\mathcal{O}(\log_2 d)$ qubits. This compactness comes at a representational cost: normalization makes conventional amplitude encoding invariant to nonzero real rescaling of the input, up to an unobservable global phase. We show that this invariance induces an exact, architecture-independent restriction on the resulting hypothesis class. No downstream quantum circuit can distinguish inputs differing by nonzero real scaling. We develop a general condition under which deterministic preprocessing can remove this ambiguity and show that nonlinearity alone is insufficient. Based on this analysis, we introduce a fixed nonlinear feature map that provably distinguishes all real inputs under subsequent amplitude encoding, without adding trainable parameters and while retaining logarithmic qubit scaling. We evaluate the resulting QNN on three canonical classification benchmarks and a nonlinear synthetic dataset against conventional amplitude and angle encoding, as well as a classical neural network. Across these tasks, the proposed encoding consistently improves classification accuracy over both quantum encoding baselines by 21$-$44 %. Our results establish normalization-induced scale invariance as a concrete representational bottleneck of amplitude encoding and demonstrate that it can be removed while preserving a compact quantum representation, marking a step toward quantum models capable of capturing arbitrary relationships in classical data.

Genetic weight of a mutant in a biparental Moran model with infinite selection

Camille Coron, Olivier Hénard — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study, after Coron Le Jan (2024), the genetic contribution of a single strongly advantageous mutant in a biparental Moran model under infinite selection. We prove a functional limit theorem for the entire beneficial mutation spreading period: after normalization by \(\sqrt N\), the contributions carried by the advantaged and disadvantaged subpopulations converge jointly in law to a non-degenerate random multiple of an explicit deterministic profile. Randomness is generated during the initial microscopic growth of the mutant lineage and subsequently propagated through the population along a deterministic macroscopic trajectory. At the heart of this phenomenon is an eigenmode martingale that measures the imbalance between the mean genetic weight of the founding ancestor within the two classes; its limiting value provides precisely the random amplitude of the macroscopic profile and is characterized by a smoothing equation.

Solar powered membrane distillation for sustainable desalination from configuration selection to deployment readiness

Muthia Elma, Aulia Rahma, Evia Salma Zaurida, Rhafiq Abdul Ghani, Arun Dwi Nugroho — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s42452-026-09135-6

Water scarcity is increasingly coupled with energy insecurity, particularly in remote and arid regions where conventional desalination is constrained by electricity demand, brine management, and infrastructure cost. Solar powered membrane distillation (SPMD) is attractive because MD can operate with low- to moderate-temperature heat supplied by solar thermal collectors, photovoltaic-assisted subsystems, waste heat, or hybrid energy inputs. This review synthesizes SPMD from an integrated engineering perspective rather than as a catalogue of isolated technologies. A structured literature-selection procedure was applied using database searches, duplicate removal, keyword screening, eligibility criteria, and a quality check focused on configuration, membrane, module, energy, techno-economic, sustainability, and pilot-readiness evidence. The synthesis shows that DCMD provides simple operation and high flux but remains vulnerable to conductive heat loss; AGMD and PGMD improve heat recovery but typically reduce vapor transport; and CGMD/MGMD concepts attempt to recover internal heat while limiting mass-transfer resistance. The revised analysis further demonstrates that collector choice must be matched to the MD temperature window, thermal storage strategy, module geometry, membrane wetting resistance, and deployment scale. Major barriers are wetting, scaling, module heat loss, membrane lifetime, collector and storage cost, inconsistent reporting of GOR/STEC/LCOW, and limited long-duration outdoor validation. Future SPMD research should therefore prioritize collector–module co-design, durable omniphobic or low-impact membranes, standardized performance normalization, brine-management strategies, and field demonstrations that report reliability under real solar variability.

Integrative systems biology identifies shared neuroimmune co-expression programmes across pulmonary tuberculosis and Alzheimer's disease

Oluwaseun Oluwatosin Taofeek, Solomon Adesola Adigun, Elijah Kolawole Oladipo, Aganze Gloire-Aimé Mushebenge, Afrah Khairallah — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11528962/v1

Abstract Background : There is growing evidence that pulmonary tuberculosis (PTB) exerts long-lasting systemic effects on brain function, whereas Alzheimer's disease (AD) is increasingly recognized as a systemic neuroimmune disorder. BCG vaccination studies suggest that tuberculosis-associated immune pathways influence AD-related biology. However, whether PTB-associated co-expression programmes exhibit concordant activity in AD and display reproducible neuroanatomical organization within the human brain remains unknown. We hypothesized that PTB-associated co-expression programmes would exhibit concordant transcriptional activity in AD, retain aspects of their underlying co-expression architecture across diseases, and identify shared neuroimmune mechanisms relevant to chronic infection and neurodegeneration. We implemented an integrative systems biology framework combining whole-blood transcriptomics, weighted gene co-expression network analysis, functional enrichment, immune-cell deconvolution, and machine learning to identify PTB-associated co-expression programmes and prioritize biomarkers. These programmes were validated in two independent PTB cohorts, evaluated in an AD dataset, and mapped onto the Allen Human Brain Atlas to characterize neuroanatomical organization. Results: Active PTB was associated with widespread transcriptional remodeling, with 1,389 dysregulated genes organized into 16 PTB-associated co-expression modules. Nested machine learning identified a 12-gene biomarker signature that demonstrated excellent diagnostic performance in the discovery cohort (AUC ≈ 0.99) and retained performance in two independent external PTB datasets without model retraining. Majority of PTB-derived immune co-expression modules exhibited concordant activity in the independent AD cohort. Formal module-preservation analysis demonstrated that several PTB-derived networks retained moderate preservation in AD, supporting partial conservation of co-expression architecture across diseases. Brain transcriptomic mapping demonstrated reproducible region-specific organization across adult brain donors. Integrated neuroanatomical contextualization prioritized a subset of PTB-derived neuroimmune modules using cross-disease transcriptional activity, regional specificity, and inter-donor reproducibility, revealing neuroimmune transcriptional programmes shared across infection and neurodegeneration. Conclusions: Our findings establish a systems-level framework for investigating shared neuroimmune processes across chronic infection and neurodegeneration while identifying reproducible co-expression modules and candidate biomarkers for future studies.

Spatial multicellular coordination networks are associated with neoadjuvant immunotherapy response in hepatocellular carcinoma

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Prateek Mittal, Joohi Chauhan, Deepu Philip, Prabal Pratap Singh — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-74108-z

Abstract The response to immune checkpoint blockade in hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) remains highly variable, and the abundance of immune cells alone has been shown to be insufficient as a predictive biomarker. We analyse spatial transcriptomics data from seven neoadjuvant cabozantinib- and nivolumab-treated HCC patients to test whether the therapeutic outcome is associated with spatial coordination of multicellular programs rather than their presence. Modeling immune, stromal, and malignant transcriptional programs as continuous spatial fields, we introduce a graph-based framework that quantifies their organization using field energy and inter-program gradient coupling. Across tumors, immune exhaustion and cytotoxic programs display a comparable spatial structure irrespective of response, indicating that immune presence is not discriminatory. In contrast, responding tumors exhibit structured cancer-associated fibroblast organization and permissive malignant-stromal interfaces, whereas non-responders show pronounced malignant-CAF segregation and immune exclusion. Integrating three biologically motivated coupling terms, we define a Spatial Immune Coordination Index (SICI) that shows directionally consistent separation of response states across 21 of 27 coefficient parameterisations under sign-flip sensitivity testing, independent of immune abundance. Malignant–CAF coupling is the primary discriminating metric (Cohen’s $$d=2.40$$ , Cliff’s $$\delta =+1.00$$ , $$p_{\textrm{exact}}=0.029$$ , $$n=7$$ ). In an exploratory bulk RNA-seq analysis in TCGA-LIHC ( $$n = 533$$ ), coordination-informed composite scores show statistically significant but modest associations with overall survival. These hypothesis-generating findings suggest that spatial coordination topology is associated with the immunotherapy response in HCC and provide a principled graph-based framework for developing and evaluating future spatial biomarkers.

Expression programs (NMF), module scores, PROGENy pathway and CytoSig cytokine inference, cell–cell communication, contact-molecule comparisons and scPagwas genetic associations. • Spatial analyses. Visium CytAssist deconvolution and spatial programs across LUAD progression stages, and HALO-based multiplex immunofluorescence analyses of in-house treatment-naive and neoadjuvant chemo-immunotherapy cohorts (cell densities, nearest-neighbor distances and association with pathological response). • TWAS. Transcriptome-wide association study in the OTTERS framework using GTEx lung eQTL weights and ACAT, and construction of the TWAS score. • Bulk-cohort and clinical analyses. Construction of the AM score (contrast of the AM-2 and AM-4 signatures), correlation analyses across eight LUAD/NSCLC cohorts, and exploratory trial-stratified Cox models of the relative progression-free survival effect of atezolizumab versus docetaxel in the OAK and POPLAR trials. • Figure scripts. In-house mIF data are available from the corresponding authors on reasonable request. Software environment. Analyses were run in Python (scanpy, scikit-learn, CellTypist) and R (Seurat, survival). Package versions are given in the manuscript Methods and in the repository’s environment files. Citation. TMC8 is associated with a paradoxical immune landscape and promotes clear cell renal cell carcinoma progression

Kuanyin Wang, Xingshu Zhu, Minghui Li, Lingling Lin, Na Lin — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fonc.2026.1979912

Background Clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC) progression is closely linked to a complex tumor microenvironment (TME), yet key regulators remain unclear. This study aimed to systematically investigate the roles of TMC8 in ccRCC, focusing on its immunological and biological potential. Methods Multi-cohort transcriptomic data were integrated to analyze TMC8 expression and prognostic value. Functional enrichment and multiple immune deconvolution algorithms were applied to characterize TME associations. Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) analysis characterized TMC8 -associated transcriptional features across immune-cell populations. Sex-stratified analyses were performed to identify dimorphic effects. In vitro and in vivo experiments were conducted to validate the biological functions of TMC8 . Results TMC8 was significantly upregulated in ccRCC and associated with poor overall survival, particularly in males. Its expression correlated with higher estimated immune infiltration and antigen-presentation-related signatures, alongside elevated expression of exhaustion-associated markers. scRNA-seq analysis suggested the uncoupling of T-cell activation and effector competence. Notably, TMC8 exhibited marked sex-specific associations, with stronger oncogenic and immunological associations in males. Functionally, TMC8 promoted tumor progression by enhancing proliferation, migration, and invasion, which were suppressed upon TMC8 knockdown in vitro and in vivo . Conclusion TMC8 is associated with dysregulated oncogenic signaling and an immunologically active yet dysfunctional TME in ccRCC, with pronounced sex-specific associations, and may represent a putative therapeutic target warranting further validation.

Atmospheric Cold Plasma Induced Modification of the Carob Germ Protein Isolate

Halil İbrahim Odabaş — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/foods15213896

Carob germ is a protein-rich industrial by-product derived from locust bean gum processing. This study evaluated the potential of atmospheric cold plasma (CP) as a physical modification strategy to improve the structural and techno-functional properties of the protein isolated from carob germ (CGP) over treatment durations of 5, 10, and 15 min. SDS-PAGE and FTIR analyses revealed that CP treatment preserved the subunit composition and main functional groups of CGP. However, deconvolution of the Amide I region demonstrated a shift from ordered β-sheets toward flexible random coils. 5-min CP exposure induced partial protein unfolding, which significantly increased surface hydrophobicity, free sulfhydryl content, and solubility. Simultaneous TGA/DSC analysis indicated that short-term CP treatment altered the thermal response of CGP, with the 5-min treatment showing the highest thermal decomposition rate, whereas the highest denaturation temperature and enthalpy were observed after 10 min. Techno-functional evaluations revealed that 5-min CP exposure produced the highest emulsifying activity index and a 2.1-fold increase in emulsion stability index. Extending CP treatment to 10 min maximized the foaming capacity, whereas prolonged 15-min exposure led to reduced solubility and emulsifying performance while maximizing water holding capacity. Overall, moderate CP treatment (5 min) serves as an effective, eco-friendly physical modification method to enhance the techno-functionality of CGP.

Kinetic analysis of MgB 4 O 7 :Dy,Na thermoluminescence under large heating-rate-dependent glow-peak shifts

I.A. Zakharchuk, D.S. Daibagya, Анна Владимировна Осадченко, Alexander Selyukov — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1142/s0217984926502684

Temperature lag between a thermoluminescent sample and the heating system can distort glow curves and the kinetic parameters derived from them. The main MgB 4 O 7 :Dy,Na glow peak was recorded at heating rates of 1–8 K s -1 and analyzed using the Chen, Hoogenstraaten, initial-rise, and computerized glow-curve deconvolution methods. An increase in peak temperature from 439.6 to 569.6 K was observed. Following a common model-based temperature transformation, effective activation-energy estimates of 0.762–0.966, 0.443, 0.555–0.820, and 0.691–0.878 eV were obtained using the four methods, respectively. The transformation increased the Hoogenstraaten estimate from 0.255 to 0.443 eV but did not eliminate the disagreement between methods. The results quantify the sensitivity of kinetic estimates to temperature treatment and data processing.

An Immunoinformatics-Based Multi-Epitope Vaccine Targeting Multiple Stages of Tuberculosis Pathogenesis

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Sema Karabudak, Sümeyye Altunok, Emre Can Buluz — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.55262/fabadeczacilik.1860907

Tuberculosis (TB), caused by Mycobacterium tuberculosis (M. tuberculosis), remains one of the leading causes of mortality from infectious diseases worldwide, underscoring the urgent need for more effective vaccine strategies. Advances in immunoinformatics have enabled rational, cost-effective vaccine design through systematic epitope identification and optimization. In this study, a multi-epitope vaccine candidate was computationally designed by integrating cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL), helper T lymphocyte (HTL), and B-cell epitopes derived from three M. tuberculosis antigens associated with distinct stages of infection: Ag85B, ESAT-6, and HspX. Selected epitopes were screened for antigenicity, immunogenicity, allergenicity, toxicity, and global population coverage, and assembled into a single construct using rational linker and adjuvant strategies. Structural modeling and refinement were performed, followed by molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations to evaluate interactions with human Toll-like receptors (TLRs) TLR2, TLR4, and TLR9. The designed construct exhibited favorable antigenic and safety profiles, structural stability, and broad population coverage. Docking, molecular dynamics, and binding free-energy analyses suggested stable and energetically favorable interactions with all three TLRs, with the strongest binding observed for the TLR9 complex. In silico immune simulations predicted robust cellular and humoral immune responses, characterized by elevated interferon-γ (IFN-γ) and interleukin-2 (IL-2) levels and the formation of immunological memory. Overall, this study presents a stage-spanning, multi-epitope TB vaccine design and provides a comprehensive immunoinformatics framework that supports further experimental validation and optimization.

Duality Vaccination through Nanodesign of Click-Ligated B-Cell Inhibitory Receptor Bridging

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Gabrielle Admans, Rory A. Hills, Diana Melnyk, Alexander A. Cohen, Claudia L. Driscoll — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.28.762652

Synthesis of precise and robust molecular architectures is critical to harness the power of the immune system. The dimensions, rigidity and multivalency of nanoparticles contribute to eliciting high levels of antibodies from vaccination. However, antibodies vary hugely in quality: some antibodies are highly effective at neutralizing pathogens, while others provide little or no benefit. Here we establish switchable and modular platforms for protein and nanoparticle assembly that program the target of antibody responses. We introduce Duality vaccination: the simultaneous delivery of activating and inhibitory stimuli for focusing immune responses. To construct activatory immunogens, we employ click biology with spontaneous amidation to functionalize plug-and-display self-assembling porous dodecahedral nanocages. We construct inhibitory immunogens using SpyMask protease-gated bispecific assembly, ligating the antigen to binders that bridge the B cell receptor to immune checkpoint receptors. This dual immunogen architecture tailors the elicited antibodies to down-modulate undesired B cell responses. We establish the effect of temporal and spatial separation of activatory and inhibitory immunogen delivery. We further explore sensitivity to valency, linker flexibility and adjuvant delivery, employing model targets and coronavirus antigens. Dual programmable functionalization identifies key nanodesign features to control in vivo outputs, providing logic-gating towards urgent challenges in the creation of protective vaccines.
