Stability and Renormalization of Jackson Networks with Non-idling Mobile Servers

Guy Fayolle, Christine Fricker — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.61102/1024-2953-mprf.2026.32.3.004

A tandem of two queues sharing a pool of servers, where users take the time to switch to the second queue, is used to model a typical pathway through an emergency department (ED), where patients undergo two consultations separated by diagnostic tests. In this article, explicit conditions for ergodicity, transience and null-recurrence are given and proven using linear Lyapunov functions. This result is extended to a Jackson network, with the key feature that the nodes share a pool of servers, with a non-idling service policy. Further, the delay times for customers to move from one node to another must be taken into account. This covers the main characteristics of models for emergency departments, namely priorities (triage) between patients. As for the tandem queue, by scaling the arrival rate and the number of servers by N, we obtain a renormalized process converging to the solution of an ordinary differential equation (ODE) subject to boundary conditions. When the system is ergodic, we discuss the solution of this ODE as t → ∞.

A Hybrid Yang Transform–Adomian Polynomial Method for Nonlinear Partial Differential Equations

Waziri I. M., N. H. Manjak, A. M. Kwami, M. S. Adamu, O. J. Okai — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.58578/mjms.v4i3.10346

Nonlinear partial differential equations (PDEs) require solution techniques that handle nonlinear terms while maintaining accuracy and practical implementability. This study presents the Yang Transform–Adomian Polynomial (YTAP) method, a hybrid numerical approach combining the Yang transform with Adomian polynomials to approximate solutions of nonlinear differential equations. The Yang transform reformulates the governing equations in the transform domain, while Adomian polynomials systematically represent nonlinear terms. Integrating these components provides a framework for constructing approximate solutions and examining their accuracy through benchmark applications. Three benchmark problems were solved to evaluate the proposed method, including linear PDEs and a nonlinear ordinary differential equation (ODE). Performance was assessed through reported approximation errors and comparison with the Kamal method. The results showed that YTAP achieved errors as low as 10⁻¹⁷ for linear PDEs. For the nonlinear ODE comparison at t = 1, the approximation error was 2.45 × 10⁻³ using YTAP, compared with 9.07 × 10⁻² using the Kamal method. These results indicate high accuracy for the tested linear problems and a lower approximation error for the nonlinear benchmark. The applications also indicated rapid convergence and straightforward implementation, supporting the potential of combining integral transformation with polynomial treatment of nonlinearities. The findings establish the method’s performance for the examples considered and provide a basis for further investigation of its applicability to nonlinear PDEs. Additional benchmarking should examine convergence behavior, computational cost, and robustness across a broader range of equations and parameter settings. Overall, YTAP offers a promising framework for obtaining accurate approximate solutions, with its wider computational advantages requiring evaluation beyond the reported benchmark cases.

What a size-only threshold for holding cancer treatment loses in a spatial tumour model

Arjun Grandhe — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11542372/v1

Abstract Gallagher et al. derived a size threshold for holding adaptive therapy between clinic visits in non-spatial ordinary differential equation (ODE) models. I tested it in one spatial agent-based model (ABM) against a tolerance of 0.05 for premature progression (progression during a treatment hold). At the derived threshold, premature progression occurred in 0.976, 0.974 and 0.480 of runs at intervals of 7, 14 and 28 days, and at most 0.014 at 42 to 84 days. The ABM’s safe threshold differed from the ODE’s own by − 4.40% to − 8.95% at 7 to 56 days, and recalibration to the ABM’s drug-free regrowth did not remove this shortfall. Most of it (84.2% to 94.1% at 7 to 28 days) arose in the step from a well-mixed stochastic model to the ABM; that step changes several features at once, so no mechanism is identified. With error-free size readings, measurement history reduced the test log-loss of predicting premature progression at equal size by 48.6%; with 10% measurement error, history was only 2.0% better than exact size. Under a common nominal risk constraint, a hold rule using history and image geometry changed time on drug by − 1.4 percentage points and restricted mean time to progression by + 4.0 days over 750 days (+ 0.95 over 365). The gain rested on two of four intervals; non-inferiority sat on the margin at 7 days and was not shown at 14. On new runs its risk was not shown to meet the tolerance at any interval (upper Wilson bounds above 0.05).

Thermal Analysis of an Exponential Longitudinally Porous Fin with Hybrid Nanoliquid Using Physics-Informed Neural Networks

K. Ganesh Kumar, K. Preethi Salian, Dalia H. Elkamchouchi, K. Jithender Reddy, Arvind Tiwari — 2026-10-30 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1142/s0217979227500123

Fins are widely used in industry, especially in heat exchangers, because they are small, light, and inexpensive. Artificial neural networks (ANNs) have become an important tool for modelling complex, nonlinear heat and flow behaviour, especially where conventional numerical methods are too costly or impractical. This study uses an artificial intelligence (AI)-based numerical approach to analyse steady-state heat transfer in an exponentially profiled longitudinal porous fin wetted with a hybrid nanoliquid. The governing ordinary differential equation (ODE) is formulated using Darcy's law and Fourier's law of heat conduction, and it is reduced to dimensionless form using suitable dimensionless variables and boundary conditions. The physics-informed neural network (PINN) solution is validated against the bvp5c solver, and the two are in close agreement. The results show that the hybrid nanoliquid and the temperature-dependent properties of the exponential fin have a significant effect on the temperature distribution. Furthermore, the PINN and bvp5c results agree well with earlier published studies.

An extension of the classic Buneman theorem from phylogenetic trees to networks

Mirko Wilde, Mareike Fischer — 2026-10-30 — arxiv

DOI : —

Phylogenetic networks provide a natural framework for representing reticulate evolutionary events such as horizontal gene transfer and hybridization. In the present manuscript, we investigate the identifiability of phylogenetic networks under (hardwired) parsimony, a natural extension of Maximum Parsimony from trees to networks. More precisely, we ask which structural features of a network can be uniquely inferred from so-called $A_k$-alignments under this criterion. While identifiability has been studied extensively for phylogenetic trees under parsimony, little is known about the identifiability of networks under hardwired parsimony. We take a first step towards addressing this gap by developing a general framework for studying identifiability under hardwired parsimony. We then use this framework to show that the tree of blobs of a phylogenetic network can be reconstructed from its $A_1$-alignment. This result can be viewed as a natural extension of Buneman's well-known splits equivalence theorem. Finally, we show that, under hardwired parsimony, cycles of length at least $5$ cannot in general be uniquely identified from character data, establishing a fundamental limitation of hardwired parsimony.

Turning time series into algebraic equations: Symbolic machine learning for interpretable modeling of chaotic time series

Madhurima Panja, Grace Younes, Chittaranjan Hens, Tanujit Chakraborty — 2026-10-29 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0358326

Chaotic time series are notoriously difficult to forecast: small uncertainties in initial conditions amplify rapidly, while strong nonlinearities and regime-dependent variability constrain predictability. Although modern deep learning often delivers strong short-horizon accuracy, its black-box nature limits scientific insight and practical trust in settings where understanding the underlying dynamics matters. To address this gap, we propose two complementary symbolic forecasters that learn explicit, interpretable algebraic equations from chaotic time series data. Symbolic neural forecaster adapts a neural network-based equation learning architecture to the forecasting setting, enabling end-to-end differentiable discovery of compact and interpretable algebraic relations. The symbolic tree forecaster builds on evolutionary symbolic regression to search directly over equation structures under a principled accuracy–complexity trade-off. We evaluate both approaches in a rolling-window one-step-ahead forecast setting using several accuracy metrics and compare against a broad suite of baselines spanning classical statistical models, tree ensembles, and state-of-the-art deep learning architectures. Numerical experiments cover a benchmark of 132 low-dimensional chaotic attractors and two real-world chaotic time series (weekly dengue incidence in San Juan and the Niño 3.4 sea-surface temperature index). Across datasets, symbolic forecasters achieve competitive one-step-ahead accuracy while providing transparent analytical equations that reveal salient aspects of the underlying dynamics.

Dynamic Behaviour of Mechanical Capping: Equilibrium-Based Analysis and MPM Modelling

R.C.J. Voeten, C.K.L. Man, E. Meshkati, Leon A. van Paassen, R. Hoedemaker — 2026-10-29 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.59490/6ad298502510b3ff1d3cb7ac

Mechanical capping is applied to create access on land reclamation projects where soft fine-grained sediments such as clay and silt are used as fill material. Although widely implemented in practice, the underlying physics governing the displacement of the capping material and underlying soft sediments during placement, and the resulting equilibrium conditions remain insufficiently understood. This study investigates the dynamic behaviour of a single lump of capping material placed on soft soil using two complementary approaches: a dynamic equilibrium-based analytical formulation and a dynamic simulation using the Material Point Method (MPM). The analytical model describes cap penetration through a force-inertia balance for which the cap is idealized as a rigid block of material that only moves vertically. The resulting Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) is solved numerically to obtain time-dependent sink profiles and steady-state depths. The MPM simulations incorporate large deformation behaviour and a Mohr–Coulomb constitutive model for the soft sediment. Comparison of both approaches shows good agreement in steady-state sink depth, while dynamic responses differ due to the simplified energy dissipation mechanisms in the analytical model. A parametric study shows that the final penetration depth is mostly influenced by the density of the cap material, whereas initial plunging height only affects the dynamic behavior. The combined analytical–numerical assessment provides improved insight into the mechanisms governing the placement of capping material on soft soils and offers a foundation for more reliable design and execution of capping operations in very soft soil environments.

A progressive ODE model of seasonal allergic rhinitis: from core Th2 axis to 21-dimensional multi- pathway integration with seasonal dynamics and directional assessment

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Qiang Zhang, Houyang Hu, Yi Yue, Wenwen Qi, Wenqing Zhu — 2026-10-29 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fimmu.2026.1971520

Background Seasonal allergic rhinitis (SAR) affects approximately 400 million individuals worldwide, yet a quantitative framework integrating the complete immunological cascade from allergen exposure to clinical symptoms is lacking. Individual pathways (Th2/IgE, TLR4/NF-κB, Th17/Treg) have been characterized in isolation, but their interactions and relative contributions to symptom generation remain poorly understood. Methods We constructed a progressively extended ordinary differential equation (ODE) model evolving from a 10-dimensional core Th2/IgE/mast cell/eosinophil axis to a 21-dimensional system integrating TLR4/MyD88/NF-κB innate immunity and Th17/Treg imbalance with seasonal priming and tolerance dynamics. The model was calibrated against published cytokine data (Leaker 2016, Linton 2025) and validated against clinical trial drug efficacy (Corren 2021, Bauer 2023). Sobol global sensitivity analysis (5,888 samples, 21 parameters) was performed. A 2,000-patient virtual cohort was generated with lognormal parameter distributions (CV = 30%), and PCA-reduced K-means clustering (PC1–PC2 of six output metrics) identified virtual subtypes. Convergence was verified by doubling the base sample size to N = 512: all S1 and ST indices changed by less than 0.013, confirming adequate convergence at the original N = 256. Results The core model reproduces the biphasic symptom profile (immediate peak 7.0 at 1.9h, late plateau 3.4 at 12-24h) and reveals a mechanistic dichotomy: the immediate peak is governed by the mast cell-mediator axis while total burden is controlled by the eosinophil-resolution axis. The 19-dimensional extension shows that TLR4 and Th17/Treg pathways primarily modulate the late-phase response (11.3% AUC reduction via IL-10 suppression). Sobol analysis identifies the IL-10/Treg axis as the 3rd-4th most important determinant of total symptom burden. The seasonal extension captures immunological priming (IL-5 + 46.1%). Three virtual subtypes map to published AR phenotypes. The model predicts that the Th17/Treg ratio shows a stronger directional association with late-phase severity than IgE, consistent with published clinical data. Conclusion This progressive ODE model provides a quantitative framework for understanding SAR pathway interactions and generates five testable hypotheses for future experimental validation. The finding that 22% of simulated individuals in the virtual cohort exhibit a severe IL-17-high phenotype supports the hypothesis that Th17/Treg-targeted therapies may benefit a biologically defined subgroup of SAR patients, warranting validation in real patient cohorts.

An ODE-based Sampler for Discrete Diffusion Models

Samson Gourevitch, Yazid Janati, Éric Moulines, Eric P. Xing, Alain Durmus — 2026-10-29 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2610.40618

The marginals of a continuous diffusion model can be reproduced by a deterministic ordinary differential equation (ODE), known as the probability flow ODE, which provides a foundation for accelerated sampling and consistency distillation. In this paper we address the question whether an analogous formulation exists for discrete diffusion models and answer it affirmatively by introducing exponential residual flows (ERFs). Our framework augments the discrete state with a continuous residual variable for each token and position. These residuals evolve deterministically according to piecewise-defined ODEs whose vector fields depend on the current discrete state and the forward and backward rate matrices of the prescribed diffusion model. The discrete state changes whenever a residual reaches zero. We prove that this construction preserves the marginals of the original diffusion. We derive a practical discretization of our sampler and specialize it to masked and uniform diffusion. The deterministic trajectories of ERFs support consistency distillation and yield empirical improvements over previous approaches across a variety of benchmarks including code and text generation tasks.

Asynchrony‐to‐averaging stabilization shift in alpine grassland restoration

Qinghui Fang, Bin He, Min Zhang, Di Lu, Wenyin Wang — 2026-10-28 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1111/1365-2745.70471

Abstract Maintaining temporal community stability during ecological restoration is essential for sustaining ecosystem services, yet how stabilizing pathways shift directionally across restoration stages remains poorly understood. Here, we investigated the temporal dynamics of three stabilizing effects (dominance, asynchrony and averaging effects) during alpine grassland restoration on the Qinghai–Tibetan Plateau over a 13‐year sequence. Temporal stability was partitioned into dominance, asynchrony and averaging effects and tracked across nine consecutive 5‐year rolling windows, integrating longitudinal monitoring (Years 1–5) with a chronosequence approach (Years 6–13). Community evenness, species richness and stability trajectories were modelled using linear mixed‐effects models, and mediation analysis examined the pathway linking evenness to the dominance effect via Taylor's scaling exponent b . Community evenness increased overall despite declining species richness ( R 2 = 0.834, p < 0.001). The composition of stabilizing pathways shifted progressively along the restoration sequence, from asynchrony‐dominated in early stages to averaging effect‐dominated in the later, chronosequence‐based windows. Notably, the averaging effect reversed from destabilizing ( ω > 1) when diversity was low to strongly stabilizing as the community developed. Evenness regulated the dominance effect primarily through Taylor's scaling exponent b , with the indirect pathway accounting for most of the total mediation effect (Sobel test: Z = −4.54, p < 0.001). Synthesis . Stabilizing pathways shift sequentially during restoration, with statistical averaging superseding asynchrony‐based stabilization in the later stages. Rather than net stability alone, it is the changing composition of stabilizing mechanisms that best characterizes how communities are stabilized as restoration proceeds. These findings identify a management opportunity in mid‐to‐late restoration, during which promoting species evenness may strengthen the mechanisms underpinning long‐term community stability.

Two-sample inference for sequence-derived feature distributions from deep sequencing data

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

James Peng, Florian Stijven, Michal Juraska, Allan C. deCamp, Elena E. Giorgi — 2026-10-27 — arxiv

DOI : —

Many biological contexts involve heterogeneous populations of related molecular variants within a single individual. For example, some viral infections such as HIV-1 exhibit substantial diversity within a single host. Advances in sequencing technology now make it possible to characterize such populations by generating up to thousands of genetic sequences from a biological sample. In many settings, each sequence can be mapped to a numeric feature, such as Hamming distance to a reference sequence. This collection of feature values defines a one-dimensional distribution that characterizes individual-level viral diversity. When comparing these features across independent groups, past analyses have typically reduced each individual's data to a single summary value, such as the mean or mode. We propose a framework that retains the full individual-level distribution as the object of analysis. Drawing on existing tools from distributional data analysis, our framework uses Wasserstein barycenters to summarize the observed individual-level distributions within groups into a single representative distribution. These group barycenters are then compared using differences in quantiles and tested for equality. A practical challenge is that the number of sequences obtained, known as sequencing depth, can vary across biological samples. Through simulation studies, we examine when low sequencing depth can distort inference and present practical strategies for addressing these issues. We then illustrate the framework using data from the HIV-1 Antibody Mediated Prevention efficacy trials and a human B cell receptor sequencing study of vaccine-elicited immune responses. By using the entire collection of sequences for each individual, our approach can reveal group differences that may be obscured by single-number summary analyses.

Novel tailormade 3D-printed glue-on horseshoes and biomechanical changes over a shoeing cycle: a pilot study

Merel Charlotte Hartmann, Jeanne I. M. Parmentier, Nikae te Moller, Harold Brommer — 2026-10-27 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.22541/authorea.15011148/v1

Background: Healthy hooves are essential for equine welfare and performance. Although traditional steel shoes remain the standard in farriery, these have disadvantages and may be unsuitable for some horses. Advances in additive manufacturing have enabled the development of tailormade 3D-printed shoes that can be glued directly to the hoof. Objectives: To describe the development of a tailormade 3D-printed shoe manufactured from a thermoplastic elastomer and to evaluate its effects on biomechanical parameters over a seven-week shoeing cycle. Study Design: In vivo experimental study. Methods: Six horses were fitted with tailormade 3D-printed glue-on shoes and monitored throughout a seven-week shoeing interval. A conventional steel shoeing cycle was also evaluated as reference. Kinematic data were collected using 3D inertial measurement units attached to the hooves to measure accelerations. Kinetic data, including vertical ground reaction forces and hoof balance curves, were obtained using a pressure-force measurement system. Assessments were performed during the first week (W1) and the seventh week (W7) of the shoeing cycle. Data were summarised descriptively, and exploratory comparisons between W1 and W7 were conducted using linear mixed-effects models. Results: All 3D-printed shoes remained securely attached throughout the seven-week period without requiring replacement. No consistent changes were detected in the evaluated discrete kinematic or kinetic parameters between W1 and W7 in the 3D-printed shoes. However, temporal shifts in both the toe–heel and mediolateral hoof balance curves were observed over the shoeing cycle. Main Limitations: The study was limited to a small sample size. Furthermore, the statistical analyses were exploratory in nature. Conclusions: The tailormade 3D-printed glue-on shoes remained functional and securely attached throughout a complete shoeing cycle. Their use was not associated with marked alterations in kinematic or kinetic parameters. Continuing research is indicated to further characterise their mechanical properties and to substantiate the clinical value of this type of horseshoe.

Domain-validated residual XGBoost surrogates for model-informed biopharmaceutics and population pharmacokinetic inference

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Sergio Sánchez-Herrero, D. Garcia-Alvarez, Laura Calvet, Ángel A. Juan — 2026-10-27 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejpb.2026.115260

Mechanistic population pharmacokinetic (popPK) models support model-informed biopharmaceutics, but repeated exact simulations can make nonlinear mixed-effects inference computationally expensive. We developed a domain-validated residual XGBoost surrogate embedded in stochastic approximation expectation-maximisation (SAEM) for a two-compartment popPK model implemented in PhysPK®. The emulator learned local residual deviations from a mechanistic baseline and was activated only after held-out validation against exact simulations. Fixed-effect updates were constrained within a log-parameter trust region, with exact fallback outside the validated dosing and parameter domain and exact-model recomputation of final diagnostics. Evaluation used a published six-animal preclinical monoclonal-antibody dataset, internal cohorts, 200 balanced noisy parametric bootstrap replicates of 30 virtual animals each, and sparse-sampling variants. In the external proof-of-concept cohort, surrogate-assisted SAEM reproduced the exact reference solution with profile-level mean relative error of 0.0041 and fixed effects within 3.1%. All 200 bootstrap runs completed and met the prespecified fixed-effect stability criterion by iteration 49. Bootstrap emulator error remained low (mean MRE 0.0027; maximum 0.0028), whereas θ3 (Q) showed the greatest uncertainty. Exact local and Morris sensitivity analyses supported weak practical identifiability rather than surrogate bias as the source of this instability. Single-profile evaluation was approximately 49-fold faster, supporting auditable surrogate-assisted inference within the validated applicability domain.
